# ستوري بورد الرواية الكامل — تحت ظل النخلة
## Full Storyboard Reference — Under the Palm Tree (All 36 Palms)

**دليل مرجعي لكل مشهد في الرواية كاملة (36 نخلة، 221 مشهد)**، مستخرج مباشرة من نص الرواية — ما فيه أي إضافة أو اختراع.

لكل مشهد تقسيم لوحات (Panels)، كل لوحة فيها:
- **وصف بصري** للمخرج/الرسام: المكان، الشخصيات الموجودة، الحركة والتعبير.
- **اقتباس/تعليق مختصر** تحت كل لوحة (من نص الرواية نفسه أو تلخيص قصير جداً له).

---


## Palm One — Thuraya's Book of Thoughts

### Scene 1 — Turkey / The Notebook

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot: a quiet room overlooking the Bosphorus at dawn. Thuraya sits upright on a wooden chair by an open window, hands still, gaze distant. A cup of Turkish coffee sits untouched on the table beside her.
> "The morning had settled over the city like a held breath."

**Panel 2** — Close-up on Thuraya's hand holding a blue Cartier pen, poised but not writing.
> "In her hand, a blue Cartier pen. Her father's gift."

**Panel 3** — Flashback inset: young Thuraya's small hand disappearing inside her father's two hands — Professor Mohammed Al-Naabi, silent, solemn, the day she received her scholarship.
> "He had simply taken her hand in both of his. Just held it."

**Panel 4** — Close-up: worn leather notebook cover, her own handwriting visible on it.
> "'A story that might have been in Thuraya's mind.'"

**Panel 5** — Thuraya opens the notebook, pen raised above the blank page, suspended, not yet writing.
> "She raised the pen above the page. Did not write yet."

**Panel 6** — She begins to write; Arabic script filling the page, her expression calm and focused.
> "Today is my birthday. I am thirty-eight, studying my Master's in Turkey."

**Panel 7** — Pen pauses mid-page, tip barely touching paper, her expression caught between thoughts.
> "Today... I had a strange dream."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya's eyes lift from the page, staring past the window into some unseen distance, fingers tightening on the pen.
> "A dream that took me back to a distant time... How could that year feel this close?"

**Panel 9** — Her hand turns the page slowly; the room around her softens at the edges like a painting left in the sun, then fades to white.
> "And just like that, the room in Turkey grew quiet in a different way — and then it was gone."

---

### Scene 2 — Transition

**Panel 1** — Close on Thuraya's pen slowing on the page, her gaze unfocused, drifting past the notebook.
> "How can that year feel this close?"

**Panel 2** — Thuraya's face in soft stillness, eyes open but turned inward, no longer seeing the Bosphorus outside.
> "Her eyes were open and yet entirely elsewhere. Turned inward. Turned backward."

**Panel 3** — Her thumb at the edge of the notebook page, turning it with ceremonial care, as if crossing a threshold.
> "As though turning it was less an act of reading than an act of crossing."

**Panel 4** — The room subtly shifts — curtains, coffee cup, and window light all seem to draw back like a tide going out.
> "The room was no longer the same room. The morning was no longer the same morning."

**Panel 5** — Thuraya lowers the pen to the page with sudden certainty; a number and a word appear on the white paper.
> "The number sat alone on the white page — stark, patient, absolute."

**Panel 6** — Wide shot: Thuraya motionless in her chair as the whole scene dissolves around her.
> "Without moving from her chair, she was no longer in Turkey at all."

---

### Scene 3 — London Apartment

**Panel 1** — Establishing shot: grey London skyline, flat pale sky pressed over rooftops, cold and permanent.
> "The sky over Britain was the colour of old linen."

**Panel 2** — Interior: Sophia curled into a wooden chair by the window, knees drawn up, deeply absorbed in a book titled *The Palm Tree*.
> "She was entirely absorbed in her book."

**Panel 3** — Close-up: a small table beside her — a closed, unlit box of Omani frankincense, untouched for weeks.
> "Omani frankincense. Closed. Unlit. Untouched."

**Panel 4** — Close-up: a framed photograph beside the box — a young boy standing between his parents, mountains and palm trees behind them.
> "John. Behind them — mountains. Palm trees. A place that was entirely not here."

**Panel 5** — Sophia's fingers go still on the page; she lifts her head slightly, sensing something in the air.
> "A scent. Arriving before he did. Oud."

**Panel 6** — Sophia turns in her chair to look toward the doorway, having taken one breath first.
> "She breathed in first. Then turned."

**Panel 7** — John standing just inside the room, still, watching her, an open letter in his hand.
> "A letter was in his hand. Open. Read."

**Panel 8** — Two-shot: Sophia and John locking eyes across the room in charged silence.
> "And in that silence, before a single word had been spoken — she already knew."

---

### Scene 4 — The Decision

**Panel 1** — John standing near Sophia's chair, voice low; Sophia's eyes still on her book, not really reading.
> "'Sophia…'"

**Panel 2** — Sophia looks up, sets the book down deliberately, gives him full attention.
> "'You sound serious.'"

**Panel 3** — John steps closer, letter held carefully in hand.
> "'I got the job.'"

**Panel 4** — Close-up: Sophia's fingers tightening on the spine of the book.
> "'What job?'"

**Panel 5** — John, steady and calm, meeting her eyes.
> "'In Oman.'"

**Panel 6** — Sophia, puzzled, processing the word.
> "'Oman… as in the Middle East?' — 'Yes.'"

**Panel 7** — John speaking plainly, listing facts one by one.
> "'They are opening schools. Building roads. Starting something new. And they asked for me.'"

**Panel 8** — Sophia sits down slowly, overwhelmed, needing ground beneath her.
> "'I don't know anything about Oman.'"

**Panel 9** — John, honest and unguarded, no false confidence.
> "'I know only a little… but I know this — it's a chance. A real chance.'"

**Panel 10** — Sophia studying his face intently, then her eyes drifting toward the table — frankincense box, photograph, mountains, palms.
> "Her eyes moved — away from him, toward the table."

**Panel 11** — Sophia's face goes distant, the room blurring around her as memory pulls her under.
> "Without crossing any distance — she was no longer in the room."

---

### Scene 5 — Memory / University

**Panel 1** — Wide shot: a large university lecture hall, students seated in rows with notebooks open, afternoon light in long diagonals. On the board: "Literature Around the World."
> "A lecture hall. Wide and high-ceilinged."

**Panel 2** — The professor, an Iraqi man with commanding presence, teaching at the front of the hall.
> "He did not simply teach. He inhabited the material."

**Panel 3** — Sophia seated mid-row, pen moving slower than the others, pausing.
> "She was not writing. She was listening to something the lecture had not put into the room."

**Panel 4** — Sophia's pen stops; her head lifts toward a voice speaking fluent Arabic from the side of the room.
> "A voice. Off to the side. Speaking in a language she did not know."

**Panel 5** — John standing at the side of the lecture hall, speaking fluent Arabic with total ease, addressing the professor.
> "Not reading from anything, speaking fluently — as though the language was already part of the body."

**Panel 6** — The professor responding warmly; other students glancing up, some confused, some impressed, most returning to their notes.
> "The exchange moved between them with the effortless rhythm of two people sharing the same native water."

**Panel 7** — Close on Sophia, leaning back slightly, watching John intently — not the words, but the person.
> "Who is he?"

**Panel 8** — Sophia looking down at her notebook, staring at an unfinished sentence, distracted.
> "She did not finish it. She was thinking about something else entirely."

---

### Scene 6 — Back to Present / The Choice

**Panel 1** — Sophia blinks; the lecture hall dissolves like smoke around her.
> "She blinked. The lecture hall dissolved."

**Panel 2** — Back in the London apartment — John still standing exactly where she left him, holding the letter, waiting patiently.
> "John had not moved. Still holding the letter, still patient, still waiting."

**Panel 3** — Sophia studies his face carefully, the same searching look from the lecture hall years before.
> "The way she had looked at him in that lecture hall years ago, for the first time."

**Panel 4** — Her gaze drifts again to the table — frankincense, photograph, the boy, the mountains, the palms.
> "A place that had always been part of him — long before she was."

**Panel 5** — Sophia closes her book softly and sets it down on the table with deliberate finality.
> "The sound it made — soft, final — seemed to mark something."

**Panel 6** — Sophia, calm and direct, looking straight at John.
> "'I'm coming with you.'"

**Panel 7** — John frozen, stunned, absorbing what she's said.
> "'You're not even going to argue?'"

**Panel 8** — Sophia shakes her head gently.
> "'No.' — 'Book the tickets.'"

**Panel 9** — John exhales, shoulders dropping in visible relief, almost to himself.
> "'Together…'"

---

### Scene 7 — Preparing

**Panel 1** — A first suitcase appears by the apartment door, then a second, then a third.
> "First one bag appeared near the door. Then a second. Then a third."

**Panel 2** — Wide shot: the apartment overrun with bags — stacked, leaning, covering every surface and the floor.
> "There was no longer a floor to speak of."

**Panel 3** — John standing frozen in the middle of the chaos, staring around him in disbelief.
> "'Sophia… are we moving to Oman… or relocating London?'"

**Panel 4** — Sophia on the floor beside an open suitcase, calmly and precisely folding clothes.
> "'This is normal.' — 'For women.'"

**Panel 5** — John picking up a dress from a pile, turning it over in his hands, incredulous.
> "'…You told me you have nothing to wear.'"

**Panel 6** — John's small, defeated, involuntary smile as he sets the dress down and shakes his head.
> "A quiet smile pulled at the corner of his mouth — the smile of a man who has lost a battle."

**Panel 7** — John crouching beside an unopened suitcase, unzipping it, beginning to help pack; Sophia glances over with a small private smile.
> "Without argument, he crouched beside the nearest suitcase — and began to help."

---

### Scene 8 — The Flight

**Panel 1** — The airplane climbing steeply, London's grey rooftops dissolving into cloud below the window.
> "Britain fell away beneath it — the grey rooftops dissolving into grey cloud."

**Panel 2** — Sophia at the window seat, palm pressed lightly to the cold glass, watching the last of the city vanish.
> "She pressed her hand lightly to the cold glass. As if touching it were a kind of farewell."

**Panel 3** — John asleep beside her, head tilted, face unguarded and peaceful.
> "John was asleep. Not lightly — completely."

**Panel 4** — Sophia alert and awake, hands resting in her lap, staring out at endless white cloud.
> "Awake and still, her attention entirely outward."

**Panel 5** — The clouds break apart outside the window, revealing dark, dramatic mountain shapes below.
> "Mountains. Wide and dry and absolute."

**Panel 6** — Sophia leaning close to the glass, startled — the terrain below is rugged and immense, not the flat sand she expected.
> "What she was looking at was terrain of enormous presence, of density and weight."

**Panel 7** — A shaft of sharp, direct sunlight crosses the cabin and touches Sophia's face through the window.
> "The light arrived directly — clean, intense, making everything it touched more sharply itself."

**Panel 8** — Sophia glancing sideways at sleeping John, half-whispering, mountains still visible below.
> "'How…?' she whispered. Not expecting an answer."

**Panel 9** — Sophia pressing her forehead to the glass, gazing down at the mountains in quiet realization.
> "This was a life. Beginning now. Nothing after this would be the same."

---

### Scene 9 — Arrival in Oman

**Panel 1** — The airplane door swings open; visible heat shimmer rolls into the cabin doorway.
> "The airplane door opened — and the heat did not wait."

**Panel 2** — Sophia frozen mid-step at the top of the boarding stairs, fanning herself with a boarding pass, squinting in the sun.
> "'This is not warm.' — 'This is fire.'"

**Panel 3** — John descending the stairs behind her, hand raised to shield his eyes from the glare.
> "He lifted his hand against the sun instinctively, squinting into the brightness."

**Panel 4** — Close on John's face — a flicker of old, wordless recognition as he looks out at the tarmac and low buildings.
> "'I don't remember it being this hot… when I was a child. Or maybe… I forgot.'"

**Panel 5** — Wide shot: heat shimmering visibly off the tarmac in waves, the two of them small against the bright, open airfield.
> "'How do people live in this?'"

**Panel 6** — Interior: a small, quiet airport terminal, few people, no rush, everyone moving unhurried.
> "The airport was smaller than she had imagined. Quieter."

**Panel 7** — Sophia gripping her bag strap tightly, uneasy, looking to John.
> "'What if we get lost?'"

**Panel 8** — John scanning overhead signs calmly, pointing the way forward.
> "'Exit.' 'Taxi.' — 'We'll take it step by step.'"

---

### Scene 10 — Airport / Luggage & First Realization

**Panel 1** — John standing at the luggage belt, leaning forward, watching bags circle past.
> "John stood at the edge of it. Focused. Watching each bag as it came into view."

**Panel 2** — A growing pile of suitcases stacking up beside him — one, two, five, eight, ten.
> "The pile beside them grew with a kind of inexorable momentum."

**Panel 3** — John muttering under his breath, gesturing at the mountain of luggage, half-smiling.
> "'Every day you say you have nothing to wear… and yet… you brought London.'"

**Panel 4** — Sophia, ignoring him completely, her gaze fixed on something across the arrivals hall.
> "Something else had taken her attention completely."

**Panel 5** — Across the hall: a composed woman in a neat hijab standing perfectly still, waiting, raising her hand in a single unhurried wave.
> "The woman raised her hand — a single, unhurried gesture — and waved."

**Panel 6** — Close on Sophia's face, recognition dawning slowly.
> "'That's her…'"

**Panel 7** — Sophia walking briskly through the crowd toward the woman, John turning to follow.
> "Sophia was already moving, walking through the crowd with certainty."

---

### Scene 11 — First Meeting / Thuraya

**Panel 1** — Sophia and John approaching Thuraya across the thinning crowd of the arrivals hall.
> "They walked toward her — the distance closing in the unhurried way of things that are meant to meet."

**Panel 2** — Sophia extending her hand toward Thuraya, then pausing slightly as a light scent of oud reaches her.
> "Something had reached her before the woman had. Oud — lighter, quietly feminine."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya, warm and composed, greeting them.
> "'I'm Thuraya.' — 'Welcome to Oman.'"

**Panel 4** — John stepping forward, gesturing to Sophia beside him.
> "'I'm John. This is my wife, Sophia.'"

**Panel 5** — Sophia smiling, a little uncertain but genuine, shaking Thuraya's hand.
> "'Nice to meet you.'"

**Panel 6** — Thuraya nodding back warmly.
> "'Nice to meet you too.'"

**Panel 7** — Thuraya's gaze sliding past them to the enormous pile of luggage stacked behind John and Sophia, a small knowing smile forming.
> "A small smile — knowing, unhurried — moved across her face."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya, amused, addressing them both.
> "'Do you need help with the bags?'"


---


## Palm Two — The First Walk in the Village

### Scene 1 — The First Night

**Panel 1** — Interior, night. A small clay-walled room lit by a trembling qandeel lamp, a thin bisat on the floor, no bed. Sophia stands near the open window, one hand resting lightly on the wooden frame. John sleeps in the background.
> "The room did not announce itself — quiet, waiting to be lived in."

**Panel 2** — Close on Sophia's face at the window, eyes half-closed, breathing in the night air layered with scent.
> "Oud first. Then musk. Then — Luban."

**Panel 3** — Sophia glances back at John, who sleeps entirely, without resistance; a faint smile pulls at the corner of her mouth.
> "Behind her, John was asleep — entirely, without resistance."

**Panel 4** — Wide shot from the window: dark village rooftops below, a distant minaret against the pre-dawn sky.
> "The adhan for Fajr rose — deepening the quiet, not breaking it."

**Panel 5** — Sophia leans forward, eyes searching the path below; small running figures appear in the dim light.
> "Then — movement. Footsteps, faster now. Children."

**Panel 6** — Low-angle view of children running down the dark path toward the mosque, urgent and joyful.
> "'يالله يالله صلااااه!' — 'أنا أسبق! أنا أسبق!'"

**Panel 7** — Sophia watching, thoughtful, the path now empty again.
> "If they wake before the sun does — if they move through the dark this willingly—"

**Panel 8** — Sophia turns back toward sleeping John, mouth parting as if to speak, then closing it.
> "She almost said something. She didn't."

**Panel 9** — Sophia murmuring quietly to herself at the window, alone with the thought.
> "'Where are the girls?' she murmured. 'And the women?'"

**Panel 10** — Sophia sinks down and sits against the clay wall, eyes closing, sleep finally taking her.
> "Tomorrow — she would walk this place."

### Scene 2 — Letj

**Panel 1** — Bright morning, sun already high over the village path. Thuraya stands waiting, still and at ease.
> "'Ready?' Sophia nodded. 'Yes.'"

**Panel 2** — Sophia, John, and Thuraya walking together along an uneven earthen path between close-set clay houses.
> "The houses stood close to the land — not placed upon it, but growing from it."

**Panel 3** — Sophia looking around at the open, quiet village as they walk.
> "'Everything feels very open,' Sophia said. 'And very quiet.'"

**Panel 4** — John gesturing as he speaks; Thuraya glances at him, approving.
> "'It's like the village is breathing slowly,' John said."

**Panel 5** — A group of girls in a loose circle at the path's edge; one throws a stone into rectangles drawn in the dirt.
> "'This is letj,' Thuraya said."

**Panel 6** — Close on a girl hopping precisely through the drawn squares, focused and practiced, avoiding every line.
> "One foot in the single squares, both feet apart in the paired ones."

**Panel 7** — Sophia watching intently, exhaling as if she'd been holding her breath.
> "'That's not easy.' 'No,' Thuraya said. 'It isn't.'"

**Panel 8** — Sophia steps forward and takes the stone from a girl, studying the ground; John smiles beside her.
> "'I want to try.' John: 'Of course you do.'"

**Panel 9** — Sophia mid-hop, her foot landing right on the line; the girls around her burst into laughter.
> "Her foot found the edge of the line. She stopped. 'I lost.'"

**Panel 10** — Malood the goat comes charging through the game, scattering the drawn lines; girls shout and chase after him.
> "'هيييه! مالود!' — furious and delighted in equal measure."

**Panel 11** — The girls crouch back down, redrawing the shapes in the dust with their fingers, unbothered.
> "No debate. No decision required. They simply returned to it."

### Scene 3 — The Falaj

**Panel 1** — The group walking; ahead, a narrow stone water channel comes into view, the sound of running water rising.
> "'Do you hear that?' Sophia slowed. John nodded. 'Yes.'"

**Panel 2** — Close on the falaj: clear water flowing through a carved stone channel, light catching its surface in shifting patterns.
> "It did not rush. It did not pause. It simply flowed."

**Panel 3** — Wide shot along the falaj: women washing clothes and scrubbing large pots at its edges, children splashing in the water.
> "Women washed clothes. Children played in the same water."

**Panel 4** — Malood lowers his head and drinks from the channel, unbothered, right beside the splashing children.
> "And just to the side — Malood. He drank. Without consulting anyone."

**Panel 5** — Sophia and John standing at the edge; Sophia's expression is astonished as she takes it all in.
> "'They are using the same water for everything.'"

**Panel 6** — Across the channel, Nasser pours water from the falaj over the pickup truck in long arcs, scrubbing it clean.
> "Across the channel, Nasser poured water over the truck in long arcs."

**Panel 7** — John crouches at the water's edge and splashes his face with both hands, looking up refreshed.
> "He looked up — entirely refreshed, entirely unconcerned."

**Panel 8** — Sophia, hands raised in disbelief, staring at John.
> "'John! You just washed your face in that!' 'Yes.'"

**Panel 9** — Two women by the water notice Sophia and John, smiling and speaking to each other, one laughing.
> "'They're talking about us, aren't they?' 'Yes.'"

**Panel 10** — Sophia's eyes widen; Thuraya smiles beside her, amused.
> "'They are asking who is your husband.' 'Already?'"

**Panel 11** — Sophia gives a small, uncertain wave to the women, who wave back warmly; her expression settles into quiet thought as she looks back at the water.
> "The same water. Different uses. She was beginning to understand."

### Scene 4 — Sadoo's Shop

**Panel 1** — John and Sophia walking on; John glances back the way they came, mid-conversation about the falaj.
> "'It's still there. Like a long line running through everything.'"

**Panel 2** — John mid-stride is suddenly knocked off balance by something unseen from the side, toppling toward the water.
> "Something hit him. Fast. Entirely without warning."

**Panel 3** — John sits soaked in the falaj, stunned; Thuraya and Sophia laugh above him; Malood stands at the edge looking down placidly.
> "The water received him with cold, democratic completeness."

**Panel 4** — John stands up dripping; Malood presses close, following him persistently.
> "'Now you have two [friends].' 'The falaj… and Malood.'"

**Panel 5** — A small open-fronted shop comes into view around a bend in the path, simple and well-worn.
> "'This is Sadoo's shop,' Thuraya said."

**Panel 6** — Children swarm toward the shop from every direction, shouting and reaching.
> "'Sadووو!' — 'أنا باغي مينو!' — 'أنا باغي داغوص!'"

**Panel 7** — Inside the shop, Sadoo calmly hands out snacks amid the crowd of children, composed and efficient.
> "Sadoo moved between them — all of this is simply Tuesday."

**Panel 8** — A barefoot, dusty boy proudly dips his snack into spicy daghoos sauce.
> "'Children eat spicy food?' 'Yes. They start early.'"

**Panel 9** — Sophia holds up a colorful boba bottle, reading the label and shaking it curiously; John watches, amused.
> "'Bo… ba?' 'A drink.' 'It's gas?'"

**Panel 10** — Malood bursts into the shop, jumping onto boxes and sniffing shelves; children chase him back out in chaos.
> "'يالله! مالود! برا!' — half the village now involved."

**Panel 11** — Sophia laughs so hard she has to hold the doorframe; John, still wet, stands beside her with Malood nearby.
> "Sophia laughed so hard she had to hold the door."

**Panel 12** — Across the road, Saeed stands alone and still, watching everything without joining in; Thuraya's expression softens as she notices him.
> "'Who is that boy?' 'That is Saeed. He is not allowed to go to school.'"

### Scene 5 — Thursday / Khamis

**Panel 1** — The path ahead; Sophia walks slightly slower than before, taking things in more fully.
> "She was watching differently now. With more of herself in it."

**Panel 2** — A tall man walks ahead of them in a simple dishdasha and tightly wound turban, carrying a ma'ool with a worn metal edge.
> "Tall. Firm posture. A ma'ool in his hand, its edge darkened from years of use."

**Panel 3** — Behind the man, a boy walks in the exact same rhythm — same pace, same weight, same angle in the shoulders.
> "Behind him — a boy. Same pace, same weight, same angle in the shoulders."

**Panel 4** — Sophia asks Thuraya quietly who they are; Thuraya answers simply.
> "'Who is that?' 'That is Khamis.'"

**Panel 5** — John stops, surprised, turning to Thuraya.
> "'Like… Thursday? You name people after days of the week?'"

**Panel 6** — Thuraya explains with an amused smile; John laughs.
> "'If the child is born on Thursday, he can be called Khamis.'"

**Panel 7** — Close on the boy Khamis walking behind his father, entirely absorbed in the rhythm, unaware of being watched.
> "'This doesn't feel like a child,' Sophia said. 'He looks older.'"

**Panel 8** — Wide shot: the man and boy recede into the distance down the path; Sophia watches them go.
> "'I want to understand.' Thuraya: 'You will.'"

### Scene 6 — Nahsher / Another World

**Panel 1** — The path leads into a greener, shaded grove; trees heavy with clusters of colorful fruit.
> "'Look!' Trees — farsadh and lamba, Thuraya explained."

**Panel 2** — A group of boys under the trees; one throws a stone up into the branches, fruit and leaves falling.
> "They are doing nahsher. They throw stones so the fruit falls."

**Panel 3** — Other boys run forward, quickly collecting the fallen fruit in a well-practiced system.
> "No wasted movement. A system arrived at through practice."

**Panel 4** — John repeats the new word carefully; Sophia smiles at him; Thuraya laughs.
> "'Nah… sher.' 'Let's go and… nahsher.'"

**Panel 5** — Nearby, boys eat fruit dipped in something bright orange — salt, pepper, and chili.
> "'They put salt and pepper… on mango?' 'And chili.'"

**Panel 6** — John reaches forward and takes a small piece of the spiced mango; Sophia watches, skeptical.
> "Because he was the kind of man who believed in trying things."

**Panel 7** — Close on John's face mid-chew, working through the flavor, then breaking into a slow smile.
> "'It's… strange. But good.'"

**Panel 8** — Wide shot, sky turning orange; the family walks back toward the apartment as the village settles into evening — Sadoo closing his shop, children heading home.
> "The village settled around them as the afternoon folded into evening."

**Panel 9** — In the distance, Saeed still stands unmoved, watching quietly as the light fades.
> "Still silent. Still thinking. Dreaming of a place called school."

**Panel 10** — Interior, night. Sophia sits on the bisat, looking around the clay-walled room, then at John.
> "'John… I think we didn't move to another country.'"

**Panel 11** — John looks at her, questioning; Sophia smiles.
> "'Where did we move?' She smiled. 'We moved to another world.'"


---


## Palm Three — The First Day at School

### Scene 1 — The Assembly Yard

**Panel 1** — Establishing shot: a small mud-brick village school, stone and clay walls, two long classrooms, a wooden door, a small dusty yard out front. Morning light.
> "The school did not announce itself."

**Panel 2** — Interior classroom: worn wooden desks carved with children's names, a not-very-black blackboard, chalk. Empty, waiting.
> "For the children of Al-Qurawashiyah, this place was something new."

**Panel 3** — Wide village shot: mothers walking children to school, some boys running ahead, some girls walking slowly clutching books like treasures.
> "That morning, the village moved differently."

**Panel 4** — Inside the classroom, Thuraya alone at the board, arranging chalk, wiping the table with her hand, listening to distant footsteps and noise, smiling to herself.
> "They are coming."

**Panel 5** — John stands near the door holding a book like a soldier with a map; Sophia beside him in a simple long dress and light headscarf, looking around nervously.
> "I feel like I am about to meet one hundred small judges."

**Panel 6** — John smiling reassuringly at Sophia.
> "They are not judges. They are students." / "They look dangerous."

**Panel 7** — The teachers' room: wide blue wooden doors, a bisat flat on the floor, a slow ceiling fan, wooden tables, shelves of books in disorder. Sophia moving through it slowly, John running his hand along a table edge.
> "It's different." / "It's enough."

**Panel 8** — The empty classroom itself: stone walls, one blue wooden door, a single unfiltered window, wooden benches. Sophia standing alone in the center, looking around.
> "This room was ready. Whether she was, was another question."

**Panel 9** — The assembly yard: wide, flat, sun-hardened ground, a wooden flagpole with a folded cloth at its base. Thuraya, John, and Sophia standing near it.
> "The flag."

**Panel 10** — John and Sophia unfolding the flag together — red, white, and green colors emerging, the emblem catching the light against the dusty clay yard.
> "Against the clay and dust — the flag did not blend. It stood."

**Panel 11** — Children pouring into the yard in a rush, dust rising behind their running feet, then slowing into loose rows.
> "They were coming because they had decided to come."

**Panel 12** — Sophia stepping forward toward a group of boys, Thuraya translating beside her.
> "Why are you late?" — "They had a Qur'an circle before school."

### Scene 2 — The Students

**Panel 1** — Classroom doorway: children flooding in loudly, laughing, pushing, scattering toward seats with no order at all.
> "They came like birds."

**Panel 2** — Thuraya at the front, clapping her hands once, sharp and practiced. Some children sitting, some still standing.
> "Sit down, please."

**Panel 3** — Close on Mansoor: small, thin, dusty boy with mischievous eyes, mid-motion — moving from one chair to another, about to fall.
> "Mansoor." — the smile that means trouble.

**Panel 4** — John watching in disbelief from the side; Sophia leaning toward him, murmuring.
> "That one will kill us." / "Yes. But slowly."

**Panel 5** — Ahmed in the front row: perfectly straight posture, book and pencil squared. Beside him Ali, calm-eyed, watching the board intently.
> "That one is very clever."

**Panel 6** — Khalil: smiling, restless — sitting, standing, helping a classmate, whispering, laughing, all in quick succession.
> "Helpful. But dangerous."

**Panel 7** — Noura striding in, rapid-fire questions on her face; Sophia blinking at the pace of her.
> "Where do I sit? Is this my book? Is today a long day?"

**Panel 8** — Mathila distributing papers and pencils to other girls unasked; Salima, Rahma, and Marwa arriving together, shy, sitting close in a tight cluster.
> "Here. Take."

**Panel 9** — Wide shot: the classroom now completely full, loud, alive with unrestrained energy.
> Alive — "using every remaining second of that freedom."

**Panel 10** — Thuraya at the front addressing the class; Mansoor's hand shooting up, pointing at John and Sophia.
> "Are they the new teachers?" — "They look strange."

**Panel 11** — Sophia with a hand on her chest in mock offense, John laughing beside her; Thuraya introducing them formally.
> "This is Mr. John. And this is Ms. Safiya."

**Panel 12** — Close on a few children's faces quietly mouthing the new name to themselves; Sophia's expression softening, pleased.
> "Safiya…"

### Scene 3 — Good Morning / The First Word

**Panel 1** — John at the blackboard, chalk in hand, writing HELLO in large letters, then turning to face the class.
> "Good morning."

**Panel 2** — Rows of children staring blankly at John and the word on the board, dead silence.
> Silence.

**Panel 3** — Mansoor standing confidently, mouth open mid-shout, chest puffed with certainty.
> "Good mourning!"

**Panel 4** — The whole class laughing; John laughing along, shaking his head good-naturedly.
> "Close enough."

**Panel 5** — Sophia at the board writing "My name is…", pointing to herself; children repeating haltingly in their seats.
> "My name is Safiya."

**Panel 6** — Mansoor standing again with exaggerated dignity, announcing proudly; Khalil doubled over laughing beside him.
> "My name is monster."

**Panel 7** — Sophia covering her mouth, then composing herself, gently correcting Mansoor, who considers it seriously before answering; John clapping.
> "My name is Mansoor." — "Very good!"

**Panel 8** — Quick succession of portraits: Ahmed upright and precise, Ali steady, Mathila polite, each stating their name.
> "My name is Ahmed." / "My name is Ali." / "My name is Mathila."

**Panel 9** — Noura standing tall, adding extra unnecessary detail with total confidence; Sophia blinking, then laughing.
> "My name is Noura. And this is my friend. And this is my book."

**Panel 10** — Chaotic mix shot: some children shouting phrases, some whispering, Mansoor standing on his chair with arms raised.
> "I am tall!" (He was not.)

**Panel 11** — Malood strutting into the yard/doorway, utterly composed, bleating loudly; the classroom erupting into laughter.
> "ماaaaaaع!"

**Panel 12** — Sophia pointing at Malood with deliberate teacherly timing, the class repeating the word after her; Thuraya stepping in sharply to settle them; John leaning toward Sophia, impressed.
> "This… is a goat." — "Well… that worked."

### Scene 4 — The Way to School / Marhoon and Khamis

**Panel 1** — Wide shot of the village path crowded with students walking to school — some running ahead, some dawdling, voices filling the air.
> "The path had been filled."

**Panel 2** — Malood trailing behind the children, stopping, then bursting into a run, bleating; boys turning back and laughing, waving him on.
> "خلّوه يجي!" — Let him come.

**Panel 3** — Two still figures ahead by the path's edge: Marhoon, leaning on his ma'ool, watching the students pass with quiet, heavy attention; Khamis standing just behind him in the same posture.
> Marhoon — the stillness of a man who does not need to move.

**Panel 4** — A group of boys running past them, laughing, joyfully unserious.
> Their steps failing entirely to look serious.

**Panel 5** — Close on Marhoon speaking, low and heavy, each word deliberate; the boys' postures visibly tightening as they pass.
> "شفت؟ يعلموا الصغيرين يجيوا كما الهوش."

**Panel 6** — Malood strutting past the two men, bleating loudly; Khamis's composure cracking into a quick, stifled laugh.
> "ماaaaaaع!"

**Panel 7** — Marhoon turning his head slightly toward Khamis; Khamis instantly straightening, his face snapping back toward stillness.
> Approaching stillness — but not quite reaching it.

**Panel 8** — Sophia, John, and Thuraya watching the exchange from a distance, Sophia asking a question, Thuraya translating calmly.
> "He told them to behave."

### Scene 5 — Malood and the Morning Disaster

**Panel 1** — Interior of Sophia and John's small room, dawn light; Sophia stirring awake to an odd rhythmic chewing sound, John still half-asleep beside her.
> "There is something eating our house."

**Panel 2** — Reveal shot: Malood standing on John's notebooks on the small table, calmly chewing a page corner into his mouth.
> Chrrrkkk… chrrrkkk…

**Panel 3** — Sophia sitting bolt upright, horrified, pointing; Malood pausing to consider her, then continuing to chew, unbothered.
> "That is PAPER!"

**Panel 4** — John lunging out of bed, grabbing one end of the notebook; Malood gripping the other end, both pulling.
> "Malood! Stop!"

**Panel 5** — The page tearing cleanly in two; John staring at the torn half in his hand, stricken.
> "These were my lesson plans."

**Panel 6** — Malood leaping off the table and bolting outside; John chasing after him through the doorway, shouting.
> "You are not a goat — you are a criminal!"

**Panel 7** — Sophia frozen just outside the doorway, staring at her bare hand.
> "My ring…"

**Panel 8** — Sophia and John standing together, looking slowly around the empty room, the bed, the table, the door — realization dawning on both faces.
> "Malood was here."

### Scene 6 — Commands, Chaos, and Control

**Panel 1** — Sophia and John running through the village toward Thuraya's house, words tumbling out breathlessly; Thuraya's calm visibly tightening as she listens.
> "Malood ate my ring — he destroyed the notebooks — he ran—"

**Panel 2** — Village street: word spreading between houses, neighbors emerging; a man standing in his doorway, aghast, gesturing at the wreckage inside.
> "Who owns this goat?!"

**Panel 3** — Interior of the neighbor's house: Malood standing amid scattered food, torn clothes, and a broken basket, entirely unrepentant.
> "He took my food! He broke my basket!"

**Panel 4** — Sophia and John standing side by side, Sophia hollow-eyed with defeat, John deadly serious.
> "If he eats my passport, I will personally end this friendship."

**Panel 5** — A chaotic chase scene through the village: Malood bolting ahead, John and Sophia and Thuraya running after him, a crowd of laughing children joining in.
> A procession of disaster.

**Panel 6** — Malood leaping into their kitchen, knocking over a large pot; rice, meat, and sauce spilling across the floor. Sophia, John, and Thuraya frozen in the doorway.
> "My lunch."

**Panel 7** — Malood standing amid the mess, chewing calmly, utterly victorious; Thuraya delivering the verdict with a resigned expression.
> "He probably… swallowed it."

**Panel 8** — Evening: Sophia and John sitting together outside, tired but smiling faintly; Thuraya nearby.
> "I love Oman." — "We will never be bored." — "Welcome to village life."

**Panel 9** — Malood asleep, curled contentedly, looking every bit like a king who has finished a good day's work.
> Not like an animal — like a king who found his day good.

**Panel 10** — Sophia walking back into the classroom the next day, students' faces carefully not showing that they already know the story; John beside her holding half a torn notebook.
> The complete dignity of a man who has decided to find this funny.

**Panel 11** — Sophia at the front of the class, pointing firmly at the door, acting out "Stop" — students standing, stepping, freezing in place, delighted.
> "Stop." — "Don't."

**Panel 12** — Malood appearing again at the doorway mid-lesson, bleating; the whole class shouting "STOP!" and "DON'T!" together as Malood finally sits down; the room erupting into joyful laughter, Sophia and John laughing together at the end of the day.
> "Good." — "This is only the beginning."


---


## Palm Four — The Morning Departure to the Souq

### Scene 1 — The Morning Departure

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot of the village at dawn: pale gold light sliding down the mountain, then roofs, then walls, then dusty paths, catching a lone pair of sandals already moving.
> "Morning arrived by small degrees — light on the ridges, then the roofs, then the paths."

**Panel 2** — John stands at the wooden door of their small mud house, adjusting his wristwatch, squinting down the empty dusty road.
> "Safiya? Thuraya said early. I think she meant… this early."

**Panel 3** — Interior: Safiya bent over a large cloth bag on the floor, hair tied back, scarf in place, folding items in with focused concentration.
> "I am coming. I am only… confirming."

**Panel 4** — Safiya still packing, half-turned toward John in the doorway; both mid-conversation, faces mildly exasperated/amused.
> "Have you seen our luggage from the airport?" "That was relocating. This is shopping."

**Panel 5** — A small goat (Malood) bursts between John's legs through the open door and darts into the house; John startled, looking down.
> "MALOOD." "Not again." "Yes again."

**Panel 6** — Malood standing proud in the middle of the room, legs apart, head raised, eyeing the packed bag with interest; Safiya crouched beside it, hand raised.
> "No, no, no. We are not doing this today."

**Panel 7** — Malood sitting obediently in place; through the open door/window, an unseen woman (Azizah) calls out in the distance.
> "Maloooood! Ta'al hna, ya majnoon sagheer!"

**Panel 8** — Malood standing, ears perked toward the door, not moving; John and Safiya exchange a knowing look.
> "She sounds close." "She always sounds close. And he always sounds confident."

**Panel 9** — John tying the cloth bag shut, then handing half its weight to Safiya outside the doorway.
> "Share." "This is marriage." "This is arithmetic."

**Panel 10** — Wide shot: John, Safiya, and Malood walking together down the dusty village road, unhurried, small figures against the landscape, a pickup truck waiting faintly in the distance.
> Three figures and a goat, already part of the landscape.

---

### Scene 2 — Dukkan Al-Hillah

**Panel 1** — Thuraya stands waiting on the path, composed and poised, watching John and Safiya approach with a patient, amused expression.
> "Good morning." "We brought… supplies." "Yes, I see."

**Panel 2** — Thuraya walking ahead, gesturing back at John and Safiya as she leads them toward the shop.
> "First — Dukkan Al-Hillah. We need rope. And a basket. And qit."

**Panel 3** — Exterior/interior of a small open-doored shop: wooden boxes of rice, sugar, tea by the entrance; baskets and ropes on one wall; dried awal and qashie' fish hanging in a corner.
> A shop small the way a thing is small when it never considered being anything else.

**Panel 4** — Sadoo behind the wooden counter, beaming a warm, familiar smile at the entering customers.
> Sadoo — the smile of a man glad simply to see faces he knows.

**Panel 5** — Malood already inside ahead of everyone, casually inspecting the shelves with proprietary confidence; Sadoo laughing at him from behind the counter.
> "Malood! shuftak! What do you want today, ya habibi?"

**Panel 6** — John pointing to a bundle of green leaves on the counter, Sadoo and Thuraya exchanging an amused glance.
> "I want this." A small silence. Sadoo looks at Thuraya.

**Panel 7** — Thuraya correcting John's pronunciation, smiling; Safiya nearby asking what it means; John's face registering realization.
> "Qit. Not 'kit'. Qit." "And what is qit?" "Food for goats."

**Panel 8** — Safiya looking between John and Malood (who has perked up with interest), stifling a laugh; John taking the joke on the chin.
> "John. You just requested goat food." "Then I will buy it. For Malood."

**Panel 9** — Safiya tilted head, studying the hanging dried awal fish with visible skepticism.
> "Thuraya… how do people eat this?" "Slowly. And with rice."

**Panel 10** — The group exiting the shop into brighter morning light, arms full — rope, basket, purchases — Malood trotting behind proudly with the wrapped qit in his mouth.
> Malood walked behind them with the qit in his mouth, proudly.

---

### Scene 3 — Uncle Nasser's Pickup

**Panel 1** — An old, weathered pickup truck resting at the edge of the path: faded paint, thin rust lines along the doors, bent mudguards, a faint tick from under the hood.
> Old not in the sentimental way — old because it worked.

**Panel 2** — Uncle Nasser standing beside the truck: clean but worn dishdasha, tightly wound turban, calm settled eyes, a smooth dark bamboo stick held loosely in his right hand.
> A quality that made the space around him pay attention.

**Panel 3** — Group shot of the children gathered by the truck: Ali seated on a stone with his bag, Ahmed standing straight with a pencil in his shirt pocket, Khalil helping Noura and Mathila load bundles, Marwa/Salima/Rahma whispering in a tight trio, Mansoor orbiting restlessly nearby.
> The children, already gathered, each in their own particular stillness or motion.

**Panel 4** — Uncle Nasser lifting the bamboo stick slightly, like a conductor's baton, addressing the now-still children.
> "Today, we go to Samail. Today, no trouble. Today — who makes trouble meets this."

**Panel 5** — Safiya leaning toward Thuraya, murmuring a question; Thuraya watching the children, quietly certain.
> "Does he really use it?" "He has never needed to."

**Panel 6** — John peering into the open, seatless, beltless flatbed of the truck, then back at Safiya, his face working through doubt.
> "There are no seats." "Yes." "Is this safe?" "It is safer than walking to Samail."

**Panel 7** — Safiya placing one hand on the warm metal side of the flatbed, taking John's offered hand to climb up.
> "If we die, tell my mother it was your idea."

**Panel 8** — Children settling into the truck bed in loose, practiced clusters; Ahmed sitting beside Safiya without discussion; Malood leaping up onto the tailgate last, entitled as ever.
> Malood jumped onto the tailgate like a creature who had decided he was on staff.

**Panel 9** — The engine catching roughly, dust rising in a pale cloud; Uncle Nasser leaning out the cab window shouting a cheerful greeting to a man leading a camel.
> The engine started the way an old man stands up — with complaint, then duty.

**Panel 10** — The truck bouncing hard on the unpaved road; Safiya jolted, teeth clenched, scarf lifting, dust settling on her tongue; Ahmed beside her calm and unbothered.
> "It gets worse," Ahmed said, quietly. "In the valley part."

**Panel 11** — Mansoor standing tall in the moving truck bed, arms out like wings; Thuraya turning sharply from the cab to shout at him; Mansoor dropping back down instantly.
> "I AM FLYING!" "MANSOOR, IGLUS!"

**Panel 12** — Passing landscape: a man on a donkey raising a hand in greeting, red mountains, palm groves, goats parting from the road; Safiya and John leaning into each other, both white with dust, laughing; Thuraya glancing back with a wide smile.
> "You are learning," Thuraya said, over the engine.

---

### Scene 4 — Samail Market

**Panel 1** — The truck approaching Samail: mud walls forming into streets, a young boy driving a small herd of goats, a woman balancing a water container on her head.
> Samail did not arrive. Samail accumulated.

**Panel 2** — The truck parked in a dusty patch beside a low wall, engine silent, dust still settling over everyone's hair and clothes.
> "We arrived." "We survived."

**Panel 3** — Children climbing down from the truck bed easily; John jumping down and turning to raise his hands up to Safiya, helping her down.
> John jumped down first, laughing, then raised his hands to her. "Come."

**Panel 4** — Safiya standing still just past the truck, eyes wide, overwhelmed by the market opening around her in layers — a wash of unfamiliar sound, vivid color, and thick layered smells.
> Sound first. Then colour. Then smell, arriving last, hardest to catalogue.

**Panel 5** — Safiya turning to John, both still amid the crowd and stalls, her expression full of wonder.
> "This is not a market. This is a country that has been compressed into three streets."

**Panel 6** — The group moving through the crowded market lanes: a man passing with a live chicken under each arm, a date seller calling out prices in a musical, rising-falling voice.
> A price at the top, a justification at the bottom — persuasion as art.

**Panel 7** — Safiya stopped at a frankincense stall; an elderly woman merchant crushes a small golden resin stone between her fingers and holds it out for Safiya to smell.
> Not offering to sell. Offering to share.

**Panel 8** — Safiya paying for the frankincense without bargaining; Thuraya watching her with quiet approval.
> "This is hojari. The frankincense of kings." "You cannot haggle an offering." "You are reading the market correctly."

**Panel 9** — John standing before a fish stall, pointing at a large dried awal fish laid on a cloth; the white-bearded fish seller raising an eyebrow.
> "For family?" "For… experience." The seller laughed. "Tayyeb. Good."

**Panel 10** — The fish seller wrapping the awal in paper, cloth, and palm twine, handing the heavy parcel to John, who is visibly startled by its weight and smell; Safiya stepping back with a hand near her nose.
> "It smells." "I know." "It smells with conviction."

**Panel 11** — Safiya wandering a few stalls further among a cloth merchant, a man brazing a metal pot, a date seller arranging his fruit; a small boy collides with her, blurts "sorry" in careful English, and dashes off, leaving her laughing freely.
> She was no longer inside this market as a visitor. She was inside it as a person.

**Panel 12** — Thuraya briefly resting a hand on Safiya's arm in silent acknowledgment, then clapping once to gather the scattered children, John, and Malood (caught mid-theft of someone's cabbage).
> "Now," Thuraya said, "we learn."

---

### Scene 5 — I Want

**Panel 1** — Thuraya standing before the gathered children in the market, addressing them; Noura curious, Mansoor briefly still.
> "Today we will learn one sentence." "Only one?" "One that will work a hundred times."

**Panel 2** — John holding up a small, carefully folded piece of paper with large capital letters written on it, showing it to the children.
> I WANT…

**Panel 3** — Thuraya modeling the phrase slowly; the children repeating it back with varying confidence, faces concentrating.
> "I want…" "I want." "I… want." "Very good. Now — we use it."

**Panel 4** — Thuraya pointing toward a nearby date stall with a kind, half-listening shopkeeper waiting; children looking at each other.
> "Who will go first?"

**Panel 5** — Ali stepping up alone to the date stall, standing straight, addressing the shopkeeper.
> "I want… this." The shopkeeper smiled hugely and handed him a date. "Take. Take."

**Panel 6** — Ali walking back to the group holding the date, face full of quiet astonished pride.
> "It worked," he said, amazed.

**Panel 7** — Mansoor pushing to the front, taking a huge theatrical breath, then shouting at an orange-cloth stall while pointing wildly; the seller handing him three oranges as children erupt laughing.
> "I WANT THIS! THIS! THIS!" Three "this" — three oranges.

**Panel 8** — Noura standing before a fruit stall, pausing dramatically before delivering an elaborate sentence; the seller laughing, then patiently pointing out and naming each fruit as she nods seriously.
> "I want… to know the name of every fruit in this market."

**Panel 9** — Montage-style grouping: Ahmed speaking precisely at a stall, Khalil charming a seller, Mathila already handing out what she bought before finishing, Rahma/Marwa/Salima asking together and receiving three identical bags.
> One by one, the children went — each in their own way.

**Panel 10** — Safiya watching the children with quiet, moved attention; she turns to John beside her.
> "We have a lot to learn." "Not just the children."

---

### Scene 6 — Habbabouh Saif

**Panel 1** — Safiya fascinated by a deep crimson cloth at a textile stall; an unseen voice speaks flawless, unhurried English from behind her.
> "Good morning. You must be the new teachers."

**Panel 2** — An elderly, dignified man turns to face them: long white beard, simple dishdasha, an old and finely embroidered kummah, calm kind eyes.
> The word 'old' did not quite fit him. Better: settled.

**Panel 3** — John, stunned, stammering as he faces the man; Safiya and Thuraya watching.
> "You… you speak English." "I do." "Very well." "I had time."

**Panel 4** — The old man introducing himself with a small explanatory gesture, Safiya and John listening closely.
> "My name is Saif. But here — the people call me Habbabouh Saif."

**Panel 5** — Saif gesturing outward, as if toward the sea, describing his years with the Omani fleet and life in Zanzibar; Safiya's face shifting with surprise.
> "I worked many years with the Omani fleet. And I lived — for many years — in Zanzibar."

**Panel 6** — Close on Safiya, quietly repeating the word, struck by the scale of what it implies about Oman's history.
> "Zanzibar," she repeated softly. "Most people who come here do not know."

**Panel 7** — John sitting down on a wooden box beside the stall without quite deciding to, looking up at Saif eagerly.
> "You must have stories." "Many." "Will you tell us?" "Yes. Slowly."

**Panel 8** — Saif seated, speaking quietly to the group — Stone Town's market, cloves on ships, Swahili and English learned from sailors; the children gathered silently around, Malood sitting calm and attentive at his feet.
> He told them, quietly, about Zanzibar, and the smell of cloves on ships.

**Panel 9** — Saif looking directly at John and Safiya, delivering his closing thought with quiet weight.
> "Language is not a thing you carry. It is a thing that carries you. You will see."

**Panel 10** — Saif rising to leave with a small smile and goodbye; Safiya standing still afterward, turning to Thuraya with a question.
> "How many more people like him does this village have?" "Every one of them. You have only met three."

**Panel 11** — Evening: the truck rumbling back toward Al-Qurawashiyah under an orange sky, children quieter, Mansoor asleep against Ahmed's shoulder, Malood curled against John's boot, purchases stacked between them.
> Slower now, under a sky beginning to turn orange.

**Panel 12** — Safiya writing by fading light in a small notebook; John reading over her shoulder and silently placing his hand on hers.
> "It is simply a country that keeps itself in very small places."


---


## Palm Five — The Souq, the Children, and the First Pickup

### Scene 1 — The Village Before the Village Woke

**Panel 1** — Interior of a small clay-walled house before dawn. Safiya lies still in bed, eyes open, listening; John asleep beside her, one arm across the empty space where she'd been. Thick clay walls, small window, dim pre-dawn light.
> "Something had woken her, and it had woken her gently."

**Panel 2** — Close on Safiya's face, eyes closed, concentrating — visualize the falaj's water sound, distant doves, a rooster, a woman humming, as faint layered motifs around her head (thought-cloud style).
> "One sound at a time, the morning assembled itself around her."

**Panel 3** — Safiya rises carefully from the bed, tucking the sheet around John's shoulder, bare feet stepping onto a woven bisat rug on a cool mud floor.
> She steps onto the bisat, careful not to wake him.

**Panel 4** — Safiya at the small window, wrapped in her shawl and scarf, looking out at a sky that is pale, undecided silver — not night, not morning.
> Fajr — the in-between hour with no English word.

**Panel 5** — Safiya steps outside her door alone into the cool, clear pre-dawn air, scarf pulled over her head, the village silhouetted around her.
> The air outside was different — cooler, clearer.

**Panel 6** — Wide village shot: Umm Hamad walking the narrow path with an empty clay jar balanced on her shoulder, heading toward the falaj; smoke curling faintly in the distance.
> A woman with an empty jar, walking toward the falaj.

**Panel 7** — A man carrying a bundle of qit (fodder) on his shoulder passes Safiya, inclining his head slightly in a spare, wordless greeting.
> He inclined his head — a greeting with no unnecessary motion.

**Panel 8** — The small mosque at the village center, unamplified adhan rising into the still air; Safiya stands listening, still, respectful, not praying.
> She was not praying — but she was being given something.

**Panel 9** — Thuraya appears behind Safiya, already dressed and scarved, holding a small steaming cup of qahwa, unsurprised to find her there.
> "You are up." / "The village wakes people sometimes."

**Panel 10** — Thuraya hands Safiya the cup; both women stand together in the strengthening light, Thuraya smiling with quiet promise.
> "Today I will show you the falaj." / "You have seen the surface of it."

### Scene 2 — The Women at the Water

**Panel 1** — Safiya and Thuraya walk together toward the main falaj channel; behind them the village waking — men talking, a tea glass clinking, a door scraping. Sky now committed to sunrise pink.
> The sky had committed to a colour — the working pink of sunrise.

**Panel 2** — Wide shot of the falaj at its "women's hour": six women spaced along the stone channel, each at her own task, calm and unhurried, no children, no chaos.
> Six women, each at her own station, each at her own task.

**Panel 3** — Close on the older woman with hennaed hands lowering a clay jar into the channel by rope, watching the women downstream rather than her own hands.
> She watched the channel further down — to make sure nothing flowed the wrong way.

**Panel 4** — Safiya and Thuraya standing at the edge, Safiya asking a question, gesturing toward the ordered line of women; Thuraya answering with a small economical wrist gesture.
> "How do they know the order?" / "The falaj has laws. Older than any of us."

**Panel 5** — Diagram-like visual moment: the channel shown in sequence — drinking, wudu, cooking, washing, clothes, pots, then the water flowing on to the aflaj for palms and farms.
> Drinking first. Then wudu. Then cooking. Then washing. Last, the farms.

**Panel 6** — Safiya's realization face, looking at the channel and the child at the end rinsing a cloth; Thuraya watching her with quiet teacherly approval.
> "It is the same channel. But it is not the same water."

**Panel 7** — The hennaed woman looks up, smiles, and calls out to them; Thuraya leans in to translate quietly to Safiya.
> "Welcome, ya Safiya. She has seen you from her window."

**Panel 8** — The older woman speaks again, longer, gesturing warmly toward Safiya's face and eyes; Safiya laughs quietly, careful not to disturb the hour.
> "You have English skin but your eyes are like an Omani."

**Panel 9** — Final wide shot: Safiya watching the women's practiced, unhurried rhythm — dipping, wringing, folding — in the silver-pink light, understanding she has crossed a second threshold.
> A second threshold, older than the market's.

### Scene 3 — John Enters the Water

**Panel 1** — Mid-morning falaj, now full of noise and children: Mansoor and Khalil already in the water, three smaller boys splashing, Ahmed seated dry on the stone edge reading, Noura lecturing with arms folded, three girls (Mathila, Rahma, Marwa, Salima) clustered talking, Malood the goat drinking proprietarily in the middle of the channel.
> The falaj was the children's kingdom now.

**Panel 2** — John and Safiya arrive with Thuraya, John relaxed and freshly rested, unaware anything has already happened without him.
> John had slept well — and missed the fajr hour entirely.

**Panel 3** — John crouching at the channel's edge, warning Safiya with mock solemnity before he acts.
> "I would like you to remember that I have been warned."

**Panel 4** — John plunges both hands into the water, visible shock rippling up his arms and shoulders as the cold hits him.
> The water hit him with its absolute, uncompromising cold.

**Panel 5** — John splashes the cold water onto his face repeatedly, water running down his chin and collar, eyes shut, completely unguarded.
> He washed his face — because he could not decide anything else.

**Panel 6** — John looks up, dripping, to find every child in the falaj frozen, staring at him in stunned silence.
> Every child in the falaj was watching him.

**Panel 7** — Mansoor, waist-deep, pointing at John and shouting joyfully as the children erupt into noise and laughter around him.
> "MR. JOHN! YOU ARE ONE OF US!"

**Panel 8** — Down the path, a cluster of older village women pause and call out comments about John, amused and warm; Thuraya translating, hesitating slightly before finishing the sentence.
> "A man who washes in the falaj belongs a little to the village."

**Panel 9** — John stands very still, moved; he raises a hand slightly toward the women, not waving, just acknowledging. Safiya beside him rests her hand quietly at the small of his back.
> He raised his hand — not waving, just acknowledging.

**Panel 10** — Thuraya, watching both of them, speaks softly, almost to herself, her face open.
> "After this, you are not a stranger. You are a character."

### Scene 4 — Why We Say Good

**Panel 1** — The group moves from the falaj to a large old ghaf tree with wide shading branches; the ground beneath packed hard from years of use, like a natural classroom.
> A classroom long before any classroom had a wall.

**Panel 2** — Children settling under the tree: Mansoor sitting too close to Ahmed, Ahmed shifting away, Mansoor following, Ahmed sighing.
> Ahmed, once again denied the dignity of solitude.

**Panel 3** — John kneels and draws two words in the dust with a stick — "Hello" and "Good morning" — Thuraya standing by, giving a small teacherly nod of approval.
> He drew, in the dust: Hello. Good morning.

**Panel 4** — Children repeating the words in ragged unison — Mansoor too loud, Khalil in a funny voice, Ahmed precise, Mathila whispering to Rahma first to check she has it right.
> "Hello." "Good morning." — each child, in their own way.

**Panel 5** — Noura raises her hand with a thoughtful, deliberate expression, the other children watching her expectantly.
> "Why we say good? Why not just — morning?"

**Panel 6** — John, caught off guard, looks toward Thuraya for help; Thuraya only smiles, offering nothing — the question is his.
> He had never been asked this question before.

**Panel 7** — John admits honestly, in front of all the watching children, that he does not know — Ahmed looking on with quiet alarm at a teacher without an answer.
> "I do not know the answer. But I will tell you what I think."

**Panel 8** — John speaking earnestly to the circle of children under the tree, gesturing gently, sincere.
> "Good morning says: the sun is up, and I am glad you are here."

**Panel 9** — Noura turns to the small boy beside her and says "Good morning" firmly, gift-like; the boy, confused, then smiling, echoes it back in careful English. The children begin passing the phrase around the circle.
> "GOOD MORNING." — a small gift, handed around the circle.

### Scene 5 — Malood Takes the Page

**Panel 1** — Quiet aftermath under the ghaf tree: Safiya writing in her notebook, Thuraya reading over Ahmed's shoulder, John looking pleased with himself, a breeze stirring the dry leaves.
> The morning, having handed over its gifts, settled into its middle hour.

**Panel 2** — Malood the goat trots into frame from the falaj path, eyeing John's bag with sudden, total focus.
> Into all of this — like a small, horned comet — Malood.

**Panel 3** — Malood leaps lightly onto John's bag and seizes a corner of paper in his teeth, already turning to run.
> He seized the corner of a piece of paper in his teeth.

**Panel 4** — Freeze-frame beat: the children and adults frozen mid-motion, stunned, as Malood bolts down the path with the paper flapping from his mouth.
> Nobody moved. Malood was already halfway down the path.

**Panel 5** — John, shouting his goat's name in disbelief, the whole scene erupting into motion — children scrambling up around him.
> "MALOOD."

**Panel 6** — Noura springs up first, declaring she'll catch him, already running; behind her a cascading chase — Khalil, the smaller boys, Mansoor, the girls' cluster, then a reluctant Ahmed closing his exercise book.
> "I will catch him!" — and she was already running.

**Panel 7** — John himself joins the chase at the rear of the ragged line, shouting English words at a goat that has no interest in learning them; Safiya and Thuraya stay behind under the tree, watching and laughing.
> The goat ahead, the children behind, the Englishman at the rear.

**Panel 8** — Ahmed alone, walking calmly in the opposite direction from the chase, unhurried, thinking.
> "I walked in the other direction. Goats always go back to qit."

**Panel 9** — Ahmed returns to the tree first, calmly carrying the recovered (slightly chewed) paper, handing it back to John's place without ceremony, then sitting to resume his exercise book exactly where he left off.
> He handed the paper back, without ceremony, and kept reading.

**Panel 10** — Safiya and Thuraya exchange a look, then question Ahmed together; Ahmed answers simply, without pride, while the rest of the chase returns hot, laughing, and defeated in the background with a second stolen paper story from Sadoo's shop.
> "Will you be a teacher when you are older?" / "I will see."

### Scene 6 — The River That Is Not a River

**Panel 1** — Late afternoon light, long gold lines through the palm fronds. Safiya and John walk slowly home along the falaj path, tired and content; Thuraya has gone her separate way.
> The village, having spent its midday loudly, entered its quieter hour.

**Panel 2** — The pair pass the now-empty spots that held the day's scenes — the women's station, the children's swimming place, the ghaf tree — each quiet and still.
> Each spot now held the faint echo of the hour it had belonged to.

**Panel 3** — Safiya and John sit together on the low stone edge of the falaj channel, close enough to hear and touch the water, the channel quiet, alone with itself.
> "I want to sit here for a moment." / "Here."

**Panel 4** — Close on the water in the channel, calm and continuous, catching the late gold light — a visual montage suggestion of all the day's uses (drinking, wudu, bathing, washing, John's face) layered faintly in the flow.
> Nothing was lost. Nothing was wasted. The water simply circulated.

**Panel 5** — Safiya, thoughtful, turning to John, recalling her first-week judgment about the falaj.
> "Do you remember what I said the first day? 'It cannot be clean.'"

**Panel 6** — Safiya dips one finger into the water and lifts it, watching a single drop fall back, explaining her realization to John.
> "In London, clean means separated. Here, clean means ordered."

**Panel 7** — John looking out at the long channel toward the palms, absorbing what she's said, quiet and serious.
> "It is not dirtier than London. It is cleaner. Differently cleaner."

**Panel 8** — The two of them sitting in silence as the light lowers further, the first far-off notes of the Maghrib call beginning to warm in the distance.
> "We moved to another world," she said.

**Panel 9** — John reaches for Safiya's hand and holds it with both of his, wordless, echoing an earlier memory of her father and the pen.
> He held her hand long enough for something to pass between them.

**Panel 10** — Night interior: their small clay house lit by a single hanging qandeel lamp. Safiya writes three careful sentences in her notebook with her father's Cartier pen; John reads over her shoulder, then adds one line of his own and closes the notebook. In the corner, Malood the goat lies curled and asleep on the bisat, one ear twitching.
> "Tomorrow, we teach again." — and the falaj, outside, continued.


---


## Palm Six — A Fan, A Bird, and a Lesson on Tongues

### Scene 1 — The Dust That Follows

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot of Al-Qurawashiyah at mid-afternoon: low clay houses, hard sunlight, shadows shrunk tight against the walls, the whole village looking dozy and still.
> "Even the shadows had decided to wait this hour out."

**Panel 2** — Sophia stands outside her and John's small clay house, beating a thin carpet draped over a low wooden beam; a pale cloud of dust puffs up around her.
> Sophia beats the dust — and the dust keeps winning.

**Panel 3** — Close on Sophia's hands, turned palm-up, now grey-brown with dust; her expression is bemused, slightly defeated.
> "This dust is not normal. I am sure it follows me."

**Panel 4** — John sits nearby on a wooden box against the wall, a shoe between his knees, awl in hand, absorbed in repairing it without help.
> John, unbothered, looks up slowly, ready with a reply.

**Panel 5** — John, still seated, delivers his line with mock solemnity; Sophia stands with lowered carpet, unimpressed.
> "This is not dust. This is history."

**Panel 6** — Sophia, arms crossed, giving John a flat, patient, long-married stare.
> "I am not — wearing history."

**Panel 7** — A hot breeze stirs the dust in the empty compound; a dove sits on a rooftop, calling slowly and sparingly.
> A hot breeze that only moves the heat around, never cools it.

**Panel 8** — Sophia lifts her head toward the sound of distant laughing, shouting, and a bright playful scream carried down the white dust road.
> "Something is happening."

**Panel 9** — John stands, brushes uselessly at his dusty trousers, and offers Sophia his hand; they set off together down the path toward the noise.
> "In this village, something is always happening."

### Scene 2 — The Girls Who Were Flying

**Panel 1** — Sophia and John arrive at the edge of an open sandy area between the houses and stop, taking in the scene: about eight girls, ages thirteen to sixteen, in bright red, green, orange, and saffron dresses, running and laughing.
> The sound grew the way sound grows when you approach a flock.

**Panel 2** — Two parallel lines drawn in the sand, five steps apart; one girl stands guard in the middle while the others try to dart across without being touched.
> A game of Leteg — lines in the sand, one girl guarding the middle.

**Panel 3** — A taller girl in a long green braided-hair dress leaps, nearly topples sideways, catches herself on the sand with one hand, and sprints away laughing.
> She almost fell — and laughed at her own near-disaster.

**Panel 4** — Sophia watches wide-eyed from the edge of the sand, unable to follow the rules everyone else seems to know instinctively.
> "This looks dangerous."

**Panel 5** — John tilts his head, studying the game with the look Sophia recognizes as pre-lecture.
> "No. This looks educational." / "Everything you find educational, I find dangerous."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya arrives behind them unhurried, a small woven basket on her arm, smiling at the sight of the girls.
> "You found the girls."

**Panel 7** — Sophia, delighted and baffled, watches the girls dart across the lines; Thuraya's smile carries an old, private memory.
> "They are flying." / "They are playing Leteg. We all played this when we were young."

**Panel 8** — A confident gap-toothed girl stops mid-game, spots Sophia and John, and calls out; the other girls take up the chant.
> "Come! Come play!"

**Panel 9** — Sophia points at herself in disbelief, then turns a pleading look to Thuraya, who wears the calm face of someone who has already decided.
> "I think this is a bad idea." / "It is a very good idea."

### Scene 3 — The Game is Honest

**Panel 1** — Sophia bends to remove her shoes at the edge of the sand, feet bracing against the warm ground.
> "This is a very bad decision."

**Panel 2** — The gap-toothed girl takes Sophia gently by the elbow, leads her to the line, and mimes the rules with quick hand gestures.
> Stand here. Wait. When she moves, you move.

**Panel 3** — Sophia jumps too early, landing squarely on the forbidden line; the girls around her erupt in cheerful, urgent correction.
> "No! No! Not yet!"

**Panel 4** — Second attempt, full commitment, also wrong; the girls collapse into each other laughing, one sitting down in the sand.
> Tender laughter — the delight of watching an adult fail like a child.

**Panel 5** — Sophia, hands on hips, addresses the sky in mock complaint while John watches from the sidelines, arms folded, insufferably calm.
> "This game hates me." / John: "No. The game is honest."

**Panel 6** — Sophia sets herself again, this time watching only the middle girl's eyes and shoulder-tilt, reading her weight.
> She watched the middle girl's eyes, her weight, the tilt of her shoulder.

**Panel 7** — Sophia clears both lines cleanly in one continuous motion, pivoting mid-crossing without falling.
> Her foot cleared the first line — her third step crossed the second.

**Panel 8** — Sophia stands triumphant in the sand, both arms raised.
> "I did it!"

**Panel 9** — The girls clap and cheer genuinely; the gap-toothed girl touches Sophia's arm; John and Thuraya both smile with quiet pride.
> "Good. Good."

### Scene 4 — Darwazat Mutrah

**Panel 1** — A small quick-footed girl shouts a new game's name and the whole group scatters at once, running in scrambled, overlapping directions.
> "Now we play Darwazat Mutrah!"

**Panel 2** — Mansoor appears abruptly among the running girls, already sprinting and shouting, as if he had always been part of the game.
> "I AM FAST!"

**Panel 3** — Mansoor's small feet lose the fight with the deep sand; he goes down sideways, arms out, in a burst of dust.
> On the fourth second, he fell — spectacularly.

**Panel 4** — Mansoor sits up with sand in his hair and mouth, spits dramatically, then laughs at himself as two girls check on him.
> His one reliable grace: laughing at himself.

**Panel 5** — Sophia retreats from the chaos to a tree at the edge of the sand, plants her back against the trunk, and folds her arms.
> "I am not a teacher today. I am a tree."

**Panel 6** — Every runner freezes mid-motion for a beat — then the whole sandy area bursts into laughter, welcoming the joke.
> "She said: then we will play around the tree."

**Panel 7** — The girls and Mansoor weave and run in loops around Sophia at the tree trunk; she stands still, arms still folded, unmoved.
> She had become part of the game without playing it.

**Panel 8** — The game's energy finally drains; girls sit down one by one on the warm ground, Mansoor flopping onto his back like a small dusty star.
> The afternoon itself had decided the game was over.

**Panel 9** — Sophia leaves the tree and lowers herself onto the ground beside the girls; a puff of pale dust rises from her dress as she sits.
> She looked at her hands, her legs — thoroughly, comprehensively dirty.

**Panel 10** — Sophia gestures at her dust-covered self with dry humor; a girl answers in careful English; the group points as one toward the water channel.
> "I am a very dirty tree." / "You must wash." / "In the falaj."

### Scene 5 — The Majaza

**Panel 1** — Sophia's tired, dusty face lights up at the mention of water.
> "Nice! I want to swim!"

**Panel 2** — Every girl reacts in unified horror, hands thrown up; Mansoor, still lying down, joins in without knowing why.
> "NOOOOO!"

**Panel 3** — Thuraya laughs properly, hand over her mouth, then explains gently to a bewildered Sophia.
> "Women do not swim in the falaj. Women go to the majaza."

**Panel 4** — The group — Thuraya, Sophia, and several older girls — walks a narrow path beside the falaj to a small stone building with a single blue wooden door.
> "You will see."

**Panel 5** — Sophia steps inside a modest stone chamber: a shallow channel of clear falaj water cuts across the floor, and the roof stands open to the pale sky above a low privacy wall.
> A women's bath-house older than any plumbing she had known.

**Panel 6** — Three women already inside — one about sixty, two younger — look up and greet Thuraya warmly, then smile at Sophia.
> "Come. We will help."

**Panel 7** — The older woman works a pale-green sidr paste into Sophia's hands, arms, and the back of her neck, foaming gently.
> The sidr smelled like a garden more than a bathroom.

**Panel 8** — A younger woman applies fragrant sandalwood paste in a small circle to the inside of each of Sophia's wrists.
> A smell that would follow her the rest of the day.

**Panel 9** — The older woman leans in close, steadying Sophia's face, and draws a careful line of kohl along her lower and upper eyelids.
> She drew the kohl, very carefully, along each eye.

**Panel 10** — A younger woman holds up a small round hand mirror; Sophia examines her transformed, dark-eyed reflection and asks a question the women answer only with a knowing, silent smile.
> "Do I look beautiful?"

### Scene 6 — The Bee, the Kohl, and the Goat

**Panel 1** — Outside the majaza, John waits restlessly under a neem tree, tossing small stones at a wall, thoroughly out of patience.
> He had exhausted his patience for standing, sitting, and leaning.

**Panel 2** — John suddenly screams and bolts into a frantic loose circle, slapping and jumping, something having gotten into his trouser leg.
> "AAAAAA!" — a stripped, unprepared, animal scream.

**Panel 3** — Thuraya sprints out of the majaza toward him as he hops and slaps at his calf.
> "A bee! A bee? A wasp?" / "I don't KNOW!"

**Panel 4** — John stands unnaturally still, forcing composure, sneaking anxious glances at the blue majaza door.
> Attempting a kind of Britishness about the whole thing.

**Panel 5** — The majaza door opens; Sophia steps out proud and glowing, unaware that her kohl has melted into long dark streaks running down both cheeks and across her forehead.
> She looked like a very angry ghost.

**Panel 6** — John, Thuraya, the girls, and even a nearby goat all freeze mid-motion, staring at her in stunned silence.
> Every girl, every woman, every goat — stopped.

**Panel 7** — John collapses backward into the dust, laughing with his whole chest; Thuraya kneels beside him worriedly, then starts laughing too.
> He laughed until he could not stand.

**Panel 8** — Sophia touches her own face in alarm, sees her fingers come away black, and a girl quickly hands her the small mirror.
> She looked. She screamed.

**Panel 9** — Sophia spins on her heel and marches back through the blue door, declaring loudly, leaving John still wheezing on the ground.
> "I wash my face! I wash my hands! I wash EVERYTHING!"

**Panel 10** — Later, kohl-free and composed, Sophia and John walk home past the quieter falaj, where a small unbothered boy splashes freely in the channel.
> "He swims in the falaj." / "I wash my hands in the falaj. I do not swim."

**Panel 11** — At their house in the golden evening light, the goat Malood bursts through the open door, snatches a piece of paper off the table, and dodges Sophia's grasp on his way back out.
> "MALOOD!" / "WHY does this goat love our house?!"

**Panel 12** — That night, John lies on the bisat with his stung leg raised on a folded cloth while Sophia brushes out her damp hair across the room.
> "Today we learned new words." / "Wash. Swim. Hospital. Bee." / "And pain."


---


## Palm Seven — The Three Letters of John

### Scene 1 — Mr. John and the Invisible Pillow

**Panel 1** — Mr. John enters the classroom, shoulders back and chin up, trying to look composed; students at their desks look up as he crosses to the front.
> "A man who had very recently, very completely, lost a fight with a very small enemy."

**Panel 2** — Close-up on John's face: one eye normal and mild, the other swollen shut, puffed like it's "wearing a tiny invisible pillow."

**Panel 3** — Wide shot of the classroom frozen mid-motion — pencils down, notebooks still, every child staring at John's eye.

**Panel 4** — Mansoor stands up on top of his desk, pointing dramatically at John's eye like a herald announcing news.
> "Teacher! Your eye is… BOOM!"

**Panel 5** — The whole class erupts — desks shaking, two boys sliding off a bench, one girl collapsed onto her notebook laughing.

**Panel 6** — Noura in the front row claps twice, delivering her verdict with total confidence.
> "It's true! It's big!"

**Panel 7** — Khalil leans forward, chin in hand, mock-serious expression like a village elder diagnosing a patient.
> "Maybe he cried all night."

**Panel 8** — Ms. Thuraya enters behind John carrying chalk and a notebook; she registers the laughing class, then turns and sees John's swollen eye — a flicker of alarm crosses her face.

**Panel 9** — Thuraya writes "WASPSTING" on the chalkboard in capital letters and taps the board twice for order.
> "Good morning, everyone."

**Panel 10** — Mansoor, still half off his desk, whispers loudly to Ali; John's good eyebrow shoots up in response.
> "He looks like he slept inside a beehive."

**Panel 11** — The class calls out greetings to John, some apologizing for his eye; John gives a small courteous bow in return.
> "Good morning… and sorry for your eye!"

### Scene 2 — Yesterday I Felt Pain

**Panel 1** — Thuraya points to the word "WASP" on the board; the class repeats it back in a ragged chorus of accents.
> "Wasp."

**Panel 2** — Thuraya points to "STING"; the class shouts it so loudly a lizard darts off the outer wall.
> "STING!"

**Panel 3** — John points carefully at his own swollen eye, modeling the sentence slowly for the class.
> "Yesterday… a wasp… stung me."
