**Panel 4** — Thuraya writes two columns on the board — present tense and past tense sentences lined up like mirrors.
> "Today: I feel pain. Yesterday: I felt pain."

**Panel 5** — Students copying into notebooks: Ahmed painstakingly neat, Khalil writing at a careless angle, Mansoor sketching a tiny wasp in his margin.

**Panel 6** — Khalil raises his hand with exaggerated sweetness, setting a grammar trap.
> "Teacher, your eye is swollen… or was swollen?"

**Panel 7** — John sighs wearily and answers with careful precision; the class laughs, warmer this time.
> "It is swollen, and it was swollen."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya announces the day's activity to an excited class — everyone sitting up, buzzing with anticipation.
> "Today, we practice past simple. Everyone will tell a funny story."

**Panel 9** — Quick lineup of reactions: Ali and Ahmed sitting ramrod straight, Khalil striking a performer's pose, Mansoor practically vibrating with energy.

**Panel 10** — Thuraya scans the room and points at Ahmed to begin.
> "Ahmed. You start."

### Scene 3 — The Mijz

**Panel 1** — Ahmed stands, hands clasped behind his back, adopting a formal, meeting-room posture.
> "Yesterday, I… went to the farm."

**Panel 2** — Ahmed narrates with growing momentum — playing, running, climbing, falling — while Khalil whispers a jab to his neighbor.
> "He fell because he is too straight."

**Panel 3** — Ahmed clutches his stomach dramatically; Noura gasps aloud from her seat.
> "Then I got pain in my stomach." / "Oh no!"

**Panel 4** — Ahmed lists relatives' remedies one by one, building suspense, then pauses dramatically before the big reveal.
> "My uncle said, 'You need… mijz.'"

**Panel 5** — The whole class lets out a long collective "Ooooooh!"; John leans toward Thuraya, puzzled, and she answers carefully.
> "It is… an old treatment. Traditional."

**Panel 6** — Ahmed holds up one finger like a lecturer; Thuraya turns to the board and writes a vocabulary list (heat, burn, treat, hurt, cure, traditional medicine).
> "They heated it."

**Panel 7** — Ahmed mimes his uncle building a fire and heating the mijz until it glows red; Khalil jokes to Ali and Mansoor sits up indignant.
> "I don't lie! I just… change the truth!"

**Panel 8** — Ahmed re-enacts sitting bravely, closing his eyes, bracing himself.
> "My grandmother said, 'Be strong.' I said… 'Ready.'"

**Panel 9** — Climax: Ahmed pinches his fingers like a hot rod and brings them to his stomach, making the sound effect; Khalil tips off his bench, Mansoor collapses in mock agony.
> "Then… TSSSSS!"

**Panel 10** — Ahmed leans back holding his stomach, searching for the perfect comparison; the class roars, Thuraya fights back a smile.
> "I felt like… I became a chicken."

**Panel 11** — Thuraya raises a hand to settle the class; Ahmed supplies the correct past-tense verb and Noura claps approval.
> "I screamed." / "Good verb!"

**Panel 12** — Ahmed sits down to applause; John, stunned, raises his hand like a student, then touches his own swollen eye.
> "This is real? You burned him?" / "I prefer… hospital."

### Scene 4 — One Sentence, Mansoor

**Panel 1** — Mansoor leaps to his feet the instant John says "hospital," bursting with his own story.
> "Teacher! I went hospital one time!"

**Panel 2** — Thuraya points at him with practiced authority, cutting him off before he starts.
> "Sit. One sentence only."

**Panel 3** — Mansoor sits but keeps talking rapid-fire, listing out a whole mini-story anyway.
> "I went hospital… I cried… I ran away…"

**Panel 4** — Thuraya's voice turns sweetly firm; Mansoor freezes mid-sentence.
> "ONE sentence."

**Panel 5** — Mansoor looks up at the ceiling, down at the floor, then at Thuraya, visibly deliberating over which single sentence to choose.

**Panel 6** — Mansoor delivers his verdict; the class laughs at the sheer economy of it.
> "I cried."

**Panel 7** — Thuraya writes five corrected sentences on the board, each marked with a checkmark, and the class reads them aloud together.
> "✅ Yesterday, I went to the farm."

**Panel 8** — Safiya, watching quietly from the back of the room, observes Thuraya weaving the morning's chaos into a clean grammar lesson (narration beat, no dialogue needed but include a line).
> "An extraordinary teacher. And an even better diplomat."

**Panel 9** — Thuraya turns to John and invites him to contribute his own past-tense sentence.
> "Your turn. One sentence about yesterday."

**Panel 10** — John composes his sentence carefully in front of the class; Mansoor sits up, delighted, whispering proudly to Ali.
> "A wasp stung me. I ran like Mansoor." / "See? I teach him."

**Panel 11** — Noura raises her hand with a suggestion; Khalil adds his own comparison; the class bursts into laughter and John joins in with a lopsided smile.
> "Like a tired camel."

### Scene 5 — The Ghoul

**Panel 1** — The classroom settles into quiet work — Ahmed rereading his notebook proudly, Noura decorating her page, Mansoor repeating his one sentence to anyone nearby, Thuraya circulating between desks.

**Panel 2** — John, more relaxed now, writes his own sentence on a clean page, about to ask Thuraya a small grammar question.

**Panel 3** — Khalil leans forward casually, delivering unsettling news in a calm voice; the whole room goes silent, every face turning to John.
> "Teacher, last night… we saw a ghoul."

**Panel 4** — John freezes, eyes wide with imagined horror, picturing a monster from old Arabian Nights stories.
> "A… what?"

**Panel 5** — Thuraya quickly steps in to clarify with Khalil, who nods cheerfully, unaware of the misunderstanding.
> "Do you mean snake?" / "Yes. Ghoul."

**Panel 6** — John's face drains of color, his one working eye wide with worry.
> "A snake? In the village?"

**Panel 7** — Ali and Noura chime in helpfully — Noura waving her arm in a long, ribbon-like snake motion.
> "It moved like this."

**Panel 8** — John instinctively steps backward and bumps into the blackboard, a small cloud of chalk dust rising around him, unnoticed by him.

**Panel 9** — Thuraya calmly writes a new vocabulary list on the board: snake, afraid, dangerous, near, outside, inside.

**Panel 10** — John's eye stops on the last word; Thuraya pauses, then answers with a mischievous glint.
> "Inside?" / "Sometimes… yes."

**Panel 11** — John recites his grim summary of the day with resigned calm; Thuraya laughs warmly at the line.
> "Tonight… I will be eaten." / "Not eaten. Just… frightened."

### Scene 6 — Animals Do Not Knock

**Panel 1** — Abu Salim, the elderly caretaker, rings the small brass hand bell out in the school yard.

**Panel 2** — Students burst out of the classroom in a chaotic wave, calling goodbyes over each other.
> "Bye, teacher! See tomorrow!"

**Panel 3** — The empty schoolyard falls quiet, dust settling in the aftermath of the rush.

**Panel 4** — John stands motionless at the front of the classroom, chalk dust still faint on the back of his shirt, board still covered in past-tense verbs; Thuraya calmly packs her chalk bag nearby.

**Panel 5** — John hesitantly asks his question, choosing his words with visible care.
> "In your village… do snakes come inside houses?"

**Panel 6** — Thuraya shoulders her chalk bag, adjusts her scarf, and moves toward the door, pausing to answer.
> "You live near farms. Near the falaj. Sometimes… animals visit."

**Panel 7** — At the doorway, Thuraya turns back with a small grin before stepping out.
> "And sometimes… they don't knock."

**Panel 8** — John alone in the empty classroom, listening to the falaj and a bird outside, visibly unsettled by the silence.

**Panel 9** — John walks home slowly under the still-high sun, distracted, his mind clearly elsewhere — imagining long, ribbon-like shapes.

**Panel 10** — Night: John lies on his bisat staring up at the ceiling — wooden beams, packed palm fronds, a single qandeel lamp flickering in the corner.

**Panel 11** — Close on John's anxious face as small sounds — a shifting frond, an insect, a shadow in the corner — register as imagined threats.

**Panel 12** — Sophia turns over beside him; a brief drowsy exchange, then she drifts back to sleep while John lies wide awake listening to the breeze and the distant falaj.
> "Why are you not sleeping?" / "I am thinking. About animals. And knocking."


---


## Palm Eight — A Goat Named Malood

### Scene 1 — The Morning Walk to School

**Panel 1** — A dusty village road in Samail at early morning, sun already warm. Mr. John walks slowly, canvas London bag in one hand, rubbing his eyes with the other; his eye is still faintly swollen from a wasp sting.
> "Why does everything here wake up so early?"

**Panel 2** — Behind him, Safiya (Sophia) walks fixing her wind-blown scarf, glancing down at her sandaled feet on the sandy path with a mix of discomfort and affection.
> "This place is beautiful, but my feet are not happy."

**Panel 3** — Ms. Thuraya walks a step ahead of them, laughing without turning her head.
> "Your feet will learn. Everything learns here."

**Panel 4** — Wide shot: the small school comes into view — white walls, a deep-blue wooden door, open windows, no fans or electricity, birds and distant goats implied in the background.
> The school waits, simple and open to the morning.

**Panel 5** — Inside the classroom: Ahmed sits ruler-straight with his slate; Ali doodles an animal instead of writing; Khalil whispers a joke to a laughing neighbor.
> Each child settles into their own private ritual.

**Panel 6** — Noura peppers Salima with rapid questions; Marwa and Rahma share a single pencil between them.
> The children, already there, as always.

**Panel 7** — Mansoor stands triumphantly on his chair while Thuraya, unbothered, delivers the line she's said many times.
> "Sit down, Mansoor."

**Panel 8** — Mansoor sits — then, five seconds later, is shown mid-motion standing right back up.
> Neither of them expected it to last.

**Panel 9** — Mr. John claps twice at the front of the room, brightening despite his sore eye, addressing the class.
> "Good morning, everyone!" — "Good morning, teacher!"

**Panel 10** — Mr. John begins writing on the chalkboard ("He is… She is…") when suddenly the classroom door slams open/shut behind him, startling everyone.
> BANG.

---

### Scene 2 — The Shadow on the Wall

**Panel 1** — Close on the children's startled faces — Khalil's eyes wide, Mansoor ducking behind Ahmed's back.
> All the children jumped.

**Panel 2** — Noura leans in, voice dropped to a whisper, pointing toward the door.
> "It's him…"

**Panel 3** — Safiya turns to Thuraya with a questioning look; Thuraya's expression shifts to quiet, composed alertness.
> "Who?" — "Stay calm. Stay calm."

**Panel 4** — Through the open window, a tall thin shadow glides slowly across the classroom wall as heavy footsteps sound outside.
> THUMP. THUMP.

**Panel 5** — The children whisper fearfully to each other — Ali, Marwa, and Rahma huddled together, faces uneasy.
> "The old man…" "The magic man…" "My mother says he is scary."

**Panel 6** — Mansoor peeks out from behind Ahmed's shoulder, delivering his line with mock authority while Ahmed looks annoyed.
> "My father says he can talk to goats."

**Panel 7** — Mr. John's good eye widens in disbelief; Khalil finally supplies a name.
> "Talk to… goats?" — "Teacher… it is Khalaf."

**Panel 8** — Chaotic overlapping shouts from multiple children, hands gesturing wildly.
> "He is scary!" "He has a big stick!" "He makes smoke!"

**Panel 9** — Mr. John holds up a calm, teacherly hand, trying to restore order.
> "There are no magicians. Only teachers."

**Panel 10** — A loud crash outside sends the whole class into panic — kids diving under desks, Mansoor pulling Ahmed's bag over his own head.
> CRASH. "ABOOYAAAAAH!"

**Panel 11** — Safiya instinctively grips Mr. John's arm; Thuraya walks calmly to the window and looks out, then begins to laugh.
> "It's just Khalaf."

---

### Scene 3 — The Man in the Doorway

**Panel 1** — Thuraya opens the classroom door to reveal an old man standing outside: very tall, very thin, long white beard, worn clothing, a walking stick in hand, a small cloth herb-bag at his feet.
> A quiet, commanding presence fills the doorway.

**Panel 2** — Close on the old man's face, dry and calm, addressing Thuraya directly.
> "Your goat is eating my plants again."

**Panel 3** — Behind him, Malood the goat is visible in the doorway, chewing contentedly and unrepentant. Safiya's eyes go wide.
> "Oh no. Not again."

**Panel 4** — Thuraya bows her head slightly in respect toward the old man.
> "Sorry, Uncle Khalaf. We will keep him away."

**Panel 5** — Khalaf's gaze moves slowly past Thuraya to study Mr. John and Sophia standing in the classroom doorway.
> "You are the new teachers."

**Panel 6** — Mr. John clears his throat, using his careful teacher-voice, gesturing to introduce himself and his wife.
> "I am John. This is my wife, Sophia."

**Panel 7** — Khalaf's eyes rest on Sophia's face for a long moment; the faintest softening appears at his mouth.
> "You look afraid."

**Panel 8** — Sophia laughs nervously, caught off guard by being read so clearly.
> "No… just… surprised."

**Panel 9** — Khalaf nods once, picks up his cloth bag, and turns to walk away with his slow, heavy stride, disappearing around the school wall.
> He accepted the answer, and walked on.

**Panel 10** — The moment he's gone, the children burst out from hiding — Mansoor emerging from under Ahmed's bag triumphantly, Khalil already talking fast.
> "See? He is strange! He doesn't smile!"

**Panel 11** — Mr. John at the chalkboard, writing simple descriptive sentences about Khalaf in neat capital letters.
> He is old. He is thin. He has a stick. He walks slowly.

**Panel 12** — Ahmed raises his hand, composed and serious, delivering his verdict to a suddenly attentive class.
> "He is not a magician. He is just… quiet." / "A kind old man," Ali later adds.

---

### Scene 4 — The Missing Ring

**Panel 1** — Inside the small clay house that afternoon, Safiya looks down at her bare hand, realizing something is missing.
> "My ring! It's not here!"

**Panel 2** — Mr. John, startled awake from dozing on the bisat, sits up abruptly.
> A husband instantly alert.

**Panel 3** — Montage of searching: checking the table, the small shelf of books, bags, under and inside the bed, the stone bowl, sweeping the kitchen floor twice.
> They searched every surface a ring could plausibly be.

**Panel 4** — Safiya sits down on the bisat, deflated, staring at her bare finger.
> "It's gone."

**Panel 5** — Outside on the path, Khalil and Ali pause mid-walk, overhearing Safiya's voice through the open door, and lean in to whisper.
> "Maybe Khalaf took it." — "The magician!"

**Panel 6** — A small crowd of children has gathered outside the house, talking excitedly among themselves.
> "He took it!" "He will turn it into a frog!"

**Panel 7** — Mr. John steps to the doorway to address the children firmly, though his voice wavers slightly.
> "No one is turning anything into a frog."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya arrives quickly, taking in the scene — worried Safiya, uncertain John, excited children — with one calm glance.
> "We will go to his house. We will ask."

**Panel 9** — The group sets off walking: Thuraya leading, John and Safiya behind her, a trail of curious children following at a cautious distance.
> A small procession heads toward Khalaf's house.

**Panel 10** — They approach a small, low mud house near the farms with a faded blue door. Safiya reaches for Thuraya's hand, visibly nervous.
> "Thuraya, why is my heart beating like this?" — "Because stories are stronger than truth."

**Panel 11** — Thuraya knocks on the faded blue door.
> KNOCK. KNOCK.

**Panel 12** — The door opens slowly; Khalaf stands inside, unsurprised by their arrival.
> "Yes?"

---

### Scene 5 — The Open Hand

**Panel 1** — Thuraya speaks respectfully to Khalaf in the doorway, Safiya standing beside her, the children silent behind them.
> "Sorry to disturb you, Uncle Khalaf. Sophia lost her ring."

**Panel 2** — Khalaf studies them silently for a moment, then turns and disappears into the dark interior of his house.
> He said nothing, and went inside.

**Panel 3** — He reappears in the doorway moments later and opens his closed hand toward them.
> He came back — and opened his hand.

**Panel 4** — Close-up on Khalaf's weathered, thin palm: the small gold ring sits in its center, catching the light.
> Bright and small and entirely impossible.

**Panel 5** — Safiya stares at the ring in his hand, stunned, processing that the man they suspected is the one who found it.
> A silent, re-explained afternoon.

**Panel 6** — Khalaf explains calmly, without any change of expression.
> "It was near my plants. Your goat likes shiny things."

**Panel 7** — Everyone turns at once to look at Malood, who is standing near the door contentedly chewing something, entirely unbothered by the attention.
> All eyes on the goat — who just kept chewing.

**Panel 8** — Safiya laughs, relief washing over her face.
> "Oh… Malood."

**Panel 9** — Khalaf carefully places the ring into Safiya's open palm, meeting her eyes just once.
> "Next time, close your door."

**Panel 10** — Khalaf steps back and closes his door slowly, without ceremony, disappearing inside.
> The door closes, unhurried as ever.

**Panel 11** — Safiya slides the ring back onto her finger as the group turns to walk home; the children behind them are, for once, quiet.
> The small hollow on her finger closed up again.

**Panel 12** — Walking home, Mansoor, Khalil, and Ali speak in turn, each softening their earlier verdict of Khalaf.
> "He is not a magician." "Just an old man." "A kind old man."

---

### Scene 6 — The Notebook and the Thump

**Panel 1** — Night in the small clay house. Mr. John sits at the wooden table under a low, warm oil lamp, notebook open, Parker pen from London in hand.
> The village outside has gone quiet.

**Panel 2** — Close on the notebook page as John writes in his neat, slanted handwriting, then underlines one word.
> "Not every scary story is true. And not every quiet man is bad."

**Panel 3** — John closes the notebook, stands, stretches, and blows out the oil lamp — the room plunges briefly into darkness before moonlight through the window takes over.
> The flame goes out; moonlight remains.

**Panel 4** — John walks toward the bisat where Sophia already lies asleep, cloth pulled to her shoulder.
> He crosses the room toward her.

**Panel 5** — John suddenly stops mid-step, head turned toward the window, listening.
> THUMP. THUMP.

**Panel 6** — A tall, thin shadow passes slowly across the edge of the window outside, same unhurried stride as that morning.
> The same heavy footsteps, out in the dark.

**Panel 7** — John sits up slightly on the bisat, turning to Sophia beside him.
> "Sophia…" — "Yes?"

**Panel 8** — John, hesitant, voices the question he'd promised himself not to ask.
> "…you don't think he really makes smoke, right?"

**Panel 9** — Sophia, eyes still closed, smiles in the dark, her voice calm and unafraid.
> "No. But if he does, please go and talk to him."

**Panel 10** — John pulls the blanket up to his chin, staring at the ceiling, thinking of Khalaf and the returned ring.
> "I will talk to him… tomorrow."

**Panel 11** — Final quiet shot of the house at night, the last thump fading into the steady hush of the falaj.
> The village settles back into its night.


---


## Palm Nine — The Smoke and the Sahir

### Scene 1 — The Truck at the School

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot: early morning Samail, soft honey-colored light, an old dun-colored pickup truck (once white or green, now road-dust colored) parked outside a small school building.
> "The sun was still soft when everyone started to gather near the small school."

**Panel 2** — Mansoor mid-jump in the schoolyard, pure excited energy; nearby Khalil wears a focused, scheming look while young Ali listens in, delighted to be included.
> Khalil was already planning trouble.

**Panel 3** — Ahmed standing apart, holding his small bag with both hands pressed to his chest, careful and composed.
> The bag held three pencils, a slate, a cloth, and one prized pebble.

**Panel 4** — Noura talking rapidly, hands gesturing, at Thuraya, who answers calmly without looking flustered.
> "Where are we going?" "Al-Lajal." "Is it far?" "A little."

**Panel 5** — Marwa, Rahma, and Salima sitting close together, passing dates from a small paper twist in an easy, practiced rotation.
> One date for Marwa, one for Rahma, one for Salima — again.

**Panel 6** — Ali on his third attempt to climb into the truck bed, now dragging over a small stool to boost himself up.
> Ali was trying to climb into the truck first — again.

**Panel 7** — Safiya, in her longest skirt and a scarf she's still learning to tie, catching her own smiling reflection in the dusty truck window.
> She was genuinely smiling, looking forward to the day.

**Panel 8** — Safiya peering into the bare, high-sided open truck bed — no seats, no belts — looking uncertain.
> "Are you sure this is safe?"

**Panel 9** — Mr. John standing by the front tyre, hands on hips, eyes wide and shining, both eyes now fully healed.
> "Adventure! I love adventure."

**Panel 10** — Uncle Nasser, broad and sun-dark-faced, clapping his hands once beside the truck's cab.
> "Yalla! Everyone up!"

**Panel 11** — Children scrambling into the truck bed in different styles — Ahmed composed, Mansoor nearly toppling over the far side, Noura still talking; Thuraya settles into the front seat beside Uncle Nasser.
> The children began to climb, each in their own way.

**Panel 12** — The loaded pickup pulling away from the school, John and Safiya braced on the metal floor among the children, dust rising behind.
> The engine caught, held, and began to move.

### Scene 2 — Al-Naabi and the Fenzuz

**Panel 1** — Wide shot of the truck rolling down a rough, stony brown road, a long plume of dust trailing behind.
> The road was the Omani concept of a road — worn by feet, hooves, and tyres.

**Panel 2** — Mansoor shouting over the engine noise from his spot against the truck's high side.
> "Where are we going?"

**Panel 3** — Thuraya leaning half out of the truck's front window to be heard over the engine.
> "To Al-Lajal. In Wadi Al-Maawil."

**Panel 4** — Ali looking up at Thuraya as she turns partway in her seat to answer him.
> "It is a village. And it is full of my family. The Al-Naabi tribe."

**Panel 5** — John's puzzled expression as a new Arabic-inflected word lands on him.
> "The tribe? What is a tribe?"

**Panel 6** — Thuraya explaining with warmth, ticking off three ideas as the truck rolls on.
> "This is my tribe. This is my name. This is my history."

**Panel 7** — Safiya seated in the truck bed, hands braced against the metal side, quietly absorbing the three separated sentences.
> Arabic had decided, long ago, that they were three different gifts.

**Panel 8** — The truck jolting over a deep ridge — children briefly airborne, laughing; John's bag slides across the floor to Ahmed's feet, who calmly picks it up.
> Every child rose briefly into the air and came down laughing.

**Panel 9** — Thuraya raising her voice over the engine, introducing a new word with visible pride.
> "We also have something called tagheera — a family symbol."

**Panel 10** — Close on Thuraya's satisfied pause before she delivers the tribe's word, children leaning in.
> "For Al-Naabi, the tagheera is fenzuz."

**Panel 11** — John sounding out the unfamiliar word carefully, brow furrowed in concentration.
> "Fen… zoz?"

**Panel 12** — Mansoor sitting up with grave, delighted authority to correct him; John bows his head good-humoredly.
> "Fenzuz," Mansoor said precisely. "Thank you, teacher."

### Scene 3 — My Name Is, I Am From, I Belong To

**Panel 1** — Wide shot from the truck bed: mountains on the left, palm farms edged by falaj channels sliding past on the right.
> Farms appeared on the right, each edged by its own small falaj.

**Panel 2** — Thuraya holding up a hand-written piece of paper with three careful lines for the children to see.
> My name is… I am from… I belong to…

**Panel 3** — Safiya clapping her hands with delight at the lesson Thuraya has just handed her.
> "Great! Let's practice!"

**Panel 4** — Ahmed sitting up straight despite the bumping truck, reciting with deliberate care.
> "My name is Ahmed. I am from Samail. I belong to… my family."

**Panel 5** — Khalil delivering his line with a mischievous grin, the other children already laughing.
> "My name is Khalil. I am from here. I belong to… trouble."

**Panel 6** — Noura speaking earnestly; Thuraya's expression softening as she listens.
> "My name is Noura. I am from Samail. I belong to my mother."

**Panel 7** — Marwa and Rahma, softer-voiced, taking their turns in sequence.
> "I belong to the farms." / "I belong to my grandmother."

**Panel 8** — Mansoor standing up dangerously in the moving truck bed, arms wide, shouting his line.
> "My name is Mansoor. I am from everywhere!"

**Panel 9** — Thuraya, quiet and unshowy amid the lesson she orchestrated, speaking her own three sentences plainly.
> "My name is Thuraya. I am from Al-Lajal. I belong to Al-Naabi."

**Panel 10** — John looking at Thuraya with new attention, both eyes now fully healed and wide.
> "Thuraya, that is a beautiful name."

**Panel 11** — Thuraya's soft, inherited smile as she reveals the family connection.
> "My grandmother's name was also Thuraya."

**Panel 12** — Thuraya gazing out at the road, mountains closer now, a falaj catching sunlight in the distance.
> "Names have stories here."

### Scene 4 — Falaj Al-Lajal

**Panel 1** — John pointing excitedly from the truck at a bright ribbon of water curving along stone banks below, edged by palms.
> "Is that the falaj?"

**Panel 2** — The truck pulling off the road onto flat earth beside a palm grove; Uncle Nasser cutting the engine, sudden quiet settling.
> The specific, sudden quiet that follows the cutting of a loud engine.

**Panel 3** — Wide shot of Al-Lajal village: old settled houses, green palms, water moving between stones.
> Water moving between stones, houses older than Samail's.

**Panel 4** — Children scrambling out of the truck and running straight for the water, unstoppable.
> "Wait! Careful!" Safiya shouted.

**Panel 5** — John briskly pulling off his shoes on a flat stone, grinning, fully certain of himself.
> "Cold water! I am ready!"

**Panel 6** — Thuraya opening her mouth to warn him as John leaps toward the falaj mid-air.
> "John —" Too late. John jumped.

**Panel 7** — John's face mid-shock as hot water closes around his legs instead of the cold he expected.
> The falaj was not cold. The falaj was hot.

**Panel 8** — John launching himself out of the water in a wild leap, arms flailing, shouting.
> "AAAAAAAAAA! WAFAQRIYA!"

**Panel 9** — John standing dripping and red-faced on the stone bank, hopping on one foot, arms raised.
> "It's HOT! The water is HOT!"

**Panel 10** — Children collapsing with laughter — Mansoor rolling backward into the grass, Khalil following, Noura hiding her giggles, Ahmed snorting despite himself.
> They fell on the ground laughing — literally.

**Panel 11** — Mansoor and Khalil calling out from the grass between fits of laughter.
> "Teacher is cooked." "Falaj soup."

**Panel 12** — Thuraya standing with arms crossed on the bank, calm and satisfied, facing a still-dripping John.
> "I told you not to be too excited." "Falaj Al-Lajal is warm."

### Scene 5 — Tell Me About Yourself

**Panel 1** — Safiya climbing down from the truck bed, crossing to the stone bank, arranging her skirt as she sits by the water.
> "Okay. Lesson time!"

**Panel 2** — Children settling into a loose semicircle around her; John joining gingerly at the edge, shoes back on.
> His dignity in slightly better condition than his feet.

**Panel 3** — Safiya looking around brightly at the gathered children.
> "Now, tell me about yourself."

**Panel 4** — Safiya gently pointing to quiet, watchful Ali, the boy who used the stool that morning.
> "You."

**Panel 5** — Ali sitting up carefully, taking a breath before speaking.
> "My name is Ali. I am from Samail. I belong to my family."

**Panel 6** — Safiya's face brightening warmly; Ali smiling, small and relieved.
> "Good!" Ali smiled — small, relieved, proud.

**Panel 7** — Montage of children taking turns around the circle — Marwa, Rahma, Salima, Khalil, Ahmed, Mansoor each speaking in turn.
> Each sentence, with each repetition, grew steadier.

**Panel 8** — A quiet pause falls over the group; John clears his throat, all eyes turning to him.
> "My name is John. I am from London."

**Panel 9** — John looking slowly around the circle of small faces — Mansoor with grass in his hair, Ahmed with his bag, Noura, Ali — then at Thuraya and Safiya.
> A long pause, waiting for his third sentence.

**Panel 10** — John finishing with a small, earned smile; the whole circle bursts into laughter, Mansoor loudest of all.
> "I belong to… this lesson."

**Panel 11** — Behind the truck, in palm shade, a small white horned goat's head appears and steps out with an unhurried, proprietary stride.
> Malood — whom no one had seen climb into the truck — had done so anyway.

**Panel 12** — Malood eyeing John's open bag on the stone bank, where a piece of paper is visible, beginning to walk toward it.
> "MALOOD —"

### Scene 6 — The Practice That Did Not End

**Panel 1** — Wide afternoon shot: the lesson continuing in loops by the warm falaj, Malood being shooed from John's bag yet again.
> Malood being politely escorted away, four separate times.

**Panel 2** — Ali speaking up on a later round, extending his own sentence beyond the template; Thuraya gives Safiya the smallest nod.
> "I belong to my family. And today… I belong to Al-Lajal a little."

**Panel 3** — Rahma speaking plainly, without drama; Safiya's expression tightening gently with feeling.
> "I belong to my grandmother. Who is sick this week."

**Panel 4** — Khalil thinking hard, visibly working for a better answer than usual; Ali looking up at him, Khalil going red.
> "I belong to… my friends," he said, surprising himself.

**Panel 5** — Safiya smiling at Khalil with deliberate lightness, giving him room.
> "Beautiful. That is a beautiful third sentence."

**Panel 6** — Mansoor delivering his line with a triumphant grin, the group laughing again.
> "I belong to the truck."

**Panel 7** — John, dry and composed now, speaking his second-pass sentence shyly; Thuraya giving him a long, considering look and a single nod.
> "I belong to… this country, a little, now."

**Panel 8** — Uncle Nasser laying out a cloth bundle of bread, dates, and cold yoghurt on the stone bank; everyone eating in the afternoon sun.
> The children ate. John ate. Safiya ate slowly and completely.

**Panel 9** — Quiet vignette: Ahmed carefully writing "I belong to my family" on his slate with decorative underlines; nearby Noura listening intently as Thuraya tells her about her grandmother.
> Noura was, for once, listening more than asking.

**Panel 10** — Safiya leaning back against the warm stone bank, eyes soft, notebook left untouched beside her.
> She let a whole afternoon pass through her without trying to catch it.

**Panel 11** — Golden late-afternoon light; Safiya kneeling at the falaj's edge, dipping her fingers into the water as children reluctantly climb back into the truck.
> She held her fingers there, felt the warmth from deep in the earth.

**Panel 12** — Inside the departing truck, Safiya leaning her head against the warm metal side, speaking softly to herself; Thuraya turning to glance at her through the back window with a small smile.
> "My name is Safiya. I am from London. I belong to…" She did not finish.


---


## Palm Ten — Smoke, Truck, and an Englishman Speaking Arabic

### Scene 1 — Before the Bell

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot of the Samail village at dawn: pale sun behind mountains, dusty lanes, doorways just opening, mothers dressing children in the background.
> "The bell was a formality — the village had already woken."

**Panel 2** — Mr. John steps out of the simple mudbrick house doorway, holding a small notebook, squinting up at the pale morning sun.
> John squints at the sun like a man missing glasses he doesn't own.

**Panel 3** — Safiya stands behind John in the doorway, carefully tying her headscarf, glancing down to check the edges sit right.
> Safiya ties her scarf with the pride of a finally-mastered skill.

**Panel 4** — Ms. Thuraya strides up the neighbor's path, cloth bag in hand, a piece of chalk tucked behind her ear, smiling.
> "Good morning," she said, in English, smiling.

**Panel 5** — John grins at Thuraya in the yard; Thuraya laughs her small indulgent teacher-laugh.
> "Today is… Monday?" — "Mr. John, you still need to learn the days."

**Panel 6** — Malood the small white goat hops into the yard, marches to John's teaching bag, sniffs it, and bites the corner; John waves his arms wildly.
> "Hey! No! That is my teaching bag!"

**Panel 7** — Thuraya crouches low beside Malood, patting his flank affectionately while John looks exasperated.
> "He belongs to Aunt Azizah next door. But he loves your house."

**Panel 8** — Safiya leans toward John, whispering, watching Malood with a wry half-smile.
> "He is… too confident."

**Panel 9** — Everyone in the yard turns toward the sound of a distant engine; Thuraya's eyes narrow sharply.
> "Oh no. The municipality truck."

**Panel 10** — A pickup truck rolls slowly down the dusty road, two men in the back beside a large metal tank, a pale cloud of smoke billowing behind it; Thuraya pulls her scarf tight over her face.
> "Pesticide. They spray it to kill insects."

---

### Scene 2 — The Smoke Chase

**Panel 1** — A pack of village boys runs behind the truck, laughing and yelling, chasing the rolling smoke cloud through the street.
> The boys chase the pesticide smoke like it's a game.

**Panel 2** — Mansoor sprints in the thick of the group, small legs pumping, face lit with pure joy inside the pale fog.
> Mansoor runs like he's being chased by a lion.

**Panel 3** — Thuraya, hands cupped to her mouth, shouts across the street toward the running children.
> "MANSOOR! STOP!"

**Panel 4** — John lunges forward, snatches a thin stick from the fence, and raises it overhead like a traffic sign, shouting at the boys.
> "STOP! GO INSIDE!"

**Panel 5** — The boys freeze for a half-second in the smoke, then burst into happier shouting, treating John as part of the game; Mansoor waves at him like a fan waving at an actor.
> The children think Teacher John has joined their game.

**Panel 6** — Safiya stands by the wall, hand clapped over her mouth, shoulders shaking with laughter as she watches John.
> "John, you look like a British police officer."

**Panel 7** — John strides toward the smoke and his foot skids on a wet falaj overflow patch on the ground, arms flailing.
> John's shoe hits a wet patch and slides out from under him.

**Panel 8** — Mid-fall: John's notebook sails out of his hand into the dust and palm leaves; Malood leaps sideways out of the way.
> The notebook flies free as John goes down.

**Panel 9** — John lands face-first in the dusty ground just as the pale pesticide cloud rolls directly over his fallen body.
> The smoke rolls over him as he lies in the dirt.

**Panel 10** — John rises slowly, entirely grey with dust, hair caked, eyes bloodshot and watering — looking like a ghost.
> He looked, exactly, like a ghost that had lost a fight.

**Panel 11** — Safiya screams, then doubles over laughing against the clay wall of the house; Thuraya has a hand over her mouth, failing to stay polite.
> Safiya screams, then collapses laughing against the wall.

**Panel 12** — The children scatter and scream in mock terror at grey, dust-covered John; Mansoor points dramatically as John wipes his eyes and coughs.
> "Aboooyaaah!" — "I am… fine," John says, unconvincingly.

---

### Scene 3 — Shayb Khalaf

**Panel 1** — A tall, thin silhouette emerges slowly from the thinning smoke: an old man with a walking stick, white beard, deeply wrinkled face, sharp eyes.
> A shadow moves at the edge of the smoke — Shayb Khalaf.

**Panel 2** — The children freeze in place, eyes wide, this time with genuine fear rather than play, whispering to each other.
> "Khalaf…" — "The wizard!"

**Panel 3** — Safiya leans toward Thuraya, whispering, watching the children's reaction with curiosity rather than alarm.
> "Why do they look like they saw a monster?"

**Panel 4** — Thuraya murmurs back to Safiya, calm and explanatory, glancing toward the old man.
> "They think he is a sorcerer. He lives alone. People fear him."

**Panel 5** — Khalaf crosses the dusty ground directly to John, grabbing his arm firmly to steady him — rough but not unkind.
> "Stand up properly," he said. Rough — but not cruel.

**Panel 6** — Close on John's dazed, dust-grey face as unexpected Arabic words come out of his mouth, his own eyes widening in surprise.
> John, dizzy, blurts fluent local Arabic without meaning to.

**Panel 7** — Safiya and Thuraya both stare, mouths open — Safiya gasping, Thuraya's jaw dropped.
> Safiya gasped. Thuraya's mouth dropped open.

**Panel 8** — Khalaf stares at John for a long beat, then breaks into a short, surprised laugh, raising his walking stick playfully.
> Khalaf laughs and lifts his stick — a small, amused threat.

**Panel 9** — John answers back instantly in Arabic again, fast and almost polite, like a villager's reflex response.
> The answer comes out of him — still not English.

**Panel 10** — John blinks rapidly, turning to Safiya in open confusion, hand half-raised as if trying to catch his own words.
> "How did I… say that? I don't know those sentences."

**Panel 11** — Safiya bursts into fresh laughter, gripping the wall again; Thuraya tries to look composed but her shoulders are shaking.
> "John, you just threatened-corrected yourself in Arabic!"

**Panel 12** — Mansoor steps forward proudly pointing at grey-dusted John; Thuraya claps once, ushering the children toward the school building.
> "Teacher John… you are… ghost!" — "No. I am teacher." — "Everyone… to school."

---

### Scene 4 — The New Teachers

**Panel 1** — John walks into the dusty classroom later that day, eyes red and puffy, grey dust still clinging in his hair; chalkboard behind him faintly shows yesterday's lesson.
> John enters class still marked by the morning's disaster.

**Panel 2** — Two unfamiliar adults stand near the classroom door: a stern Egyptian man with a sharp moustache, and beside him a sporty woman in a tracksuit.
> Two new teachers wait by the door — unfamiliar faces.

**Panel 3** — The Egyptian man stands upright and formal, addressing the room loudly.
> "I am Mohammadeen. Math teacher."

**Panel 4** — The woman beside him squares her shoulders, equally loud and confident.
> "And I am Fatkat. Sports teacher."

**Panel 5** — Mohammadeen lifts a thin stick as a symbol of authority; the students, including Mansoor, sit up ramrod straight, Mansoor visibly swallowing.
> "Any noise, and I make you quiet faster than lightning."

**Panel 6** — Safiya leans toward Thuraya at the side of the room, whispering, watching the children's reaction.
> "They look scared. And respectful. At the same time." — "That's how it was."

**Panel 7** — John stands at the chalkboard, having written "PAST SIMPLE: Yesterday, I… / He… / They…", the class reciting together.
> "Repeat: Yesterday, I walked."

**Panel 8** — John points to Mansoor, who stands proudly to answer, then frowns in concentration correcting himself.
> "Yesterday, I run." — "Say: I ran." — "I ran."

**Panel 9** — John points to himself with mock-serious self-deprecation; the whole class erupts in laughter, Safiya calling out from the back.
> "Yesterday, I… fell." — "Mr. John looked like a ghost."

**Panel 10** — Safiya stands at the front using hand gestures, teaching safety phrases slowly and clearly while John writes on the board.
> "Don't run behind the truck. Go inside. Close the door."

**Panel 11** — Mansoor raises his hand eagerly, asking about Malood; the class repeats the sentence together.
> "Malood… cough?" — "Malood coughed."

**Panel 12** — John turns toward Thuraya with narrowed, investigative eyes; in the background, quiet Saeed watches everything from the back wall seat, unmoving.
> "I will ask you something," he said, "after class."

---

### Scene 5 — The Forest of Fenzuz

**Panel 1** — Split moment: Safiya sits with children by the warm falaj drilling "my name is, I am from," while some distance away Mr. John walks alone at the edge of the palm grove.
> While Safiya taught by the falaj, John walked off alone, still grumpy.

**Panel 2** — John walks through dappled light between palm trunks, muttering, his expression sour and preoccupied.
> Every step still said: warm falaj.

**Panel 3** — John crouches low in the fallen fronds, examining a scattering of small, distinctive shapes on the ground.
> He had seen something in the trees — small, particular shapes.

**Panel 4** — John's face lights up as he whispers to himself, kneeling and carefully gathering the shapes into his teaching bag.
> "Fenzuz," he whispered — and began to collect them.

**Panel 5** — John walking back toward the falaj with his now-full bag, imagining the moment he'll present his "gift," a small proud smile on his face.
> "I will surprise Thuraya. She will be pleased."

**Panel 6** — At the falaj: Mansoor mid-recitation of "I am from everywhere," Safiya clapping warmly, Thuraya sitting cross-legged on the stone bank looking satisfied.
> The children were finishing their sentences by the water.

**Panel 7** — John arrives and opens his bag toward Thuraya with quiet pride.
> "Look!"

**Panel 8** — Close on Thuraya's face as she peers into the bag — her expression shifting from blank, to surprise, to amused disbelief, then breaking into full laughter.
> Thuraya's face moves from surprise to disbelief to laughter.

**Panel 9** — Thuraya, still smiling warmly, explains gently while John stares down at the bag in dawning realization.
> "You collected my tagheera. Only Al-Naabi can use it." — "Oh."

**Panel 10** — The children around the falaj burst into laughter at John's culturally-mistaken gift; John bows his head with weary grace.
> "I will put them back," he said, "where I found them."

**Panel 11** — Later, in the back of Uncle Nasser's truck at sunset, Mansoor leans toward John with a mischievous question; Safiya and Thuraya listen, smiling.
> "Will you swim again?" — "No." — "Always ask before you jump."

**Panel 12** — That night, John sits alone at the small wooden table by lamplight, writing carefully in his notebook.
> He added one more line: "Never trust Thuraya's smile."

---

### Scene 6 — The Boy Who Collected Secrets

**Panel 1** — The now-empty classroom after the bell: desks still warm, chalk dust settling in the afternoon light; John taps the board idly, Safiya gathers papers, Thuraya cleans the slate.
> The classroom empties, still holding the shape of the children.

**Panel 2** — John turns to Thuraya with quiet seriousness, pointing at his own mouth.
> "Thuraya." — "Yes, Mr. John." — "Explain."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya sets down her slate and sits on the small front stool, considering her words before answering honestly.
> "You have been listening," she said. "More than you know."

**Panel 4** — Thuraya counts off on her fingers the places John has unconsciously absorbed Arabic — market, falaj, street, classroom, mosque.
> "Your ears are smarter than your mouth."

**Panel 5** — John stares at her, processing; Safiya listens nearby with a small smile.
> "So I will keep doing that?" — "If you are lucky, yes."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya turns to include Safiya in the explanation; Safiya smiles, pleased.
> "And your wife will too." — "Good."

**Panel 7** — Cut to outside: the village settling into its afternoon hush, children scattered in small clusters near the mosque wall in the shade.
> The village settled into its afternoon hour.

**Panel 8** — Under a ghaf tree, three boys sit together — two small ones leaning forward eagerly, and quiet Saeed cross-legged between them in a storyteller's pose.
> Saeed sat between them, composed, like a storyteller.

**Panel 9** — Saeed speaks low and measured to the two wide-eyed boys, recounting the morning's events.
> "Teacher John went into the smoke… he was grey. Like a ghost."

**Panel 10** — Saeed leans in, voice dropping for the important part; the two boys go very still, hanging on his words.
> "Shayb Khalaf came. And grabbed him. And Teacher John… spoke Arabic."

**Panel 11** — Back inside the classroom: John asks Thuraya about Khalaf directly; Thuraya's small mischievous smile returns.
> "Tell me about him." — "Tomorrow. You will be ready to hear it."

**Panel 12** — Outside, Saeed stands, brushes off his robe, and walks unhurried toward the next cluster of children to spread his version of the story.
> Saeed carried the day's secrets like small stones, and moved on.


---


## Palm Eleven — Maghsoob — And the Boy Who Watches

### Scene 1 — The Night That Would Not Sleep

**Panel 1** — Interior, simple bedroom in Samail at night; John lies awake in bed, eyes open, staring at a ceiling made of palm fronds, moonlight through a window.
> "John did not sleep well that night."

**Panel 2** — Close on John's face, eyes shut tight, tense; a hazy thought-bubble/overlay of swirling smoke, a stick, and a thin old man with sharp eyes.
> Every time he closed his eyes, he saw smoke, a stick, an old man.

**Panel 3** — John whispering to himself, propped on one elbow, glancing at Safiya sleeping peacefully beside him with her scarf folded neatly by the pillow.
> "This place is doing something to my brain."

**Panel 4** — Wide shot of the quiet village yard at night: a dog in the distance, and Malood the goat curled in Aunt Azizah's yard, twitching unhappily in his sleep.
> Outside, the village was quiet — except for one arguing goat.

**Panel 5** — Vision/dream-like inset: Shayb Khalaf standing calmly near a clay wall, holding his kheyzaran cane, looking exactly as he had that morning.
> Not menacing — just the old man, watching.

**Panel 6** — Same inset, ghostly Arabic script or spoken-word lines floating near Khalaf's mouth, John's face contorted in unease.
> The voice, in his head, in perfect Arabic: "khallik rijal."

**Panel 7** — John sitting bolt upright in bed, eyes wide open, hand raised as if pushing the thought away.
> "No. Enough," he said, in English, out loud.

**Panel 8** — John lying back down, resolute expression, looking toward Safiya's silhouette.
> "Tomorrow, I will ask Thuraya. Everything."

**Panel 9** — Final wide shot: John still awake, staring at the ceiling, the room dim, night stretching on.
> And John lay awake. For a long time.

---

### Scene 2 — Two Missions

**Panel 1** — Morning path to school; John walking with a determined, serious expression, Safiya beside him glancing at him curiously.
> John walked to school with two clear missions.

**Panel 2** — Close on John's face — tight-jawed, focused — with two small thought-icons above his head: a chalkboard and a question mark.
> One: teach English. Two: understand yesterday's Arabic.

**Panel 3** — Safiya teasing him mid-walk, smiling, nudging his arm; John still stone-faced.
> "You look like you are going to arrest someone."

**Panel 4** — John, deadpan, replying without smiling.
> "I am. A mystery."

**Panel 5** — Classroom interior; Thuraya entering cheerfully with books and chalk tucked behind her ear.
> "Good morning," she said, cheerful as always.

**Panel 6** — John giving Thuraya a serious "detective" look; Thuraya calmly setting her books down, unsurprised.
> "We need to talk. About the old man."

**Panel 7** — Thuraya raising an eyebrow, arms crossed lightly, composed.
> "I did not tell you many things. You must be more specific."

**Panel 8** — The classroom door bursts open — Mansoor nearly crashing into a desk, dust and noise, kids flooding in.
> The students ran in — noise, dust, chaos.

**Panel 9** — Ahmed sitting straight "like a ruler"; Khalil whispering to Ali, both laughing mischievously.
> Ahmed sat straight; Khalil and Ali laughed.

**Panel 10** — The girls (Noura, Marwa, Rahma, Salima, Mathila) entering together, huddled, whispering secrets.
> The girls came in together, talking in secret.

**Panel 11** — Saeed alone near the back door, standing still a moment, surveying the room before walking calmly to the far-back desk.
> One boy stood apart, watching before he sat: Saeed.

---

### Scene 3 — Telling Stories

**Panel 1** — John at the chalkboard writing "Today's Topic: Telling Stories" in large letters, class watching.
> Today's Topic: Telling Stories.

**Panel 2** — John writing sequential words on the board: First — Then — After that — Finally.
> "Small words that help us move through time."

**Panel 3** — Safiya near the back of the room, clapping warmly, teacher-style.
> "Good! Today, we talk about… yesterday."

**Panel 4** — Mansoor standing up dramatically, pointing at John, grinning wide.
> "Teacher John fell!" Mansoor announced proudly.

**Panel 5** — John, eyes closed for a beat, absorbing the humiliation with quiet resignation.
> "Yes. Thank you, Mansoor. The village will never forget."

**Panel 6** — Ahmed standing at his desk, hands clasped behind his back like a small man giving a speech.
> "First, the truck came. Then we ran. Finally he became a ghost."

**Panel 7** — The class bursting into laughter; John sighing, rubbing his forehead.
> "Excellent grammar. Terrible story."

**Panel 8** — Noura standing, thinking hard, working through the stages of composing her sentence.
> "Smoke come. Mansoor run like crazy. Teacher John… grey."

**Panel 9** — Safiya nodding happily, encouraging, not correcting the grammar.
> "Good! Very good!"

**Panel 10** — Quick triptych: Khalil acting out rescuing a bird; Ali speaking quietly about staying with his mother; Mathila miming a wave.
> Khalil's rescued bird, Ali's quiet truth, Mathila's wave.

**Panel 11** — Saeed alone at the back desk, arms folded, silent, watching everyone else tell their stories.
> Saeed, in the back, was silent. Watching. Collecting.

---

### Scene 4 — The Maghsoob

**Panel 1** — Empty classroom after the students have run out; John standing firm, facing Thuraya, arms crossed.
> "Now. Tell me the truth."

**Panel 2** — Thuraya smiling sweetly, a knowing glint in her eye.
> "About what?"

**Panel 3** — John, insistent, naming it directly.
> "About Shayb Khalaf. And why I spoke Arabic."

**Panel 4** — Thuraya considering him for a long moment, then nodding decisively.
> "Come. Let's walk."

**Panel 5** — Exterior path past the falaj and trees; Thuraya and John walking together, Safiya staying behind in the classroom organizing books.
> They walked outside, past the falaj and trees.

**Panel 6** — Thuraya leaning against an old, many-times-patched clay wall, looking at the ground, then at John.
> "People here love stories. And people here love fear."

**Panel 7** — Close two-shot, Thuraya's voice dropping, John's brow furrowing with unease.
> "Some say Shayb Khalaf is a sahir." — "A sorcerer."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya, serious and measured, telling the legend: a father handing a child away to devils, the child vanishing.
> "A man must give one of his own children to the devils."

**Panel 9** — Illustrative inset: a dusty, ragged wandering figure with a blank, lost face walking alone between houses.
> "The maghsoob comes back like a dead man. Dirty. His mind eaten."

**Panel 10** — John, visibly cold, a chill passing over him, hand touching the back of his neck.
> John felt cold — not metaphorically.

**Panel 11** — Thuraya leaning in, voice quieter still, almost conspiratorial.
> "A sahir like Khalaf... can put words in your mouth."

**Panel 12** — Thuraya's face breaking into a slow, mischievous smile; John staring at her, tense, unconvinced by his own relief.
> "Yes. Of course I am joking." She laughed properly.

---

### Scene 5 — Meanwhile, Under the Trees

**Panel 1** — Shaded patch behind the schoolyard; Saeed sitting cross-legged with two smaller boys facing him, rapt attention.
> Saeed was not playing. He was talking. Quietly.

**Panel 2** — Saeed mid-story, hands gesturing slightly, the two boys' eyes growing wide.
> "They say Shayb Khalaf is a sahir. A strong one."

**Panel 3** — One boy leaning forward, whispering, alarmed.
> "Gave him where?" — "To the devils," Saeed said serenely.

**Panel 4** — Saeed describing the maghsoob, gesturing vaguely toward the horizon; boys staring, transfixed.
> "The son came back... as a maghsoob. Dusty. Empty."

**Panel 5** — The smaller boy, wide-eyed and alarmed, whispering his question.
> "Did he put words in Teacher John's mouth?"

**Panel 6** — Close on Saeed's face, calculating, a small clever smile forming.
> Saeed considered, then chose the more durable answer.

**Panel 7** — Saeed smiling slyly at the two boys, who sit wide-eyed, nodding slowly.
> "Maybe."

**Panel 8** — Saeed leaning back against the tree trunk, eyes closed briefly, alone with his thoughts.
> He was eight. He had been taught to watch.

**Panel 9** — Saeed standing, brushing off his robe, walking calmly away down the path toward another group of children.
> He walked on to spread the "correct version" further.

---

### Scene 6 — The Afternoon of Shadows

**Panel 1** — Schoolyard, afternoon; John glancing nervously at every shadow and shape around him.
> Every shadow that afternoon looked suspicious.

**Panel 2** — Malood the goat leaping out from behind a water bucket; John jumping backward, both feet off the ground, startled.
> John jumped like he had seen a lion.

**Panel 3** — Safiya laughing at him, amused; John looking embarrassed and unamused.
> "You are becoming very local." — "Very scared."

**Panel 4** — John and Safiya walking home along the familiar path; John's eyes darting to every old man, wall, and tree they pass.
> John looked at every old man. Every wall. Every tree.

**Panel 5** — John suddenly stopping mid-step on the road, staring ahead; Safiya a few paces ahead, turning back.
> His feet had simply stopped, without asking him.

**Panel 6** — Wide shot: Shayb Khalaf sitting by the roadside, old, thin, holding his stick, looking up calmly at John and Safiya.
> Their eyes met. Khalaf said nothing. He simply watched.

**Panel 7** — John giving a small, stiff, polite nod; Khalaf nodding back, expression unreadable.
> John nodded, very politely. Khalaf nodded back.

**Panel 8** — John and Safiya walking briskly away, turning a corner; John exhaling deeply once out of sight.
> Nothing happened. No words came into John's mouth.

**Panel 9** — Safiya linking her arm through John's, both walking toward home in the fading light.
> "Home," she said gently. "Home," John agreed.

**Panel 10** — Night again: John lying awake, staring at ceiling, door, then window, restless.
> "Thuraya is enjoying this too much," he whispered.

**Panel 11** — Dream sequence: a tall, dusty, pale figure resembling Khalaf walking out of a grave along a long path toward the farms, speaking calmly.
> Dream-Khalaf: "Good morning, teacher John. I have something to tell you."

**Panel 12** — Morning: John walking into the classroom holding a small cup of coffee, red-eyed and exhausted; Ahmed noticing with concern.
> "Teacher... you tired?" — "Yes. Very."

**Panel 13** — Mansoor grinning knowingly at John; John lying with a flat, unconvincing denial.
> "You afraid?" — "No," John lied.

**Panel 14** — Saeed in the back near the door, watching the exchange, a small private smile spreading on his face.
> Saeed watched. And smiled. Just a little.


---


## Palm Twelve — A Ring, a Goat, and an Apology

### Scene 1 — Too Quiet a Morning

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot of the village of Samail at dawn: empty sandy lanes, low clay walls, palm trees, only birds and the falaj water visible — no people or animals in motion yet.
> "The day began quietly. Too quietly."

**Panel 2** — Interior, simple room: Safiya sits near a small wall mirror, calmly adjusting her headscarf, unhurried and serene.
> Safiya fixes her scarf, moving slowly, calmly.

**Panel 3** — Same room: John seated nearby with a notebook open on his knee, pen in hand, writing small observations.
> John writes in his notebook at breakfast.

**Panel 4** — Thuraya enters the doorway carrying a stack of books, smiling, glancing between Safiya's scarf and John's notebook.
> "Good morning. You both look… too relaxed."

**Panel 5** — Close on John looking up from his notebook, faint suspicion on his face.
> "Yes. And that worries me."

**Panel 6** — Safiya laughing lightly at John's remark, Thuraya smiling beside her.
> "You are becoming very local."

**Panel 7** — The three of them walking together down the village road toward school; Malood the small white baby goat trots along beside them uninvited.
> Malood follows them like a small white cloud with legs.

**Panel 8** — Wide shot of the quiet road: a woman greets them from a doorway, a boy dashes past with bread, the falaj hums softly in the background.
> The usual noisy morning simply isn't happening.

---

### Scene 2 — The Discovery

**Panel 1** — Classroom interior, students at their slates; Mansoor flips his slate over mischievously while Safiya and Thuraya teach at the front.
> The first two lessons passed almost too normally.

**Panel 2** — Break time: Safiya at the water pot in the corner of the classroom, about to wash her hands.
> Safiya stops. She looks at her hand — again.

**Panel 3** — Close-up on Safiya's bare hand, pale mark where a ring used to sit, her expression freezing.
> "My ring. It is not here."

**Panel 4** — Safiya frantically checking her pocket, then her bag, digging through it a second time.
> Nothing. Nothing. Nothing.

**Panel 5** — Thuraya turning sharply toward her, concerned; John rising from his seat.
> "My ring," Safiya said. "The one from my mother."

**Panel 6** — Mansoor slowly raising his hand among the other seated children, reluctant, everyone watching him.
> "Teacher… maybe Malood eat it?"

**Panel 7** — All heads turn toward the classroom doorway where Malood stands in a triangle of sunlight, jaw working slowly on something.
> Malood, chewing something, minding his own business.

**Panel 8** — Safiya stepping toward Malood carefully, hand outstretched, the goat tensing to bolt.
> "Malood! No, no, no…" — and he ran.

---

### Scene 3 — The Chase

**Panel 1** — Malood sprinting in a wild zigzag across the schoolyard, dust kicking up behind him.
> Malood ran like he had stolen the crown of the king.

**Panel 2** — The whole class of children sprinting after him in a chaotic pack, shouting and pointing.
> "Catch him!" "He ate the ring!" "Stop, Malood!"

**Panel 3** — John running awkwardly, one hand clamped on his loosening trousers, face full effort.
> John ran holding his trousers, refusing to lose them mid-chase.

**Panel 4** — Thuraya farther back, cupping hands to shout after the runners.
> "Careful!" Thuraya's calm teacher-shout cutting through the chaos.

**Panel 5** — Malood leaping cleanly over a low stone wall; children vaulting after him with practiced ease.
> The children jumped the wall like they'd done it all their lives.

**Panel 6** — John awkwardly clambering over the same wall, straining, sweaty, undignified.
> John did not jump. He climbed — slowly, carefully.

**Panel 7** — The group searching empty ground near trees, the falaj channel, and Aunt Azizah's vegetable rows — no goat in sight.
> No white cloud. No chewing sound. Nothing.

**Panel 8** — Safiya standing still, eyes glassy, close to tears; Thuraya's hand resting on her shoulder.
> "We will find it," Thuraya said gently.

**Panel 9** — A small solemn boy at the edge of the gathered children speaking up quietly, others turning to look at him.
> "…Maybe Shayb Khalaf."

**Panel 10** — Close on the children's faces whispering to each other — Mansoor wide-eyed, a girl mouthing "sahir"; John's expression turning cold as Thuraya hesitates beside him.
> "He is sahir…" "His son is maghsoob."

---

### Scene 4 — The Rumour

**Panel 1** — Montage-style village wide shot: women talking at doorways, men at Sadoo's tea shop, mothers at the falaj, all exchanging the same story in different forms.
> By lunchtime, the story was everywhere.

**Panel 2** — Close on a cluster of villagers mid-gossip, exaggerated whispering postures.
> "Shayb Khalaf took the ring." "Malood works for him. He uses magic."

**Panel 3** — Saeed sitting alone beneath the ghaf tree behind the schoolyard, bag on his knees, watching the other children retell the story.
> Saeed said nothing. He only listened — and remembered.

**Panel 4** — Safiya in the classroom, composed face, forcing a small smile toward Mansoor while clearly troubled underneath.
> Safiya kept her face composed, but John could see she was worried.

**Panel 5** — John and Thuraya standing at the edge of the schoolyard, John gesturing emphatically, frustrated.
> "This is crazy. We are accusing an old man because a goat is stupid."

**Panel 6** — Malood lounging smugly in the shade of the low wall, sneezing, utterly unbothered.
> Malood sneezed. "See? Even he agrees."

**Panel 7** — Thuraya sighing heavily, arms crossed, looking older and weary in that moment.
> "People here… look for stories before they look for facts."

**Panel 8** — Safiya standing up straight, resolute expression, the others turning to look at her.
> "Then let us look for facts."

---

### Scene 5 — The Visit

**Panel 1** — John, Safiya, and Thuraya walking together toward the edge of the village; behind them, a cautious line of children watches from a distance.
> The children stayed far, watching, like an audience for a play.

**Panel 2** — Exterior of Shayb Khalaf's small clay house — patched mud walls, a worn blue door, quiet and still.
> Shayb Khalaf's house was small, quiet, made of clay.

**Panel 3** — Close on John's tense face as they approach, sweat on his brow, clearly nervous.
> John's heart beat faster. He did not want to be here.

**Panel 4** — Thuraya knocking on the blue door; it slowly creaks open to reveal Khalaf, thin, holding his stick.
> "Yes?" he said.

**Panel 5** — Khalaf's gaze dropping to Malood, who sits calmly by John's ankle.
> "…This goat. Always hungry."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya speaking gently and respectfully to Khalaf in the doorway, Safiya and John standing behind her.
> "Sophia lost her ring. The children are afraid it came here."

**Panel 7** — Khalaf's face, tired rather than angry, his eyes distant with old weariness.
> "I did not take anything."

**Panel 8** — Khalaf stepping aside from the doorway, gesturing them in without ceremony.
> "You can look."

**Panel 9** — Interior of the simple clay home: a worn mat on the floor, a pot, a few hung clothes, a small shelf with three books including a gold-threaded Qur'an — no jewellery anywhere.
> No shiny things. No jewellery. No ring.

**Panel 10** — John standing in the doorway, torn expression of relief and guilt as he takes in the bare, humble room.
> Relief and guilt, both at once.

---

### Scene 6 — The Truth and the Apology

**Panel 1** — Saeed sitting on a low wall at a bend in the path, waiting calmly as the three adults approach.
> Saeed was waiting, composed and quiet.

**Panel 2** — Saeed standing to speak, the adults gathered close, listening intently.
> "Malood was chewing something white this morning. Near the big stone."

**Panel 3** — Safiya, John, and Thuraya turning and breaking into a run together down the path.
> Without a word, all three turned and ran.

**Panel 4** — They arrive at the big stone to find Malood curled up asleep, looking utterly content.
> They found Malood — sleeping, very happily.

**Panel 5** — John crouching to lift the sleepy goat into his arms, half-apologetic, half-determined.
> "Sorry, my friend — this is an investigation."

**Panel 6** — The group standing in a small circle around Malood, now awake and nibbling a dry leaf on the ground, everyone waiting anxiously.
> The longest fifteen minutes of Safiya's day.

**Panel 7** — Safiya crouched by the goat's droppings, holding up something small and filthy between two fingers, eyes wide with shock and relief.
> "MY RING!" Dirty, but unmistakably hers.

**Panel 8** — John standing with eyes closed, hand on heart, delivering his vow with mock solemnity while Malood looks on.
> "I will never say anything bad about goats again."

**Panel 9** — Malood strutting in a small proud circle in front of the watching children, who stand in a hushed arc — no one laughing.
> Malood looked proud, like a small war hero receiving a medal.

**Panel 10** — The three adults walking back toward Shayb Khalaf's house, Safiya wearing the newly cleaned, gleaming ring.
> "We need to fix this."

**Panel 11** — At Khalaf's doorway: Safiya and John apologizing earnestly, Khalaf's tired face unreadable, then softening slightly as he looks at each of them in turn.
> "We believed a story. Not the truth." / "But you came back. That is… something."

**Panel 12** — Split evening scene: John at a small wooden table writing a single line in his notebook by lamplight; under the ghaf tree, Saeed quietly corrects the two small boys as Malood sleeps nearby.
> "Stories are strong. But truth is stronger."


---


## Palm Thirteen — The Yellow Mask Disaster

### Scene 1 — A Personal Attack from the Sky

**Panel 1** — Wide shot of Samail village glowing under an intense 8am desert sun; heat shimmer visible over rooftops and palm trees.
> "The sun in Samail did not wake up gently. It attacked."

**Panel 2** — Inside a simple mud-brick house, Safiya stands before a small wall mirror, fingertips gently touching her sunburned cheeks and the skin under her eyes, wincing.
> "I think my face is… cooking."

**Panel 3** — Close-up insert: Safiya's face in the mirror, tomato-red and tender-looking.
> Her face was the shade of a tomato left too long on low heat.

**Panel 4** — Outside the house, John stands in a thin strip of shade by the door, sweat-soaked shirt, trying to look composed and failing.
> "This is not sun. This is a personal attack from the sky."

**Panel 5** — School yard: Thuraya arrives calm and unbothered, walking with easy confidence despite the heat.
> Thuraya, as always, seemed to have made peace with the sun.

**Panel 6** — Fatkat bursts in behind her, energetic, in sports clothes, whistle around her neck, fanning herself dramatically with one hand.
> "Ya binti, this sun is not normal. This sun needs discipline."

**Panel 7** — Safiya and Fatkat stand together in the school wall's shade, leaning in to inspect each other's red cheeks like amateur doctors.
> "My face feels like fire." / "Mine too. We are… red."

**Panel 8** — John walks past, glances at the two red-faced women, suppressing a grin.
> "You both look like tomatoes that went to the beach."

**Panel 9** — Group shot: Thuraya and Fatkat excitedly telling Safiya about a woman named Marhoona; Safiya claps happily while John raises a skeptical eyebrow in the background.
> "There is Marhoona. She makes good masks. Real traditional." / "Skin care!"

---

### Scene 2 — The Salon of Marhoona

**Panel 1** — Establishing shot: a small blue-painted door at the end of a dusty path, a modest mud house at the edge of the village near the farms.
> Marhoona's "salon" — a small room behind a blue door.

**Panel 2** — Interior: a bare, humble room with a floor mat and a small round mirror with a worn silver frame; Marhoona, a sturdy fifty-year-old woman with henna-stained fingertips, waits with quiet authority.
> A mat, a tarnished mirror, and a lot of confidence.

**Panel 3** — Marhoona examines Safiya's and Fatkat's sunburned faces closely, nodding slowly like a judge reaching a verdict.
> "Sun. Too much."

**Panel 4** — Close-up: Marhoona's hands mixing sandalwood and saffron into a small clay bowl, quick and practiced.
> "Sandalwood. Saffron. Very good for skin."

**Panel 5** — Marhoona spreads the golden-orange paste onto Safiya's face with steady fingers, then Fatkat's; both women sit with eyes closed, expectant.
> They closed their eyes. They waited.

**Panel 6** — Time-passing beat: Safiya and Fatkat seated with the mask drying on their faces, the small room filled with the scent of sandalwood and saffron.
> The smell was pleasant. The skin felt tight, cool, treated.

**Panel 7** — Marhoona wipes the mask away and hands Safiya the small mirror; Safiya's eyes go wide in horror as she looks.
> Safiya opened her eyes. She looked. She screamed.

**Panel 8** — Fatkat grabs the mirror next, staring at her own reflection in shock; both faces are now bright yellow, like sad lemons.
> "Ya rab! I look like a warning sign!"

**Panel 9** — Safiya touches her own yellow cheek, bewildered, while Marhoona shrugs calmly in the background.
> "Why am I the colour of a banana?" / "It will go," Marhoona said calmly.

---

### Scene 3 — The Return

**Panel 1** — Safiya and Fatkat walk slowly back toward the school along the village path, hunched slightly, trying to be inconspicuous — both faces bright yellow.
> They walked back to school. Carefully. Slowly. Hoping nobody would see them.

**Panel 2** — A village woman pauses mid-sweep at her doorway, leaning her broom against the wall, staring with open curiosity as the two pass.
> She watched them go past, already composing the story for her sister.

**Panel 3** — A boy carrying a loaf of bread stops dead in the street, mouth open, the bread dangling forgotten from his hand.
> He forgot about the bread entirely.

**Panel 4** — An old man seated on a low farm wall watches them approach, pass, and recede, then calls out to his neighbor without turning his head.
> He called to his neighbour — something with the word "banana" in it.

**Panel 5** — At the school doorway, John stands relaxed with a cup of lukewarm water, enjoying a rare quiet moment — until he spots the yellow shapes rounding the corner.
> The calm lasted until the yellow appeared around the corner.

**Panel 6** — John sets his cup down carefully on the windowsill, then drops into a wooden teacher's chair, doubled over laughing.
> He fell into a chair laughing, with his whole body.

**Panel 7** — John, wiping his eyes, stands and gestures dramatically at the two yellow-faced women.
> "You look like a school bus and a lemon had a baby."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya arrives, presses her lips together and looks at the ceiling trying to stay composed, then finally breaks into laughter herself.
> "This is called… skin care?"

**Panel 9** — John stands theatrically, delivering his punchline with mock seriousness while Safiya and Fatkat look on, unimpressed.
> "Some of you do a lot of skin care… but the skin doesn't care."

**Panel 10** — In the background, Malood the goat wanders into the yard and makes a small sound, watching the yellow-faced women with the other onlookers.
> Even Malood made a small sound that could be called a laugh.

---

### Scene 4 — Mohammadeen Arrives

**Panel 1** — Mohammadeen strides through the school gate with his usual operatic confidence, then freezes mid-step, one foot still lifted, spotting his wife.
> He did not enter rooms. He arrived in them.

**Panel 2** — Close-up: Mohammadeen's drooping moustache and wide eyes traveling slowly from Fatkat's yellow forehead down to her yellow chin.
> His eyes travelled from the top of his wife's yellow forehead to her chin.

**Panel 3** — Mohammadeen throws up his hands, exclaiming in Arabic, moustache askew with exaggerated alarm.
> "God! What is this on your face, ya Fatkat? Why are you the colour of a lemon?"

**Panel 4** — Fatkat plants her hands on her hips, glaring at her husband, unimpressed.
> "Don't talk to me," she said, in English.

**Panel 5** — The schoolchildren stream through the gate in their usual cluster — Ahmed composed, Khalil laughing with Ali, the girls in a tight triangle, Saeed trailing behind.
> The students arrived in their usual small wave.

**Panel 6** — Mansoor spots the two yellow teachers, points dramatically, and bursts out laughing at full volume.
> "Teacher Fatkat! Teacher Safiya! You are… YELLOW!"

**Panel 7** — The whole class of children erupts in laughter; even composed Ahmed lets out a small dignified giggle.
> The class laughed. Every single child.

**Panel 8** — Fatkat's face shifts from strained smile to sharp anger as she points toward the gate, ordering Mansoor out; he stands frozen mid-protest.
> "OUT! YOU! OUTSIDE!"

---

### Scene 5 — In the Classroom

**Panel 1** — John stands at the blackboard, chalk in hand, writing the day's topic in neat capital letters while the class watches.
> Today's Topic: SKIN, SOAP, AND THE SUN.

**Panel 2** — John turns to face the class, gesturing toward his still-yellow-faced wife seated among the students' desks.
> "Today we talk about… what not to do."

**Panel 3** — Safiya, seated, touches her yellow cheek again, adding her own dry remark; Thuraya smiles beside her.
> "And what not to put." / "Let's learn."

**Panel 4** — John writes a vocabulary list on the board: skin, face, soap, water, clean, dirty, sunburn, wash, care.
> A simple vocabulary list for tomorrow's lesson.

**Panel 5** — John points to Safiya, prompting her; she composes a solemn teacher-expression and answers.
> "Say: My skin is…" / "My skin is… yellow."

**Panel 6** — A small boy stands at his desk, concentrating hard, delivering his sentence carefully while classmates watch.
> "I wash my face."

**Panel 7** — Safiya performs small mimed gestures for the class — rubbing her palms, circling her cheeks, lathering imaginary soap — while speaking each sentence.
> "I wash my hands. I wash my face. I use soap."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya leads the class in a repeated chorus line, the children's voices layered together.
> "The sun is hot," the class repeated together.

**Panel 9** — The children bend over their notebooks copying John's sentences from the board; Ahmed writes neatly, Noura adds a small flower doodle, Mansoor scribbles loudly through the open window.
> The sun is hot. My skin is red. I wash my face. I use soap.

**Panel 10** — A soft knock at the door; every head in the room turns as the door creaks open a crack to reveal Mansoor's small, sheepish face.
> "I am sorry." … "But… you still look funny."

---

### Scene 6 — The Skin That Didn't Care

**Panel 1** — Late-afternoon classroom bathed in warm gold light; Safiya cautiously touches her face, the yellow visibly fading.
> "I think it is leaving," she said quietly.

**Panel 2** — Fatkat beside her, sighing wearily, having spent lunch break scrubbing her face three separate times.
> "Next time, we use water. Only water."

**Panel 3** — John smiles a small, satisfied smile nearby, resisting the urge to say "I told you so."
> "And maybe a hat," he said.

**Panel 4** — Evening: John seated at the small wooden table in their simple house, writing by lamplight in his notebook, his face calm and pale-pink after the long day.
> He wrote: "Today we learned: The sun is strong. Soap is useful."

**Panel 5** — Close insert of the notebook page, John's pen pausing on the final line, a private smile forming.
> "And not all 'skin care' cares about skin."

**Panel 6** — Across the table, Safiya gently pats plain water onto her cheeks — no soap, no sandalwood, nothing else.
> Just plain water. No soap. No sandalwood.

**Panel 7** — Malood the goat wanders into the house, sniffing at Safiya's wet hand, leaning in to lick it; Safiya yanks her hand back.
> "No. You already did enough."

**Panel 8** — Malood turns away with wounded dignity, padding to the corner of the bisat (rug), lying down and closing his eyes.
> He walked off with small injured dignity, and slept.

**Panel 9** — Final wide shot: the small clay house at dusk, sun sinking toward the mountains, faint smoke and the smell of cooking drifting along the path; Safiya's fading yellow face turned toward the window, wedding ring back on her finger.
> Not yellow. Not English. Not Omani. Something in between.


---


## Palm Fourteen — Sadou Climbs the Palm

### Scene 1 — The Man in the Sky

**Panel 1** — Dusty village road in Samail, early morning, sun still low over sleepy brown-blue mountains. John and Safiya walk beside Thuraya; the baby goat Malood trails behind, distracted by a leaf.
> "The morning in Samail was already warm — and not going to be merciful."

**Panel 2** — Close on Safiya fixing her headscarf as they walk, mid-conversation with John beside her.
> "I still can't believe how quiet this place is in the morning."

**Panel 3** — John smiling at Safiya; Thuraya laughing warmly behind them.
> "Quiet is good. I hope the class is quiet today too."

**Panel 4** — The group passes narrow palm-tree farm plots lining the road; Safiya stops abruptly, staring upward, hand rising to point.
> "Wait… what is that?"

**Panel 5** — Wide shot looking up a tall palm tree (about fifteen metres), a small man's silhouette climbing rapidly using hands and feet like a ladder.
> "He was flying — up, up, up."

**Panel 6** — Zoom on the climbing man's face and quick hand/foot placements, motion lines showing speed; the three onlookers small at the base of the tree.
> "It was Sadoo, the shop owner."

**Panel 7** — John, mouth open, staring up in disbelief, Safiya and Thuraya beside him.
> "Is… is that Sadoo?"

**Panel 8** — Thuraya calm and nodding, unbothered, while John and Safiya still gape upward.
> "Yes."

**Panel 9** — Sadoo at the top of the tree cutting a cluster of dates with a small blade, bag over his shoulder.
> "He is harvesting dates. They climb. And cut."

**Panel 10** — Sadoo leaping down from the tree with the bag of dates, landing easily and brushing off his trousers, looking toward the three watchers with a knowing grin.
> Sadoo lands, brushes his trousers, spots his audience.

### Scene 2 — The Multi-Talented Superhero

**Panel 1** — John shaking his head slowly, addressing Safiya and Thuraya with mock formality, hand gesturing toward Sadoo in the background.
> "Sadoo is… a multi-talented superhero."

**Panel 2** — Thuraya laughing, a mischievous glint before she reveals more.
> "You haven't seen anything yet."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya pausing dramatically, enjoying the moment.
> "He also has a sewing shop."

**Panel 4** — John frozen mid-step, blank stunned expression, brain visibly stalled.
> "A… what?"

**Panel 5** — Thuraya explaining simply, hands open.
> "A sewing shop. He fixes clothes."

**Panel 6** — Sadoo walking toward the group, bag of dates over his shoulder, shirt dusty, broad shopkeeper smile, striking his chest with his palm.
> "أنا سادو! أنا سادو يعرف كل شي منيه!" (I am Sadoo! I know every single thing!)

**Panel 7** — John answering back instantly in Arabic, his own face registering surprise as the words leave his mouth.
> "أبوي، ما نرومه صوبنا!" (Father, we can't reach where you are!)

**Panel 8** — Safiya and Thuraya both stopped in their tracks, staring hard at John with scrutinizing expressions.
> "What… did you just say?"

**Panel 9** — John blinking, confused, looking at the ground then the sky then Sadoo, pretending nothing happened as he walks faster ahead of the group.
> "Did I say something?"

**Panel 10** — Sadoo laughing heartily behind them, waving; the group waves back and continues toward the school.
> Sadoo laughs, waves; they walk on to school.

### Scene 3 — I Can, He Can, She Can
