**Panel 1** — Noisy Omani village classroom: Mansoor folding a paper airplane, Khalil reaching to steal it, Noura talking non-stop, Ahmed sitting ramrod-straight with his ruler perfectly aligned, Mathila sharing a pencil, quiet Saeed watching from the back.
> The classroom was noisy before the lesson even started.

**Panel 2** — John entering the room, clearing his throat, class straightening up.
> "Good morning!" / "Good morning, teacher!"

**Panel 3** — John at the chalkboard writing the lesson title in clear capitals.
> Today's Lesson: TALENTS — I can… / He can… / She can…

**Panel 4** — Safiya clapping her hands enthusiastically to the class; Thuraya smiling beside her.
> "Okay! Today we talk about what you can do."

**Panel 5** — Mansoor's hand shooting up eagerly before being called on.
> "I can run!"

**Panel 6** — Khalil standing, competitive, arms crossed proudly.
> "I can climb!"

**Panel 7** — Ahmed sitting quietly upright, speaking with understated dignity; the room noticeably stiller around him.
> "I can read."

**Panel 8** — Mathila proud, then Noura satisfied and grinning, the class laughing warmly around them.
> "I can help." / "I can talk."

**Panel 9** — John writing the completed sentence list on the board in neat rows.
> I can run. I can climb. I can read. I can help.

### Scene 4 — Sadoo Can Climb

**Panel 1** — John at the chalkboard, sketching a quick charming palm tree with curved fronds and a small climbing figure halfway up the trunk.
> John draws a palm tree — and Sadoo, climbing it.

**Panel 2** — John writing the caption sentence beneath his drawing.
> Sadoo can climb a palm tree.

**Panel 3** — The whole class laughing happily, recognizing the shopkeeper in the drawing.
> The class laughed — their own shopkeeper, on the board.

**Panel 4** — Mansoor shouting out, arms wide in imitation of a monkey.
> "Yes! Sadoo climb like monkey!"

**Panel 5** — John gently correcting, pointing back at the board with a patient smile.
> "Sadoo can climb." / "Sadoo can climb!" the class repeats.

**Panel 6** — Safiya pointing at John, playfully putting him on the spot in front of the class.
> "Teacher John — what can you do?"

**Panel 7** — John with a modest smile, hand to chest.
> "I can… teach." (The class claps.)

**Panel 8** — Thuraya and Safiya each adding their own lines from their corners of the room; Mansoor grinning and shouting his own.
> "I can explain." / "I can organise." / "And I can… make noise!"

**Panel 9** — Mohammadeen pausing in the doorway with his math books, stern Egyptian authority in his posture, surveying the room.
> "No. You cannot."

**Panel 10** — Instant total silence in the classroom — even Mansoor's mouth shut; Safiya leaning to whisper to Thuraya, John suppressing a laugh.
> "Wow. He doesn't need English." / "He is universal."

### Scene 5 — The Quiet Challenge

**Panel 1** — John turning to face Mansoor directly across the quieted classroom.
> "Mansoor. Can you be quiet?"

**Panel 2** — Mansoor standing proudly, chest puffed out with eight-year-old determination.
> "Yes. I can."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya raising a skeptical eyebrow; Safiya checking her small silver wristwatch.
> "Let's see." / "Let's try. Two minutes."

**Panel 4** — Mansoor seated, hands flat on the desk, eyes fixed on one spot on the blackboard, forcing stillness.
> Mansoor sits. He is quiet — for ten seconds.

**Panel 5** — Close on Mansoor's foot tapping lightly against the desk leg, then one finger drumming on the wood.
> His foot began to move. Then his hand.

**Panel 6** — Mansoor mid-whisper to Khalil, then snapping his mouth shut like a gate as John looks over.
> Mansoor whispers to Khalil — then stops abruptly.

**Panel 7** — Mansoor making quiet under-the-breath airplane noises, lips barely moving; Safiya's calm firm gaze on him.
> "Brrrrrrm. Brrrrrrm." / "Mansoor…"

**Panel 8** — Mansoor visibly shaking with the effort of holding still; Thuraya's kind encouraging expression nearby.
> "You can do it." (One minute, thirty seconds.)

**Panel 9** — Mansoor bursting out of his seat, breaking into honest exasperation; the whole class laughing, John laughing warmly too.
> "Teacher, I CAN'T!"

**Panel 10** — John at the board writing one final line beneath the list of talents; the class repeating it in full chorus, Mansoor loudest of all.
> I can try. — the class repeats, Mansoor loudest.

### Scene 6 — The Sewing Shop

**Panel 1** — School bell moment; Safiya and John exiting the classroom together, satisfied expressions.
> "Today was good." / "Very good."

**Panel 2** — The road near the market edge; Sadoo now seated on a low wooden stool outside his shop's front door, bent over a piece of cloth, needle and thread in hand, two folded shirts waiting beside him.
> This time, he was sewing.

**Panel 3** — Safiya's mouth open in surprised admiration, watching Sadoo work the needle with practiced care.
> "He really does everything."

**Panel 4** — John with a settled, amused smile, gesturing toward Sadoo as he lists his talents.
> "Sadoo can climb. Sadoo can sew. Sadoo can sell. Sadoo can…"

**Panel 5** — John trailing off mid-sentence, realizing the list has no end, half-smiling at the impossibility of finishing it.
> The list had no end.

**Panel 6** — Sadoo looking up, spotting them, smiling proudly and self-satisfied at being the classroom's star lesson.
> Sadoo looked up, smiled proudly.

**Panel 7** — John feeling an Arabic phrase rising in his throat, catching himself, consciously closing his lips and stopping it before it emerges.
> He stopped himself — deliberately, this time.

**Panel 8** — John nodding to Sadoo, speaking only in English; Sadoo beaming and holding up the shirt he's mending, giving a small polite nod back.
> "Good work, Sadoo."

**Panel 9** — Safiya looking at John with narrowed, suspicious eyes.
> "You're thinking in Arabic again. Aren't you?"

**Panel 10** — John pausing, honestly uncertain, scratching his head; Thuraya laughing warmly beside them.
> "No." … "Maybe." … "I don't know."

**Panel 11** — Wide sunset-toned shot: John looking out over the palm trees, the low mountains, the small white school, Sadoo still sewing, and finally at Safiya and Thuraya beside him on the dusty road home.
> "Slowly, slowly, Oman is teaching you."

**Panel 12** — John quietly composed, gazing down the road; in the distance, Malood knocks something over in a yard and Aunt Azizah's voice calls out — John doesn't even flinch.
> "Maybe I can learn more than I think."


---


## Palm Fifteen — When Marhoon Lifted His Hand

### Scene 1 — The Missing Things

**Panel 1** — Village schoolyard at a low clay wall in the cool early morning; soft dust on the ground; children clustering before the bell.
> "Some days in Samail started quietly... and some days waited for trouble."

**Panel 2** — Ali hopping angrily on one foot in the schoolyard, face scrunched in narrow-browed panic.
> "My zanoubah is GONE! My mother will KILL me!"

**Panel 3** — Rahma standing barefoot, sobbing hard, holding up her empty hands where her new buckled sandals should be.
> "My new sandals! I just got them! With a BUCKLE!"

**Panel 4** — Mansoor in the center of the group, eyes wide and theatrical, voice lowered, delivering grave news to the other children.
> "It is the work of Shayb Khalaf."

**Panel 5** — Khalil leaning in, eyes wide with anxious awe; Mansoor nodding seriously beside him.
> "The wizard?" — "He makes hura. Things disappear."

**Panel 6** — Noura gripping Salima's arm tightly, both girls staring off toward the edge of the village, frightened.
> "Maybe he took them to his clay house…"

**Panel 7** — Ahmed standing apart, adjusting his books, patient and unimpressed while the others panic.
> "That doesn't make sense."

**Panel 8** — Saeed off to the side of the group, quiet, arms loose, watching everyone with a knowing expression.
> Only one boy knew the truth.

### Scene 2 — What Saeed Saw

**Panel 1** — Flashback: the falaj stone bank earlier that morning; Ali splashing and playing with younger boys, his zanoubah lying near the water's edge.
> Saeed had seen it all.

**Panel 2** — Rahma crossing the yard in her new buckled sandals, setting them down near a tree to go play with Salima.
> She left them near the tree while she played.

**Panel 3** — Malood the baby goat trotting toward the abandoned zanoubah and sandals with a casual, unbothered air.
> Malood — moving toward both items, quietly.

**Panel 4** — Close on Malood picking up the zanoubah in his mouth and slipping behind the low wall.
> Taking them. Quietly. One at a time.

**Panel 5** — Saeed crouched at the edge of the group in his usual quiet spot, watching but silent.
> He could have spoken. He did not.

**Panel 6** — Saeed's point of view: Mansoor gesturing, Khalil gasping, Noura clutching Salima — the sahir-theory spreading.
> Thing lost → old man blamed. It happened a lot.

**Panel 7** — Saeed's face suddenly changing — eyes widening, a jolt of memory hitting him.
> He should be at the mal. Watering.

**Panel 8** — Saeed walking briskly away from the schoolyard down the dusty road toward the farms, trying to look unhurried.
> He had, perhaps, ten minutes. He was not going to make it.

### Scene 3 — At the Mal

**Panel 1** — Marhoon, tall and sun-weathered in a plain dishdasha, cloth tobacco pouch over his shoulder, walking onto the small family date-palm plot under a high sun.
> The sun was high when Marhoon arrived at the mal.

**Panel 2** — Marhoon standing in the middle of the farm, surveying the rows of young date palms and dry earth channels.
> The channels were empty. The plants were thirsty.

**Panel 3** — Close-up on the pale, cracked earth-banks around the palm bases, bone-dry.
> Ground that had not felt water since yesterday, at the earliest.

**Panel 4** — Marhoon's face darkening with anger, cupping his hands to shout across the empty field.
> "SAEED!" — No answer.

**Panel 5** — Marhoon walking past the wooden water-frame and iron bucket, everything untouched and still.
> Past the rope. Past the bucket. Nothing.

**Panel 6** — Close on the rope, neatly coiled exactly as Saeed had left it the night before.
> Nobody had touched it since.

**Panel 7** — Marhoon's jaw tightening, quiet and grim, speaking to no one.
> "He went to watch those school children again."

**Panel 8** — Marhoon bending to pick up a thin cane stick (kheyzaran) from the ground, then striding fast toward the school road.
> He picked up a thin stick. He walked. Fast.

### Scene 4 — The Road

**Panel 1** — Saeed sitting on a low stone wall beside a ghaf tree near the school, peering through gaps in the wall at the playing children, unseen by them.
> He always watched. He did not come close.

**Panel 2** — Saeed alone in his hiding spot, pensive expression, knees drawn up.
> He had never been inside a classroom. His father had forbidden him.

**Panel 3** — Saeed turning sharply, alert, at the sound of heavy footsteps approaching on the road.
> Heavy footsteps.

**Panel 4** — Marhoon looming over Saeed on the dusty road; Saeed slowly rising to face him.
> His father. Marhoon.

**Panel 5** — Marhoon shouting down at Saeed, stick in hand, face furious.
> "Why are you HERE? Why is the mal DRY?"

**Panel 6** — Saeed looking down at the ground, small and defeated, mumbling.
> "I just… wanted to see…"

**Panel 7** — The stick striking down; Saeed crying out and collapsing to his knees in the dust.
> The stick came down. Once. Twice.

**Panel 8** — Marhoon standing over the fallen boy, his whole thin frame shaking with anger, mid-shout in Arabic.
> "You want to learn 'yes' and 'no' like the unbelievers? Tie you to this palm tree?!"

**Panel 9** — A loose cluster of schoolchildren frozen at the edge of the path in the distance, watching in silence.
> Nobody moved.

**Panel 10** — Thuraya sprinting forward in her abaya, Safiya and John running behind her, closing the distance fast.
> "STOP!" It was Thuraya.

**Panel 11** — Thuraya planting herself between Marhoon and Saeed like a human wall; Safiya dropping to her knees in the dust, pulling Saeed into a protective embrace.
> "That is enough!"

**Panel 12** — John standing beside them, confused and angry, turning to Thuraya to ask a question; Thuraya's face tight and level as she answers.
> "What does koffar mean?" — "It means… unbelievers."

### Scene 5 — The Arabic That Came

**Panel 1** — Thuraya drawing a deep breath, stepping forward to mediate between Marhoon and the others.
> "Let me talk to him."

**Panel 2** — John raising his hand suddenly, all eyes turning to him in surprise.
> "Sabriiii!"

**Panel 3** — John speaking rapidly and fluently in Arabic, gesturing, his own face startled at the words coming out of him.
> "We are not unbelievers! We are Muslims! We pray and we fast just like you!"

**Panel 4** — Total stillness on the road; Marhoon's mouth hanging open in shock; children's eyes huge.
> Pure silence.

**Panel 5** — Safiya still kneeling in the dust holding Saeed, whispering to herself in bewilderment.
> "When did we pray? Or fast?"

**Panel 6** — Mohammadeen and Fatkat arriving at a run down the road, taking in the frozen scene.
> Mohammadeen had heard — and arrived at full speed.

**Panel 7** — Mohammadeen pointing dramatically at John, shouting in Egyptian Arabic.
> "It's not you, my sister! It's the jinni in him! The fellow has been possessed!"

**Panel 8** — Fatkat mid-scream, then collapsing backward in a dead faint.
> She fell like a sack of rice.

**Panel 9** — Chaos: Safiya reaching toward Fatkat with one hand while still holding Saeed with the other, everyone startled.
> "Oh no!"

**Panel 10** — Thuraya pressing a hand to her mouth, shoulders shaking, failing to suppress a laugh.
> A small laugh — muffled — escaped her.

**Panel 11** — John standing bewildered in the middle of it all, looking around at his shaking wife, the fainted teacher, the frozen father.
> "Why is everyone looking at me?"

**Panel 12** — Marhoon sinking slowly down to sit in the dust, stick forgotten, staring blankly ahead.
> He did not speak. He did not shout. He just stared.

### Scene 6 — The Goat, and What Changed

**Panel 1** — Saeed still kneeling in the dust inside Safiya's arm, voice small and shaking as he finally speaks.
> "I didn't steal anything… Malood took them…"

**Panel 2** — Everyone's heads turning at once toward the ghaf tree.

> They all turned.

**Panel 3** — Malood the goat standing unbothered in the tree's shade, Ali's zanoubah still in his mouth, Rahma's chewed sandal on the ground beside him.
> Holding, without shame, Ali's zanoubah in his mouth.

**Panel 4** — The children pointing and shouting, breaking into commotion.
> "THE GOAT!"

**Panel 5** — Thuraya closing her eyes with weary, knowing exasperation.
> "Of course."

**Panel 6** — The walk back to school: Saeed between Safiya and Thuraya, his arm looped through Safiya's, face streaked but calm; Marhoon left sitting alone on the ground in the background.
> Marhoon did not follow them. He simply sat.

**Panel 7** — Back in the schoolyard, Fatkat sitting revived with a glass of sweet tea, gesturing animatedly to a circle of women; Mohammadeen nearby still muttering about possession.
> The classroom had divided into the pro-possession and pro-confused-miracle camps.

**Panel 8** — John sitting alone in the corner of the classroom, head in his hands.
> John sat in the corner. Holding his head.

**Panel 9** — Safiya looking at him with quiet warmth from across the room.
> "You are dangerous in Arabic." — "I don't remember anything."

**Panel 10** — Thuraya's gaze crossing the room to the doorway, where Saeed sits quietly on the wooden step, arms around his knees, watching.
> Not a student. Not a guest. But not invisible anymore.

**Panel 11** — Thuraya speaking softly, almost to herself, still watching Saeed; Safiya turning to her, puzzled.
> "Tomorrow." — "Tomorrow, what?"

**Panel 12** — Thuraya, resolute, eyes still on the boy in the doorway.
> "Tomorrow we will go to the Sheikh's house. Not just for Saeed. For all of them."


---


## Palm Sixteen — The Sheikh's House and the Mandoos

### Scene 1 — The Invitation

**Panel 1** — Wide village shot at dusk, Samail's clay houses and palm trees catching late-afternoon light; the mood is hushed, a village digesting news.
> "That day, something big had happened."

**Panel 2** — A small boy runs along a dusty lane between houses, calling out at each doorway; villagers glance up from their yards.
> "After Maghrib prayer. Everyone to the Sheikh's house."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya sits on a low wooden bench outside a small clay house, folding cloth; she looks up toward the gate as the boy's voice reaches her.
> The boy's message arrives; her hands stop folding.

**Panel 4** — Close on Thuraya's hands, motionless over the folded cloth — a small, telling detail.
> Her hands had stopped.

**Panel 5** — Thuraya stands, sets the cloth aside, speaks calmly to Safiya and John.
> "They will talk about Saeed. And about school."

**Panel 6** — John sits with his notebook closed on his lap, pen unused all afternoon, looking uncertain.
> "Is that good or bad?"

**Panel 7** — Thuraya gives John a level, honest look, neither smiling nor frowning.
> "In Samail, it is serious."

**Panel 8** — Safiya, seated nearby, looks up with a practical question; Thuraya answers with a small careful smile.
> "Just your hearts. And maybe… empty stomachs."

### Scene 2 — The House of the Sheikh

**Panel 1** — Establishing shot of the Sheikh's house in the last gold light after Maghrib — large, plain, built for many people to sit together.
> A house built for large gatherings.

**Panel 2** — Interior of the long front room: a wide low sitting-room lined with carpets, incense smoke rising in soft columns from censers at both ends.
> Incense smoke rising, soft and slow.

**Panel 3** — The room packed with men seated on carpets — silver-bearded elders near the front, younger fathers and uncles filling the rest, a few familiar faces from the school gate.
> Old men, young men, fathers, uncles — the room is full.

**Panel 4** — Cutaway to a back room: fresh coffee being prepared, cardamom steam rising from a pot.
> The air smells of coffee and incense.

**Panel 5** — Through a low doorway at the side of the compound, women work busily around big pots, cauldrons, and stacks of plates.
> Very busy — big pots, big plans for dinner.

**Panel 6** — Safiya leans toward Thuraya, whispering, gesturing at the cooking activity.
> "Why is there so much food?"

**Panel 7** — Thuraya answers plainly, without hesitation, standing amid the bustle.
> "Because no Omani meeting ends without food."

**Panel 8** — Safiya nods, taking this in as a serious cultural rule, not just a custom.
> "Important rule."

### Scene 3 — The Food Fear

**Panel 1** — Safiya's gaze drifts across the kitchen area — pots, plates, rising steam, the smell of cooking meat.
> Her eye catches something worrying.

**Panel 2** — Safiya turns to Thuraya, sudden alarm on her face.
> "Thuraya… Where is Malood?"

**Panel 3** — Thuraya blinks, momentarily caught off guard, trying to recall.
> "He was outside."

**Panel 4** — Safiya's eyes go wide with dawning horror, glancing toward the big knives and pots.
> "Outside… or in the kitchen?"

**Panel 5** — Safiya clutches her chest with one hand, imagining the worst, distress on her face.
> "Oh no. They killed Malood."

**Panel 6** — Fatkat rounds the corner laughing, cupping her hands around her mouth as if shouting across a courtyard.
> "مالود إيه اللي يتدبح؟ ده بره مع الحريم!" ("Who would slaughter Malood? He's outside with the women!")

**Panel 7** — Safiya freezes, processing the relief, eyes searching Fatkat's face.
> "He is… alive?"

**Panel 8** — Fatkat plants her hands on her hips, satisfied, nodding firmly.
> "More than alive."

**Panel 9** — Outside in the courtyard, the small white goat Malood — bell around his neck — causes a commotion; a voice shouts from off-panel.
> "STOP EATING THAT!"

**Panel 10** — Safiya sits down hard on a small wooden stool, hand back on her heart, this time in relief, eyes bright with near-tears.
> Relief, not grief — Malood lives.

### Scene 4 — The Meeting Begins

**Panel 1** — Inside the majlis: the Sheikh, an older man of about seventy with a neat white beard and calm eyes, sits on a slightly raised cushion at the far end of the room.
> The Sheikh presides, calm and settled.

**Panel 2** — Marhoon, Saeed's father, sits near the wall, quiet and heavy, eyes low.
> Marhoon — quiet, not angry, just heavy.

**Panel 3** — Saeed sits half-hidden behind a pillar, small and watchful, choosing a seat that allows both presence and concealment.
> Saeed watches from behind a pillar.

**Panel 4** — John, Safiya, and Thuraya sit together near the door, slightly apart from the main circle of men.
> Guests, seated near the door.

**Panel 5** — The Sheikh clears his throat; the room falls immediately silent, all faces turning toward him.
> "We are here to talk about our children. And about learning."

**Panel 6** — Murmurs ripple along the carpet — different men voicing doubts, small nods and head-shakes.
> "School is new." / "Work is more important."

**Panel 7** — Marhoon stares at the floor, saying nothing, the weight of the day visible on his face.
> Marhoon looks down — and does not look up.

**Panel 8** — Thuraya rises to her feet among the seated men, calm and dignified, addressing the room in clear Arabic.
> "Knowledge does not take your sons away from you. It gives them a way."

**Panel 9** — John tenses beside Safiya, about to speak; Safiya's hand closes firmly around his wrist under his sleeve.
> "No Arabic today."

**Panel 10** — The majlis in full motion — men taking turns speaking, arguing, listening, the Sheikh inclining his head.
> The rotation of a majlis in action.

**Panel 11** — Marhoon speaks quietly from near the wall, head still partly down, every man in the room listening.
> "I was afraid. I thought school will change him."

**Panel 12** — The Sheikh nods slowly, meeting Marhoon's eyes; across the room, Saeed looks up from behind the pillar, his gaze moving from the Sheikh to his father to Thuraya.
> "It will. But not in a bad way."

### Scene 5 — And Then — Lunch

**Panel 1** — The mood in the majlis shifts instantly from serious to relaxed, as if by unspoken signal.
> The talking phase ends; the eating phase begins.

**Panel 2** — Two men carry in an enormous communal plate, the size of a small table, piled with golden saffron rice, almonds, and raisins.
> Enormous plates of rice arrive.

**Panel 3** — More plates follow, heaped with meat and more rice, set along the carpet.
> Meat. More rice. More meat.

**Panel 4** — Men tuck their dishdasha hems under their knees, wash their right hands from passed basins, and begin eating together.
> The men eat, coordinated and familiar.

**Panel 5** — John stares at the sheer scale of the food before him, eyes wide with astonishment.
> John, stunned by the scale of the feast.

**Panel 6** — John eating with great enthusiasm, plate piled high, Safiya watching beside him with amusement.
> He ate. And ate. And ate.

**Panel 7** — John, mouth full, grinning cheerfully at Safiya as she gently touches his wrist.
> "This is history. I must respect history."

**Panel 8** — After the meal: young men collecting plates, old men leaning back on cushions; John already leaning back, both hands on his stomach.
> John, overwhelmed, holding his stomach.

**Panel 9** — John announces loudly in English to the room, patting his belly, oblivious to the awkward pause that follows.
> "Now I understand why Omani men have big belly."

**Panel 10** — A beat of stunned silence, then Thuraya bursts out laughing from across the room, calling back to him; the men roar with laughter.
> "It's good for wearing khanjar!"

**Panel 11** — Safiya, puzzled, turns to Thuraya, who gives her a knowing smile.
> "What is khanjar?" — "You will see. Very soon."

### Scene 6 — Outside, and That Night

**Panel 1** — In the back courtyard, Malood the goat is very much alive — stealing food, making children shriek with delighted fear.
> Malood, very much alive, causing chaos.

**Panel 2** — Saeed sits close beside Malood, one small hand resting on the goat's flank, a quiet smile on his face.
> Saeed smiles — quietly, for the first time.

**Panel 3** — Close on Saeed's face — the small, private smile of a boy who has just been part of a village decision.
> He had been inside a village decision.

**Panel 4** — Wide shot of the courtyard settling into evening calm, the matter unspoken but resolved among the villagers.
> Tomorrow, Saeed will come to school.

**Panel 5** — Inside John and Safiya's simple house that night: John lies on the bisat, one hand on his stomach, notebook beside him.
> John, still feeling the weight of the rice.

**Panel 6** — John writes slowly in his notebook by lamplight.
> "Today, a village talked. And something changed."

**Panel 7** — Safiya, sitting across from him on the bisat, writes her own line beneath his in a smaller, rounder hand.
> "Also, Malood is not food."

**Panel 8** — John reads her line and laughs quietly, content, worn out by the day.
> A quiet, full-stomach laugh.

**Panel 9** — Outside, Thuraya stands alone in her parents' small courtyard, looking up at the dense night sky over Samail.
> "Tomorrow," she said, "we start something new."


---


## Palm Seventeen — Spicy Food and Saeed's First English Sentences

### Scene 1 — The Visit

**Panel 1** — Morning in Samail; Thuraya stands in a doorway or sunny courtyard smiling a knowing, mysterious smile at John and Safiya.
> "My mother wants to show you something."

**Panel 2** — Dirt path behind the village school, past two palm groves and an old disused well, leading to a low clay house with a blue door and swept front yard.
> They walked to Thuraya's parents' house.

**Panel 3** — Interior of the clay house: warm pale ochre walls, a small clay censer burning oud by the door. Thuraya's mother, a small sturdy woman with hennaed fingertips, welcomes them warmly.
> "Come, come. Sit."

**Panel 4** — The group seated on low cushions along the wall; the mother serves coffee in small finjans and a dish of dates, speaking through Thuraya's translation.
> She asked about John's leg, now fully healed.

**Panel 5** — The mother rising and disappearing through a doorway into an inner room.
> She disappeared into another room.

**Panel 6** — The mother returning slowly, carrying a large, old, heavy wooden chest studded with brass nails in geometric patterns, a brass lock, and an ornate key on a silver chain at her wrist.
> A big wooden box — the mandoos.

**Panel 7** — Close on Safiya's face, quiet and wide-eyed, looking at the chest as it's set down.
> "What is that?"

**Panel 8** — John leaning in, touching a brass stud with two fingers; the mother smiling proudly beside him.
> "It looks like a pirate treasure box." — "Better."

### Scene 2 — The Treasure Inside

**Panel 1** — The mandoos lid creaking open; warm light catching silver and gold treasures inside as everyone leans in.
> Inside: light, silver, gold, stories.

**Panel 2** — The mother lifting out a curved khanjar dagger in its silver-wrapped sheath; John's eyes wide and round.
> "Oh! The curved knife!"

**Panel 3** — Thuraya, serious and instructive, holding up the khanjar to show its crescent sheath, silver wire, and hammered rosettes.
> "It is not just a knife. It is a symbol."

**Panel 4** — Safiya carefully cradling the khanjar in both hands, treating it like glass.
> "It's beautiful."

**Panel 5** — The mother laying out silver jewelry on the cloth: wide pectoral necklaces, heavy bracelets, cone-shaped Nizwa earrings.
> Sixty years of collected silver.

**Panel 6** — The mother lifting a cascading, shimmering piece of jewelry that trembles and chimes softly in her hand.
> "That is martaasha."

**Panel 7** — Safiya sounding out the word carefully, then laughing as the piece shivers and sounds again.
> "Mar… ta… asha." — "It is shaking!"

**Panel 8** — The mother sliding thin silver banjari bracelets one at a time onto Safiya's outstretched wrist.
> One. Cling. Two. Cling, cling.

**Panel 9** — Safiya's wrist now stacked with about twelve silver bracelets; she moves her hand and they all shift together.
> It sounded like music.

**Panel 10** — John clapping, grinning at Safiya's jingling wrist.
> "You are now a walking instrument."

### Scene 3 — Silver or Gold?

**Panel 1** — Thuraya laying two necklaces — one silver, one gold — carefully on a small white cloth on the low table.
> "One is silver. One is gold."

**Panel 2** — John inspecting both necklaces closely, pointing at each in turn.
> "Silver is… gray. Gold is… yellow."

**Panel 3** — Safiya smirking across at him teasingly; John pointedly looking away with exaggerated dignity.
> "Like John after lunch, and John before lunch."

**Panel 4** — Thuraya, in teacher mode, holding the silver necklace up to catch light from a small high window, showing its handmade texture.
> "In Oman, silver is very important. Especially for women."

**Panel 5** — Safiya holding the jewelry, testing its weight in her hand, noting how substantial it feels.
> "They are heavy."

**Panel 6** — John draping a large necklace across his own palm, mock-staggering under its weight; Thuraya laughing beside him.
> "I think, if I wear this, I will fall." — "We don't put everything on one person."

**Panel 7** — The mother lifting out smaller pieces one by one onto the cloth as Thuraya explains how jewelry is distributed across a bride's body on her wedding day.
> A bride carries her family's saved history.

### Scene 4 — Trying Clothes

**Panel 1** — The mother emerging from the inner room with an armful of folded traditional clothes: an embroidered gold-threaded dress, a plain white dishdasha, a belt, and a khanjar.
> Traditional clothes, carried reverently.

**Panel 2** — John pointing hesitantly at the long shiny khanjar, half-hopeful.
> "Is that… for me?" — "Yes."

**Panel 3** — John now dressed in the flowing white dishdasha, standing tall, looking transformed.
> The dishdasha hung the way it was designed to.

**Panel 4** — Thuraya winding the belt twice around John's waist and fixing the khanjar at the correct outward-tilted angle.
> She attached the khanjar at the traditional angle.

**Panel 5** — John examining himself in a small handheld mirror the mother brought out, posture suddenly straight and serious.
> Straight. Serious. Important.

**Panel 6** — Safiya clapping in delight at John's transformed appearance; John standing even taller.
> "You look like a very hungry sultan." — "I feel powerful."

**Panel 7** — Safiya now in the embroidered dress, jewelry being added piece by piece — banjari, necklace, earrings, martaasha — as she walks carefully, jingling.
> Cling cling cling.

**Panel 8** — Safiya turning to check herself in the mirror, silver catching the light.
> "Do I look Omani?" — "Very."

### Scene 5 — The Lesson Idea

**Panel 1** — Everyone reseated on the cushions; the mother pouring a fresh, relaxed round of coffee.
> The ceremonial refill of genuine companionship.

**Panel 2** — John, still in his dishdasha, hunched over his notebook with a furrowed, focused expression.
> "We can make this a lesson."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya writing a vocabulary list on a small piece of paper in Arabic and English: Gold, Silver, Wearing, Necklace, Bracelet, Khanjar, Dress.
> "Vocabulary."

**Panel 4** — Safiya and John both contributing sentence ideas aloud while Thuraya nods approvingly.
> "I am wearing…" / "It is made of silver."

**Panel 5** — The mother lifting the now-lighter mandoos and carrying it back into the inner room as the oud censer still burns.
> The mandoos, slightly lighter, went home.

**Panel 6** — John and Safiya walking home together through golden afternoon light, Safiya glancing at her bare wrist, John walking unusually straight-backed.
> His shoulders stayed squared for three more hours.

### Scene 6 — At School: I Am Wearing

**Panel 1** — Classroom the next morning, children whispering; Safiya wears a single silver banjari, John wears only the ornate belt (no khanjar).
> The classroom was full of whispers.

**Panel 2** — Thuraya opening a small cloth bag on her desk, revealing jewelry; the children's faces frozen in a collective gasp.
> "Wow!" — "Pretty!" — "Is that real silver?"

**Panel 3** — Thuraya writing "I am wearing…" on the chalkboard and pointing to Safiya, who lifts her jingling wrist; the class repeating in chorus.
> "I am wearing… a bracelet."

**Panel 4** — John pointing to his silver-worked belt; Ahmed at his desk carefully writing the sentence twice with decorative underlines.
> "I am wearing… a belt."

**Panel 5** — Thuraya holding up a large silver necklace; Noura answering quickly, then a silence falling over the class when asked what it's made of.
> "Necklace." … "It is made of silver."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya holding up a small gold ring; Khalil shouting out confidently.
> "Gold!" — "It is made of gold."

**Panel 7** — Mansoor raising his hand eagerly, Thuraya laughing at his question.
> "Teacher… you are wearing… many things?" — "Not today."

**Panel 8** — At the back of the room near the door, Saeed sits quietly and apart, staring at the jewelry on the desk with wide, wary, wondering eyes.
> He looked at it as though it came from another world.

**Panel 9** — Safiya crouching beside Saeed's small desk, holding out her wrist to him gently; Saeed concentrating hard.
> "What am I wearing?" — "You… are wearing… bracelet."

**Panel 10** — John standing behind Safiya, leaning down; Saeed whispering shyly, sitting a little straighter with quiet pride.
> "And what is it made of?" — "Silver." — "Excellent."

**Panel 11** — Two desks away, Mansoor whispering to Khalil, grinning; Khalil smirking back.
> "One day, I will wear khanjar." — "One day, you will lose it."

**Panel 12** — That evening in the simple house, John writing in his notebook by lamplight; Safiya answering flatly without looking up; outside in the yard, Malood the goat yanks at cloth on the washing line as Thuraya shouts from the window.
> "I want my own khanjar." — "No." — "NO, MALOOD!"


---


## Palm Eighteen — Mountains, Sea, and the Magic Box of Muttrah

### Scene 1 — The Tired Fan

**Panel 1** — Wide shot of a hot, dusty Samaili classroom: mud-brick walls, packed-earth floor, chalkboard, a slow ceiling fan overhead. Heat haze suggested in the light.
> "A hot that began in the walls, saved inside the clay all night from yesterday's sun."

**Panel 2** — Close-up on the ceiling fan, blades barely turning, a faint motion-line suggesting its weak creak.
> "Tak… tak… tak…"

**Panel 3** — John standing near the window, blinking, his eyes still faintly red/irritated from prior mishaps.
> His eyes still red from yesterday's saffron and smoke.

**Panel 4** — Safiya seated on the teacher's chair, fanning her flushed face briskly with a notebook.
> "Next time, we teach English in the fridge."

**Panel 5** — Thuraya standing beside her, smiling warmly, at ease with the heat.
> "Welcome to Samail."

**Panel 6** — Group shot of the students at their desks: Ahmed sitting rigid and straight; Mansoor slumped sideways in his seat.
> Ahmed sat like a ruler; Mansoor slumped in constant motion.

**Panel 7** — Khalil leaning over to whisper to Ali, who is silently shaking with suppressed laughter.
> Khalil whispers something; Ali tries not to laugh.

**Panel 8** — Rahma and Salima sharing one pencil between them; Marwa doodling small flowers in her notebook; Noura's hand already shooting up.
> Rahma and Salima share a pencil; Noura's hand goes up early.

**Panel 9** — Saeed alone at the back of the room, in a new but still-unfamiliar seat, watching everything quietly without joining in.
> Saeed watched — no longer an outsider, still learning how to belong.

---

### Scene 2 — We Ate Lamb

**Panel 1** — Under a desk, the baby goat Malood curled in a shaded patch of floor, chewing on a scrap of paper.
> Malood had claimed the classroom floor as his own.

**Panel 2** — Safiya glancing down at Malood, leaning toward John with a wry, tired look.
> "We really need to talk about your student."

**Panel 3** — Mansoor suddenly bolt upright out of his seat, both arms thrown in the air, urgent.
> Mansoor launched to his feet: "Teacher! Teacher!"

**Panel 4** — Mansoor mid-declaration, chest puffed with pride.
> "We eat lamb yesterday! And with chili."

**Panel 5** — The whole class erupting at once — hands flying up, mouths open, mock-suffering faces.
> "Hot!" "Very hot!" "My mouth burn!" "I cry!"

**Panel 6** — Khalil grinning proudly; Ali beside him shaking his head, unimpressed.
> Khalil: "I eat more." Ali: "You crazy."

**Panel 7** — Safiya laughing, addressing the class; rows of small heads nodding in unison.
> "So… everyone ate spicy food?" — the class nods together.

**Panel 8** — John facing the board, a slow knowing smile spreading across his face.
> "Good. Very good. Today… we talk about taste."

---

### Scene 3 — The Five Senses

**Panel 1** — John at the chalkboard writing large capital letters: THE FIVE SENSES, then TASTE beneath it.
> John wrote: THE FIVE SENSES / TASTE.

**Panel 2** — Class repeating aloud, mouths stretched wide on the vowel, some exaggerating the word.
> "Taaaste," the class echoed, stretching the new word.

**Panel 3** — John writing a vertical list on the board: Sweet, Sour, Salty, Spicy, pointing to each in turn.
> Sweet — Sour — Salty — Spicy, repeated in turn.

**Panel 4** — Mansoor over-enunciating "spicy" with theatrical emphasis, other kids glancing at him.
> "Spaiiiicy," Mansoor insisted, over-emphasizing the word.

**Panel 5** — Noura's hand shooting up higher than before, straining to be seen.
> "Sugar is sweet."

**Panel 6** — Ahmed raising his hand at a careful, measured height.
> "Lemon is sour."

**Panel 7** — Khalil answering confidently from his seat.
> "Fish is salty."

**Panel 8** — Mansoor leaping out of his seat again, arms wide, triumphant.
> "Chili is… spicy and KILL YOU!" — the class bursts out laughing.

**Panel 9** — Under the desk, Malood looking up with droopy, miserable eyes, making a small pitiful sound.
> Malood's eyes held the specific suffering of a goat who regretted a food choice.

**Panel 10** — Thuraya laughing, gesturing toward Malood.
> "Malood learned the lesson the hard way yesterday."

---

### Scene 4 — I Like, I Don't Like — And Saeed Speaks

**Panel 1** — John writing on the board beneath the taste words: "I like… I don't like…"
> John wrote: I like… / I don't like…

**Panel 2** — Mansoor crossing his arms defiantly across his chest, chin up.
> "I like spicy." Khalil: "Of course you do."

**Panel 3** — Safiya walking slowly down the aisle between desks, pausing beside each child.
> Safiya walked slowly between the desks, pausing at each one.

**Panel 4** — Ali standing to answer, Rahma smiling as she waits her turn, Marwa answering after.
> Ali: "I like salty." Rahma: "I like sweet." Marwa: "I don't like sour."

**Panel 5** — Safiya arriving at the back row, looking down gently at the quiet boy in the new seat.
> "And you… what is your name?"

**Panel 6** — The whole class turning slowly in their seats to look toward the back.
> Every small head turned, sensing something significant.

**Panel 7** — Thuraya's face softening with quiet emotion as she answers for him.
> "His name is Saeed."

**Panel 8** — Saeed rising slowly and awkwardly to his feet, copying the way he'd seen other children stand.
> Saeed stood — slowly, carefully, for the first time in a classroom.

**Panel 9** — Close on Saeed's face, small but steady, speaking his first English sentence.
> "I… I like sweet."

**Panel 10** — Safiya beaming at him, hand near her chest, visibly moved; Saeed sitting back down, his shirt fluttering with his fast heartbeat.
> "Very good, Saeed." — Mansoor whispers, "He talk ENGLISH!"

---

### Scene 5 — The Window

**Panel 1** — Malood under the desk shifting uneasily, nose twitching at something in the air.
> Malood's nose caught a smell before anyone else noticed.

**Panel 2** — John pausing mid-lesson, sniffing the air with a puzzled expression.
> "Why do I smell chili?"

**Panel 3** — Safiya's eyes widening in sudden recognition, turning toward the window.
> "Oh no."

**Panel 4** — POV through the window: in the schoolyard, another class eating lamb heaped with deep red chili.
> Outside, another class ate lamb with very red chili.

**Panel 5** — Wisps of chili-scented air drifting in through the window into the classroom.
> The smell crept in — slow, insinuating, unstoppable.

**Panel 6** — John's eyes watering involuntarily as he tries to keep teaching.
> John's eyes began to water, without his permission.

**Panel 7** — Mansoor grinning, delighted by the chaos.
> "Teacher… smell is spicy."

**Panel 8** — Malood marching stiffly to the door, then sneezing and coughing dramatically.
> Malood sneezed, coughed, and gave a despairing bleat.

**Panel 9** — John leaning out the open window to look, curious, unaware of what's coming.
> John: "Let me see." — a fatal mistake.

**Panel 10** — A visible wave/heat-shimmer of chili air striking John full in the face; his eyes slam shut, mouth open.
> A wave of chili air hit him full in the face.

**Panel 11** — John stumbling backward, both hands clapped to his face, students staring in shock.
> "AAAAAA! My mouth is ON FIRE!"

---

### Scene 6 — The News from Dhofar

**Panel 1** — Safiya rushing toward John to help, then recoiling herself as the chili smell reaches her too.
> Safiya rushed to help — then smelled it herself. "Oh no—"

**Panel 2** — Thuraya trying to stay composed, failing, laughing and coughing at once.
> Thuraya's professional composure finally cracked into laughter.

**Panel 3** — The classroom in chaos: Mansoor fallen sideways off his chair laughing, Ahmed quietly helping him up while wheezing with laughter himself.
> Mansoor fell out of his chair laughing; even Ahmed lost his composure.

**Panel 4** — Malood running in a frantic circle before diving back under the desk.
> Malood ran in a circle, then hid under the desk again.

**Panel 5** — John gulping down an entire water bottle, tears streaming, students watching in amusement.
> John drank in long emergency gulps: "It is… spicy air."

**Panel 6** — Safiya at the board, laughing as she writes fast: "Hot / Burn / My mouth is burning!"
> Safiya wrote on the board mid-laugh: "My mouth is burning!"

**Panel 7** — John pointing at himself as the class repeats the sentence; Mansoor shouting gleefully.
> Mansoor shouted: "TEACHER JOHN IS BURNING!"

**Panel 8** — John at the board again, calmer now, writing the past-simple lesson: "Yesterday, I ate…" pointing to Mansoor, then Malood.
> "Yesterday, I ate lamb — with chili." "Malood ate paper."

**Panel 9** — Saeed's hand rising again at the back of the room; the class turning to look, this time expectant rather than surprised.
> For the second time that morning, Saeed raised his hand.

**Panel 10** — Saeed speaking carefully, Thuraya watching him in silent, still pride.
> "Yesterday… I ate… dates. Sweet." — "Excellent sentence."

**Panel 11** — A small office radio in the background suddenly crackling to life with a solemn Arabic news broadcast; the classroom's mood visibly shifting, fan sound now seeming smaller.
> "…this is the latest news from Dhofar…" — the room went quiet.

**Panel 12** — John closing his notebook slowly; children rising and filing out quietly and orderly; Saeed pausing at the front to give Safiya one brief, grateful look before walking out into the yard where the radio still murmurs.
> "Class is over." Saeed's eyes, passing Safiya, said thank you.


---


## Palm Nineteen — The Zanzibari Trader and the Saffron Mask

### Scene 1 — An Honest Morning

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot of a Samail village at dawn: a rooster crowing atop a low clay wall, mist over palm groves, smoke curling from a hearth in a distant courtyard.
> "Aunt Azizah's rooster shouted his opinion to the whole village."

**Panel 2** — Interior of a simple clay-walled house. John lies in bed, eyes just opening, head slightly turned toward the window as if listening hard.
> "Still listening on the old English frequency — for the quiet mornings of Norwich."

**Panel 3** — Close-up on a hot metal pan over small coals three courtyards away, dough sizzling, smoke rising — the visual source of the smell John and Safiya react to.
> "The warm yeast-and-smoke smell of khubz — the village alarm-clock."

**Panel 4** — Safiya sitting up in bed, adjusting her headscarf, a soft dreamy smile on her face.
> "Something smells amazing."

**Panel 5** — John, still lying down, sniffing the air with a wry grin, one eyebrow raised toward Safiya.
> "I think your neighbour is making breakfast for the entire planet."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya striding in through the open wooden doorway without knocking, bright and energetic, mid-morning light behind her.
> "Good morning! Are you ready?"

**Panel 7** — Wide shot of the plain clay-walled room: half-open bags on the floor, a dress hanging from a single nail in the wall, John standing looking around.
> "I'm ready — where are we going again?"

**Panel 8** — Thuraya, eyes sparkling, hands gesturing outward; Safiya's mouth open in delighted surprise; John rubbing his hands together like a teacher plotting a lesson.
> "Muscat. Muttrah Souq — a real souq!"

---

### Scene 2 — The Pickup and the Mountains

**Panel 1** — A small goat (Malood) slipping through the doorway low and fast, sniffing at John's shoe before turning toward Safiya's cloth bag.
> "Something tiny and determined slipped through the doorway like a thief with hooves."

**Panel 2** — Safiya lifting her cloth bag out of reach as Malood stares up at her, ears flicking; John pointing sternly at the goat.
> "Malood. You are not invited."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya laughing, hand on hip, gesturing at Malood; Safiya sighing with a tired half-smile.
> "He thinks you are his family now."

**Panel 4** — Exterior: a dusty, battered Japanese pickup truck waiting outside, Uncle Nasser calm behind the wheel with a long bamboo stick beside him, a wooden bench and rope in the truck bed.
> "Uncle Nasser is waiting."

**Panel 5** — John climbing awkwardly into the truck bed, grabbing the side rail as the pickup shifts under his weight; slightly panicked expression.
> "The pickup moved, just a little, under his weight."

**Panel 6** — Safiya climbing in carefully, holding her scarf, laughing at John; Thuraya hopping in effortlessly beside them.
> "Hold tight. And don't scream. Like Mansoor."

**Panel 7** — The truck rolling through the village: palm shadows, a woman lifting a water pot at a doorway, two goats watching from a low wall.
> "The village opened behind them like a storybook."

**Panel 8** — Wide dramatic shot from the truck bed: massive layered brown Hajar mountains rising like cliffs against the sky, dwarfing the small pickup on the road below.
> "Real mountains — like they had frozen, mid-rage, into stone."

**Panel 9** — John leaning forward in the truck bed, eyes wide with wonder, pointing toward the peaks; Safiya staring beside him.
> "Safiya. Look. I've never seen mountains like this."

**Panel 10** — Thuraya glancing between the two awestruck English faces with a small knowing smile, wind in her scarf.
> "These look like they belong in a movie."

---

### Scene 3 — The First Glimpse of the Sea

**Panel 1** — Close on Safiya's face in the truck bed, chin lifted, eyes closed slightly as if tasting the changing air.
> "Sea," she said.

**Panel 2** — John's face lighting up boyishly, hair blown by a cooler, saltier wind.
> "The sea!"

**Panel 3** — Between two low coastal bluffs, a sudden gap reveals a wide shining sea for the first time — POV shot from the truck.
> "The sea stretched wide. Shining."

**Panel 4** — Wide harbor panorama: white fishing launches, dhows with lateen sails gathered at the mast, a distant dark cargo freighter on the horizon.
> "The same sea that Sinbad's Arabs had sailed."

**Panel 5** — Muttrah harbour bustling with activity — porters, fishermen, dockworkers all in motion at once.
> "The harbour was alive — like a giant classroom."

**Panel 6** — John pointing excitedly at the ships, a delighted, almost boyish expression, like a man stepping into a childhood storybook.
> "Ships!"

**Panel 7** — Safiya opening her notebook eagerly as the truck slows near the harbour, pen ready.
> "I'm ready."

**Panel 8** — The three climbing down from the stopped pickup; John wobbling slightly, legs unsteady from the ride.
> "His nervous system had not yet updated to solid ground."

**Panel 9** — Uncle Nasser, arms crossed, speaking quietly and seriously to the group before they leave; Thuraya raising an amused eyebrow beside John.
> "Stay close. And no trouble." / "That sentence is dangerous in Oman."

---

### Scene 4 — The Zanzibari Trader

**Panel 1** — A loose row of sellers near the port — some with baskets, some with cloths spread on the ground displaying small goods.
> "A small loose group of sellers near the port."

**Panel 2** — A Zanzibari trader sitting cross-legged beside a worn wooden box, sun-browned skin, plain cotton shirt and cap, sharp knowing smile, small items laid out on a cloth before him.
> "Beads, tiny perfume bottles, a beaten-metal mirror, shiny silver trinkets."

**Panel 3** — Safiya slowing to look, John leaning in curiously; Thuraya close behind, whispering a warning to them both.
> "Be careful. Some sellers in Muttrah have strong talking skills."

**Panel 4** — The trader looking up sharply at John and Safiya, eyes gleaming with commercial delight, arms opening wide in welcome.
> "Welcome, welcome! Come! Look! Very good things!"

**Panel 5** — The trader grinning proudly, gesturing over his wares toward John.
> "Small price. Big happiness."

**Panel 6** — The trader patting the wooden box affectionately, leaning in dramatically like a storyteller.
> "This box? Magic box. It has everything."

**Panel 7** — Safiya whispering to John with delighted eyes, watching the trader perform.
> "He is a storyteller."

**Panel 8** — John clearing his throat in teacher-mode, turning between the trader and his companions, finger raised.
> "Okay. Vocabulary. What do you sell?"

**Panel 9** — The trader's eyes shining with respect, nodding at John's fluent negotiation phrasing.
> "Ah. You know. You learn fast."

**Panel 10** — John pointing to the beads and then a tiny perfume bottle; the trader holding up the bottle with a mock-serious face.
> "One drop, you smell like rich person. Two drops, you become problem."

---

### Scene 5 — The Ship Key

**Panel 1** — John pointing to a small shiny silver object shaped like a key with a worked handle, curious expression.
> "And this?"

**Panel 2** — The trader hesitating for a beat, eyes flicking to the object, thinking fast.
> "A… ship key."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya turning away, shoulders shaking, laughing helplessly into her scarf; John and Safiya exchanging puzzled, amused looks.
> "A key for a ship?"

**Panel 4** — Thuraya, still laughing, finally facing John directly to correct him.
> "No, Mr. John. That is not real."

**Panel 5** — The trader backpedaling quickly, hands up, switching tactics with a grin.
> "Okay okay! Not ship key. It is… decoration!"

**Panel 6** — Safiya whispering to Thuraya with a delighted smile, watching the trader work.
> "He is trying to sell a story. Not an object."

**Panel 7** — John in teacher mode again, holding up a finger toward the trader, who repeats after him eagerly.
> "I want beads."

**Panel 8** — The trader suddenly pointing straight at John, then at Safiya in turn, playing the new phrase back as a sales pitch.
> "You want beads! You want perfume!"

**Panel 9** — The trader holding the perfume bottle aloft like a trophy toward Safiya, triumphant grin.
> "One drop, you smell like QUEEN!"

**Panel 10** — Thuraya stepping firmly between the trader and her friends, polite but final.
> "We are going to the souq. Thank you."

**Panel 11** — The trader opening his wooden box with a theatrical flourish, pulling out the small beaten-metal mirror and offering it to Safiya, who hesitates.
> "Wait! Gift. Small gift. Free. Welcome gift."

**Panel 12** — Safiya taking the mirror with an embarrassed smile as the trader immediately turns to John, pointing at him.
> "Now you buy." / "I thought it was kindness."

---

### Scene 6 — I Don't Want To Buy

**Panel 1** — John facing the trader squarely, speaking slowly and clearly, one hand raised for emphasis.
> "I don't want to buy."

**Panel 2** — The trader frowning, puzzled, repeating the phrase back uncertainly.
> "You don't want to… buy?"

**Panel 3** — Thuraya and Safiya exchanging a small approving whisper behind John's back.
> "Good sentence." / "Very good sentence."

**Panel 4** — The trader's face shifting into a respectful, professional smile, already glancing toward the next passerby.
> "Okay okay. But you can… look."

**Panel 5** — The group walking away from the trader's cloth, toward the souq entrance in the near distance.
> "They started walking away — toward the souq."

**Panel 6** — John turning to Thuraya, voice low, half-suspicious.
> "Was that… a trick?"

**Panel 7** — Thuraya smiling sweetly, the same knowing smile John has learned to watch out for.
> "It is not a trick. It is… a lesson."

**Panel 8** — Safiya walking and writing quickly in her notebook at the same time, pen racing across the page.
> New words: mountain, sea, ship, port, seller, beads, perfume, gift, mirror, price.

**Panel 9** — Wide shot of the souq entrance ahead: narrow shadowed lanes, overlapping crowds, porters with trays on their heads, spice smells implied by visual haze.
> "I think this place is going to teach me English again."

**Panel 10** — Uncle Nasser calling back to the group from a few steps ahead, gesturing them forward; John squaring his shoulders.
> "Yalla!" / "Okay. Next task."

**Panel 11** — Behind them, the trader already resetting his cloth for the next customer, waving cheerfully as if nothing happened; John muttering under his breath.
> "Small price. Big trouble."

**Panel 12** — John stopping mid-step at the souq's dark entrance, hearing a loud familiar voice ahead in the crowd; Thuraya's smile widening, Safiya blinking in confusion.
> "Please tell me Sadoo is not here." / "Sadoo is everywhere."


---


## Palm Twenty — Of Goats and Grammar

### Scene 1 — Everything, All At Once

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot: the covered arcade of Muttrah Souq, cedar beams overhead, sacks of cardamom and turmeric, hanging fish stalls, oud smoke curling from braziers. John, Safiya, and Thuraya enter, John's face registering sensory overload.
> "The first thing that entered John's nose was… everything."

**Panel 2** — Same arcade, wider crowd: Indian traders in white shirts, a blacksmith hammering at a far anvil, donkeys, porters. Emphasize dense layered noise with visual motion lines/crowd.
> "The specific dense, layered, mercantile noise of a port city."

**Panel 3** — Close on John's face turning sharply toward an unseen sound, eyes wide, mid-step frozen.
> "Sadoo's voice, unmistakable, full-throated, rose above the rest."

**Panel 4** — John standing stock-still in the crowded lane; Safiya bumping gently into his back, surprised.
> "Why did you stop?" / "That… voice."

**Panel 5** — Thuraya beside them, eyes closed for a beat, composing herself before the reunion.
> "Yes. That is Sadoo."

**Panel 6** — Three-shot: Safiya questioning, Thuraya correcting gently, faint smile of village ownership.
> "Our Sadoo," Thuraya corrected.

### Scene 2 — The Happy Storm

**Panel 1** — Group weaving through a narrow souq path, past porters balancing trays of dates on their heads and a tall Baluchi man with a coiled rope over his shoulder.
> Threading through the crowd toward a familiar shout.

**Panel 2** — Sadoo standing outside a small shop, dusty dishdasha and cap, surrounded by a small mountain of bags, boxes, and ropes; a confused man nearby holds a basket.
> Sadoo, mid-commerce, surrounded by his haul.

**Panel 3** — Close on Sadoo's face lighting up in full celebration as he spots the three of them.
> [ARABIC] هااااه! الأستاذ جون! صفيه! مس ثريا!

**Panel 4** — Sadoo barreling toward them at full speed, arms already reaching out, other shoppers stepping aside.
> Like a happy storm, at full speed.

**Panel 5** — Sadoo gripping John's hand in a strong forearm handshake; John smiling carefully, a little overwhelmed.
> "Hello, Sadoo."

**Panel 6** — Sadoo reaching for Thuraya's hand; Thuraya stepping back quickly, practiced and firm.
> [ARABIC] سادو، ويش تسوي هنا؟ — "Sadoo, what are you doing here?"

**Panel 7** — Sadoo puffing his chest with pride, gesturing at his pile of goods.
> "I am a big trader now! Muttrah! All is well!"

**Panel 8** — John staring down at one of the bags beside Sadoo's feet — it visibly bulges/moves. His face frozen mid-recoil.
> "…Why is your bag moving?"

**Panel 9** — Sadoo glancing down placidly at the bag while John steps back in alarm.
> [ARABIC] طازج. — "Fresh (fish)." John: "I am not ready for this country."

### Scene 3 — The Bargaining

**Panel 1** — Sadoo gripping John's arm, hauling him toward a shop crammed with stacked copper pots, brass wedding trays, and round sinyah platters.
> "Come, come! This is a good shop!"

**Panel 2** — Interior of the metal shop: a seller standing with arms crossed, serious neutral expression, surrounded by hand-hammered copperware.
> A man with crossed arms and a very serious face.

**Panel 3** — Sadoo pointing proudly at the seller, introducing him to John, Safiya, and Thuraya.
> "This is my friend. He will give us a special price."

**Panel 4** — Sadoo and the seller in rapid-fire exchange, hands flying — palms open, closing, indicating prices and rejection.
> A whole second-language of hands running alongside the words.

**Panel 5** — John turning to Thuraya, baffled; Safiya leaning in and whispering, amused.
> "It looks like a fight." / "Both sides enjoy it."

**Panel 6** — Sadoo pushing a large brass tray toward John like presenting evidence, coaching him urgently.
> "John! Tell him! Tell him!"

**Panel 7** — John, teacher-mode posture, pointing at the tray and speaking carefully to the seller, who raises an eyebrow slowly.
> "I want this… but cheap."

**Panel 8** — Sadoo slapping his own forehead hard, mid-facepalm, as Thuraya translates the seller's flat refusal.
> "This is not cheap." John: "Okay. This is not cheap."

**Panel 9** — Seller crossing his arms tighter, turning away slightly; Sadoo staring at John in disbelief.
> [ARABIC] يا رجل! خربتها! — "Man! You ruined it!"

### Scene 4 — Sometimes English Is Magic

**Panel 1** — Safiya biting back a laugh, hand half-covering her mouth; John defending himself with open palms.
> "I said exactly what you told me."

**Panel 2** — The seller picking up the tray, carrying it back inside the shop, and turning his back on the group.
> The three-part retreat: pick up, put away, turn back.

**Panel 3** — Sadoo gasping, hands to his face, then whirling toward Thuraya in desperation.
> [ARABIC] راح! مس ثريا! قولي له شي! — "He's gone! Say something to him!"

**Panel 4** — Thuraya standing with arms calmly crossed, letting Sadoo stew, unmoved.
> "You started this."

**Panel 5** — Sadoo spinning around and locking eyes on Safiya, face lighting with a new plan.
> [ARABIC] صفيه! قولي له شي بالإنجليزي! — "Safiya! Tell him in English!"

**Panel 6** — Safiya facing the seller's back, taking a breath, speaking carefully and politely.
> "Excuse me… we are just looking."

**Panel 7** — The seller turning slowly, evaluating her tone, then answering in broken English.
> "Looking… no buying?" / "Yes. Just looking."

**Panel 8** — Sadoo whispering in amazement while Thuraya smiles knowingly at John.
> "It worked!" / "Sometimes, English is magic."

### Scene 5 — Where Is Mansoor?

**Panel 1** — Deeper into the souq: pink silk scarves, stacked lemons, dark red carpets hanging overhead, a butcher's stall with pomegranate juice on the cutting board.
> Colours accumulating — a covered market that wants the eye.

**Panel 2** — Thuraya stopping mid-stride, eyes scanning the group, doing an involuntary headcount.
> [ARABIC] وين منصوور؟ — "Where is Mansoor?"

**Panel 3** — The four remaining adults (John, Safiya, Thuraya, Sadoo) turning in a tight circle, counting each other, faces tightening.
> One. Two. Three. No Mansoor.

**Panel 4** — Sadoo shrugging easily, unworried, gesturing vaguely at the stalls.
> [ARABIC] يمكن راح يشوف شي. — "Maybe he went to look at something."

**Panel 5** — John's face gone pale, stricken, next to Thuraya's suddenly sharp, urgent expression.
> "We must find him. Now."

**Panel 6** — The group splitting into two pairs at a fork in the lane — John and Safiya one way, Thuraya and Sadoo the other.
> Four adults, silently dividing the search.

**Panel 7** — Safiya ducking under a hanging rug, checking a narrow alley between two tailor stalls, calling out.
> "Mansoor!"

**Panel 8** — John and Safiya both stopping, heads turning toward a burst of high-pitched, familiar laughter nearby.
> Very familiar laughter.

**Panel 9** — Mansoor found at a toy-seller's stall, holding up a small curved wooden toy sword, eyes shining with delight.
> [ARABIC] مس ثريا! شوف! — "Miss Thuraya! Look!"

**Panel 10** — John gripping Mansoor's shoulder firmly, kneeling to his eye level; Mansoor's excitement fading into confusion.
> "You cannot disappear like that!"

**Panel 11** — Safiya crouched at Mansoor's eye level, hand on his arm, her knees in the dirt; Mansoor's face turning properly guilty.
> "You scared us." / [ARABIC] آسف. — "Sorry."

**Panel 12** — Thuraya taking Mansoor's hand firmly as the group regroups, Sadoo catching his breath beside them.
> "Stay with the group. Always."

### Scene 6 — The Shouting

**Panel 1** — The group walking on, then all turning sharply toward the sound of raised, angry voices back the way they came.
> Angry shouting. It was Sadoo's voice.

**Panel 2** — Sadoo squared off against another seller, both shouting, a small crowd starting to gather around them.
> [ARABIC] هذا مو اللي عطيتني! — "This is not what you gave me!"

**Panel 3** — The seller pointing accusingly into an open bag, revealing something broken inside; onlookers pressing closer.
> The seller pointed at a bag. Inside was something broken.

**Panel 4** — Thuraya, John, and Safiya arriving at the edge of the crowd, tense, John registering the shift from commercial to serious.
> "This is becoming… big." / "Very big."

**Panel 5** — A Royal Oman Police officer stepping into the circle; the crowd falling instantly silent around him.
> The specific instant hush of a crowd recognising the uniform.

**Panel 6** — The officer questioning Sadoo directly, Sadoo answering fast and defensively, hands raised.
> [ARABIC] ما كسرت شي! — "I didn't break anything!"

**Panel 7** — John stepping forward into the circle, addressing the officer directly, Safiya and Thuraya watching anxiously behind him.
> "Excuse me. We are teachers. He is our friend."

**Panel 8** — The officer studying John for a long beat, then turning to speak to the seller; Sadoo's face falling as he awaits the verdict.
> "Problem?" / "Yes. But… maybe misunderstanding."

**Panel 9** — Sadoo reluctantly counting out money to the seller, shoulders slumped, the crowd already beginning to disperse.
> [ARABIC] حتى لو ما كسرت؟ — "Even if I didn't break it?"

**Panel 10** — The group walking back toward the pickup truck, visibly tired, Uncle Nasser waiting by the vehicle counting heads.
> [ARABIC] كلكم موجودين؟ — "Are you all here?"

**Panel 11** — Inside the moving pickup: Mansoor asleep clutching his wooden sword, Safiya writing in her notebook, John leaning back with eyes closed, Sadoo grinning up front, mountains rolling past the window.
> "Beautiful trip." / "Beautiful… and dangerous."


---


## Palm Twenty-One — Tofes 17 — The Day Sky Almost Fell on Samail

### Scene 1 — The Mountains in His Head

**Panel 1** — Early morning in a dusty Samail village yard; sun, quiet, ordinary calm after the Muttrah trip. John stands alone holding a teacup with both hands.
> "The village looked ordinary again."

**Panel 2** — Close on John's face, eyes slightly red from lack of sleep, steam rising from his strong black tea with cardamom and cinnamon.
> Too much thinking, not smoke this time.

**Panel 3** — Safiya at a rope clothesline, folding English clothes the Omani way — corner, third fold, press against her thigh — pausing to gaze at distant mountains.
> She watches the mountains, as if waiting for a wooden ship to appear.

**Panel 4** — Ms. Thuraya enters the yard carrying a small basket of papers and chalk, wearing an unusually bright, knowing smile.
> John: "You are smiling like you already know what I will say."

**Panel 5** — Thuraya, smile widening, sits on the edge of a low stone wall.
> Thuraya: "Maybe I do."

**Panel 6** — Safiya leans toward John, teasing, hands still holding a folded shirt.
> Safiya: "Are you waiting for a letter? A miracle? A goat?"

**Panel 7** — John points seriously toward the road, lowering his voice as if the date palms might overhear.
> John: "I'm waiting for… answers."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya, hands folded in her lap on the stone wall, speaking with quiet authority.
> Thuraya: "In Samail, warnings and jokes are cousins."

**Panel 9** — Safiya laughing openly at John, clothes still in hand, mountains behind her.
> Safiya: "You chose Oman in 1973. Normal is on vacation."

### Scene 2 — John Decides

**Panel 1** — Malood the baby goat marches into the yard like a king, sniffing at Safiya's clean clothes and chewing a scarf corner.
> Malood struts in, short legs, full confidence.

**Panel 2** — Safiya gently pulls the scarf away, sighing at the goat.
> Safiya: "This goat has no shame."

**Panel 3** — Malood, offended, abandons the scarf and charges John's worn English canvas bag.
> Malood targets John's bag again.

**Panel 4** — John lifts the bag out of reach; Malood jumps and fails, looking cross.
> John: "No, Malood. I need this bag for school."

**Panel 5** — Thuraya chuckles at the goat's failed jump, arms crossed.
> Thuraya: "He is practising. For future crimes."

**Panel 6** — John turns to Thuraya with a focused, nervous expression, finally asking the question he's held all morning.
> John: "Who is Shayb Khalaf. Really?"

**Panel 7** — Safiya freezes mid-fold of a garment; even Malood stops chewing; a tense stillness settles over the yard.
> The whole yard seems to wait at the name.

**Panel 8** — Thuraya, calm, looks off toward the older, wilder edge of the village where the clay houses grow older.
> Thuraya: "He is an old man. People fear him because they don't understand."

**Panel 9** — Thuraya shrugs lightly, explaining village rumor.
> Thuraya: "Stories grow faster than palm trees. One day a lonely man, next a wizard."

**Panel 10** — John stands straighter, jaw set, having made a decision; Safiya looks alarmed beside him.
> John: "I want to talk to him." / Safiya: "John… why?"

**Panel 11** — Thuraya raises both hands innocently, smiling sharply as Safiya narrows her eyes at her.
> Thuraya: "Go. But don't blame me if you run."

### Scene 3 — Under the Shawab Tree

**Panel 1** — John, Safiya, and Thuraya walk a quiet dirt path away from the village center; village sounds fading, Malood bouncing along behind them.
> The path to Shayb Khalaf's house felt longer than it looked.

**Panel 2** — Safiya, uneasy, glances at John as they walk.
> Safiya: "Maybe we should not bother him."

**Panel 3** — John walking faster, resolute.
> John: "We are not bothering. We are… friendly teachers."

**Panel 4** — The three of them rehearsing greeting phrases aloud as they near the house — John reciting, Safiya adding a line, Thuraya coaching.
> John: "Hello. Good morning. How are you?" / Thuraya: "Don't forget 'sorry to disturb you.'"

**Panel 5** — Wide establishing shot: a small old clay house with a rough wooden door, and the massive ancient shawab tree standing before it.
> A tree that had watched the village for a hundred years without blinking.

**Panel 6** — Under the tree's deep shade, an old man — Shayb Khalaf — sits cross-legged on a mat, holding a small Gulf-style coffee finjan, walking stick beside him, a set of dominoes already laid out neatly in front of him.
> Dominoes already arranged, as if expecting a guest.

**Panel 7** — John stops short, staring at the dominoes with a visible flicker of unease; Safiya whispers close to him.
> Safiya: "Why are the dominoes already ready?"

**Panel 8** — Thuraya, expression calm and unhelpful, murmurs back while gently restraining Malood by his neck-rope.
> Thuraya: "Because he is always ready."

**Panel 9** — John clears his throat and calls out "Good morning" twice; Shayb Khalaf finally answers without looking up, startling all three.
> Shayb Khalaf: "Good morning." (in clear English)

### Scene 4 — Sit

**Panel 1** — John stands frozen, mouth open in shock; Safiya's prepared sentence dies on her lips; Thuraya suppresses a laugh nearby.
> John: "You… you speak English."

**Panel 2** — Shayb Khalaf finally looks up — sharp, hawk-like, tired eyes — and nods once.
> Shayb Khalaf: "Little."

**Panel 3** — John, nervous and overly polite, gestures between himself and the two women.
> John: "We are teachers. New teachers. We came from Britain."

**Panel 4** — Shayb Khalaf stares a long moment, then points down at the dominoes laid on the mat.
> Shayb Khalaf: "Sit."

**Panel 5** — Safiya touches John's arm, uncertain; John swallows and nods.
> Safiya: "John… do we sit?" / John: "We sit."

**Panel 6** — All three sit down under the tree's cool, deep shade; Thuraya settles slightly to the side, watching like a referee.
> They sat under the shawab tree, in shade deeper than the rest of the village.

**Panel 7** — Shayb Khalaf pushes the dominoes toward John, who lifts a piece carefully, as though it might bite.
> John: "You want to… play?" / Shayb Khalaf: "Play."

**Panel 8** — John places a domino; Shayb Khalaf immediately places a stronger piece, unbothered, eyes calm.
> Safiya, leaning back: "He is good." / John, stiffly: "Too good."

### Scene 5 — Muttrah Is Noisy

**Panel 1** — Mid-game, Shayb Khalaf places a piece without looking up, speaking casually.
> Shayb Khalaf: "Muttrah is noisy. You went there. You saw ships."

**Panel 2** — Safiya's face changes, alarmed; she leans in.
> Safiya: "How do you know?"

**Panel 3** — Shayb Khalaf gestures loosely toward the village behind him, unbothered.
> Shayb Khalaf: "News travels. People."

**Panel 4** — Close on Shayb Khalaf looking directly at John, one eyebrow raised as John's face visibly pales.
> Shayb Khalaf: "And you got into trouble." / John: "We did not get into trouble."

**Panel 5** — Safiya whispers sharply into John's ear, off to the side.
> Safiya: "John. Your face is confessing."

**Panel 6** — Close-up on a single domino piece landing with a loud, sharp click in the silence under the tree.
> Click — louder than it should have been.

**Panel 7** — Shayb Khalaf, eyes flicking briefly to Thuraya then back to John, speaks a warning.
> Shayb Khalaf: "Next time, listen."

**Panel 8** — Shayb Khalaf glances toward Malood, who is chewing the corner of John's bag again nearby; Safiya turns her head sharply.
> Safiya: "How do you know about Malood?" / Shayb Khalaf: "Because he is doing it now."

**Panel 9** — Shayb Khalaf places his final domino piece, completing the line; John stares down at it in disbelief.
> He won. John stared like it was an official stamp of defeat.

**Panel 10** — John stands up quickly, unsettled, gathering himself to leave; Shayb Khalaf calmly lifts his coffee cup and drinks.
> John: "Thank you. We should go." / Shayb Khalaf: "You are afraid."

**Panel 11** — Shayb Khalaf leans in slightly, speaking a quiet line in Arabic that visibly rattles John; Safiya's eyes widen, Thuraya's smile turns sharp.
> Shayb Khalaf: "واحليلك باه… تفزع." ("Oh you poor dear… you're scared.")

**Panel 12** — John spins back to face the old man, voice shaking, while Shayb Khalaf leans back, unreadable.
> John: "You said that before." / Shayb Khalaf: "Maybe you imagined it."

### Scene 6 — If I…, I Will…

**Panel 1** — John grabs his sandals in a rush; Safiya rises and offers a quick thank-you to the still-seated old man.
> Safiya: "Thank you, Shayb Khalaf." (No answer.)

**Panel 2** — John turns back one last time, blurting out the real question that had been building all along.
> John: "Why do people say you are a sorcerer?"

**Panel 3** — Thuraya's smile vanishes; Safiya holds her breath; Shayb Khalaf's eyes darken though his face stays still.
> Shayb Khalaf: "People need a story."

**Panel 4** — John presses further, mentioning the "maghsoob" rumor; Thuraya's head snaps toward him in warning.
> Thuraya: "Why would you say that here?"

**Panel 5** — Shayb Khalaf stares at John for a long, heavy moment — the weight of decades of the village's false sorcery story about his son Obaid behind his eyes.
> A lifetime lived inside a story that was never true.

**Panel 6** — Shayb Khalaf delivers his final, heavy warning, unmoving on his mat.
> Shayb Khalaf: "Don't play with fear. Fear plays back."

**Panel 7** — John, barefoot, sandals clutched like a trophy, breaks into a full sprint away from the shawab tree; Safiya runs after him half-laughing, half-worried.
> Safiya: "John! John, slow down!"

**Panel 8** — Behind them, Thuraya walks calmly, glancing back once at Shayb Khalaf, still seated motionless under the tree.
> Shayb Khalaf sat unmoving, as if they were the strange ones.

**Panel 9** — Classroom interior: John arrives still out of breath; the students stare at his face; Mansoor stage-whispers to the class.
> Mansoor: "Teacher John… you run!"

**Panel 10** — Thuraya writes the day's grammar target on the chalkboard in clear lettering while Safiya claps to start the lesson.
> Chalkboard: "IF I …, I WILL …" / Safiya: "Today we use our trip to learn English!"

**Panel 11** — John stands before the class, pointing at himself, turning his own fear into a teaching example as students burst into laughter.
> John: "If I see Shayb Khalaf… I will run."

**Panel 12** — Wide shot of the village outside the window at dusk, and a final image of Shayb Khalaf alone under the shawab tree, calmly laying out a second set of dominoes.
> Silence kept its secrets — a second game, already being arranged.


---


## Palm Twenty-Two — The Sky Falls on Samail — A Village in Rain

### Scene 1 — The Sound That Did Not Belong

**Panel 1** — Dim blue-grey dawn light in a simple Omani bedroom; John asleep on a low bed/mat, roof beams visible above. A single drop of water hangs from a crack in the clay ceiling.
> "Tup… tup… tup…"

**Panel 2** — Sophia sitting bolt upright in bed, alert and listening, head tilted toward the ceiling, John still lying beside her.
> Sophia, already awake, listening carefully.

**Panel 3** — Close on Sophia's face, half-wondering, half-alarmed, whispering toward John.
> "I think… the sky is leaking."

**Panel 4** — A cold drop lands on John's nose; he jolts upright in one motion, eyes wide.
> "The sky is WHAT?"

**Panel 5** — Wide shot of the room: multiple drops falling onto pillow, floor, and the woven bisat rug, a dark wet patch spreading on the carpet.
> Drops multiply across the room.

**Panel 6** — Sophia looking up at the ceiling, then down at the spreading dark mark on the rug, her expression shifting to urgency.
> "The roof… it's dripping!"

**Panel 7** — Sophia dashing toward the kitchen, motion-blurred, reaching for bowls and a tin plate.
> Sophia races for containers.

**Panel 8** — Sophia placing bowls, a cup, and a tin plate under various drips around the room, moving fast.
> "Emergency! Emergency!"

**Panel 9** — John standing in the middle of the room, still half-asleep, holding a cup uncertainly, watching Sophia work.
> "Is this normal? Is this… a flood?"

### Scene 2 — Welcome to Oman Rain

**Panel 1** — Ms. Thuraya standing calmly in the open doorway, scarf already on, morning light and rain behind her.
> Thuraya arrives, unhurried, unsurprised.

**Panel 2** — Thuraya's point of view: dripping ceiling, Sophia mid-run with bowls, John standing frozen holding his cup with mock dignity.
> Thuraya surveys the scene.

**Panel 3** — Close on Thuraya's calm, faintly amused smile.
> "Relax. It's rain."

**Panel 4** — John frozen in place, cup still in hand, blinking.
> "Rain?"

**Panel 5** — Sophia stopped mid-stride, a bowl in each hand, stunned.
> "Rain?"

**Panel 6** — Sudden violent flash and shake: thunder rolls through the clay walls, dust or small objects trembling, everyone's bodies jolt.
> BOOOOOM.

**Panel 7** — Sophia and John both scream, John's reaction a beat behind hers, arms half-raised.
> Sophia screams; John follows a second later.

**Panel 8** — Outside the door, Malood the goat rearing back startled, mid-bleat.
> Malood screams too, from outside the door.

**Panel 9** — Thuraya laughing openly, hand near her mouth, clearly delighted by their reaction.
> "Welcome to Oman rain."

### Scene 3 — A Village Transformed

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot: the dusty village now dark and wet, thin streams of water running along walls, paths, and falaj channels.
> The dry village is dry no longer.

**Panel 2** — Close on Sophia standing outside, eyes closed, breathing in deeply, chest lifted.
> Sophia breathes in the wet-earth smell.

**Panel 3** — Group of village children running outside barefoot in dishdashas and simple dresses, no coats, faces lit with joy in the rain.
> The children take the rain into themselves completely.

**Panel 4** — Mansoor mid-jump, soaked head to toe, landing in a puddle and sending muddy spray in all directions, even onto his own face.
> "Maaaaaan! It's raining! It's REALLY raining!"

**Panel 5** — Ali standing still and upright, face lifted to the sky, eyes closed, rain streaming down calmly.
> "It's beautiful."

**Panel 6** — Noura with face turned up to the rain, grinning, arms slightly out.
> "Cold! But nice!"

**Panel 7** — Ahmed sheltering under a small clay overhang, clutching his book tightly against his chest, worried expression.
> "My book… my book is getting wet…"

**Panel 8** — Khalil crouched by the largest puddle in sight, just having thrown a stone, watching a small brown wave break against the path edge.
> "Look! River!"

**Panel 9** — Sophia standing in the open rain with an empty bowl balanced uselessly on her head, hair and dress soaked, laughing.
> "This is… wonderful. And my hair is already ruined."

**Panel 10** — Everyone flinching at once as thunder cracks again, closer this time; John jumping visibly.
> Another BOOM, closer this time.

**Panel 11** — Thuraya pointing firmly toward the distant mountains, everyone's eyes following her arm.
> "Look."

### Scene 4 — The Wadi Coming Down

**Panel 1** — Wide shot between the mountains: a thick brown mass of water advancing down a valley that had been dry scenery until now.
> Something brown and full and alive is coming down the valley.

**Panel 2** — Ali pointing at the wadi, shouting with wide eyes, clearly recognizing the sight instinctively.
> "Halaaaa! The wadi is coming down!"

**Panel 3** — Closer view of the wadi: fast, strong brown current filling the riverbed with visible force and motion.
> The dry riverbed is a real river now.

**Panel 4** — John staring at the flood, mouth open, stunned by the scale of it.
> "That is not a river. That is a moving mountain."
