**Panel 2** — Safiya stopping abruptly, pointing excitedly at a small lizard on the wall.
> "Oh my God… it is so beautiful." "The lizard!"

**Panel 3** — Thuraya's face frozen in horror, stepping backward away from the wall.
> Thuraya's face froze. "Maah… No. No. No."

**Panel 4** — Fatkat grimacing, hand near her mouth in disgust, girls beginning to giggle around her.
> "Ya satir… what is this disgusting thing?"

**Panel 5** — Safiya walking closer to the wall, delighted, reaching a hand toward the lizard.
> Safiya walked closer, happily. "It is cute!"

**Panel 6** — Safiya gently cupping the small lizard in her open palm, smiling at it.
> She took it, gently, in her hand.

**Panel 7** — The girls recoiling and screaming, pointing at Safiya's hand.
> "Throw it! Throw it!"

**Panel 8** — Safiya, unbothered, looking at the lizard in her palm, Fatkat beside her with arms crossed skeptically.
> "The grasshopper also is a living creature, but you didn't like it, right?"

**Panel 9** — The lizard leaping from Safiya's hand back onto the wall and darting away, everyone's eyes following it.
> The lizard jumped. Straight to the wall. And ran away.

**Panel 10** — Thuraya mid-scream, running a few steps away; girls doubled over laughing.
> Thuraya screamed and ran. The girls laughed so much they almost fell.

**Panel 11** — Safiya shrugging with a sheepish smile at the group.
> "Okay… maybe not so beautiful."

### Scene 4 — At Al-Mu'allim Hamad's House

**Panel 1** — Exterior/entry of a simple whitewashed clay house, palm-leaf mats visible through the open door, a small wooden shelf with religious books and a brass coffee pot in the corner.
> The house was simple. Clean. Calm.

**Panel 2** — Everyone seated cross-legged on woven mats in the front room, water basin being brought in.
> They sat on mats. Water was brought.

**Panel 3** — Thuraya leaning toward the students, whispering instructions, hands motioning to wash.
> Thuraya whispered, "First… wash hands."

**Panel 4** — Line of students at a water basin outside washing their hands; John scrubbing his hands vigorously a third time.
> John washed his hands three times. "Just in case."

**Panel 5** — Everyone seated again in a neat row on the mats; Safiya leaning over whispering etiquette reminders to the children.
> "Back straight. Legs in. Don't touch food yet."

**Panel 6** — Mansoor's hand already reaching toward the food tray; John gently pulling his hand back.
> Mansoor was already touching food. John pulled his hand back. "No."

**Panel 7** — Mansoor pouting, whispering to John.
> "But I am hungry."

**Panel 8** — Al-Mu'allim Hamad entering the room: an elderly man with a calm demeanor, white beard, kind eyes, everyone turning toward him with respect.
> Then Al-Mu'allim Hamad came in. Calm. White beard. Kind eyes.

**Panel 9** — Al-Mu'allim Hamad seated, welcoming the group with an open gesture; everyone responding in unison.
> "Welcome." Everyone answered together. "Welcome."

**Panel 10** — Large tray of food being set down in the center: rice, meat, bread, dates; Safiya's eyes wide with delight.
> Food came. Rice. Meat. Bread. Dates. "So beautiful…"

**Panel 11** — Close on John's face, quiet and focused, lips forming an unfamiliar word for the first time as Thuraya says it softly beside him.
> Thuraya said softly, "We say bismillah." John repeated it. "Bismillah…"

### Scene 5 — The Meal and the Quiet After

**Panel 1** — The group eating together on the mats, initially neat and polite, small careful bites.
> They ate. Slowly. Politely. At first.

**Panel 2** — John mid-meal, already reaching for a second helping, manners slipping.
> Then John forgot etiquette. Then he forgot the world.

**Panel 3** — John hunched over his plate, eyes glazed, cheeks full, host's hand refilling his plate yet again.
> He ate. And ate. And ate.

**Panel 4** — Fatkat leaning to whisper to Safiya, both watching John with amused disbelief.
> "He eat like he never see food before."

**Panel 5** — Tea glasses being poured after the meal; everyone settling back, more relaxed.
> After lunch, tea came. They sat.

**Panel 6** — Al-Mu'allim Hamad speaking gently, hands folded, John listening intently beside the others.
> Al-Mu'allim Hamad talked about life. Kindness. Respect.

**Panel 7** — Close on John's face, contemplative, a subtle new stillness in his expression.
> He did not understand everything. But he felt something… peaceful.

**Panel 8** — Walking back along the village lane, John unusually quiet, Safiya glancing at him with concern.
> Safiya looked at him. "Are you okay?"

**Panel 9** — John touching his stomach with a small tired smile, then turning more serious.
> "My stomach is very okay. But… today was… different."

**Panel 10** — Three-way glance: Thuraya, Safiya, and John pausing on the path, John searching for words.
> "The way he lives. The way he talks. The calm. The respect. The prayer…"

**Panel 11** — John stopping in the lane, looking down, then speaking quietly to both women.
> "I think… I want to learn more about Islam."

**Panel 12** — Thuraya's face softening, no joke this time, a quiet, warm look toward John.
> Thuraya said softly, "When you are ready… I will explain."

### Scene 6 — A Stomach Like a Separate Country

**Panel 1** — John entering the house, moving slowly and heavily.
> John entered the house. He tried to sit. He failed.

**Panel 2** — John collapsing flat onto a floor mat, groaning theatrically.
> He fell on the mat. "My stomach… is now… a separate country."

**Panel 3** — Safiya standing over him laughing, hands on hips.
> Safiya laughed. "You should not eat like that."

**Panel 4** — John, eyes closed, hand raised weakly in mock defense.
> "But we should accept hospitality."

**Panel 5** — Safiya tossing a pillow that lands on John's chest.
> She threw a pillow at him.

**Panel 6** — Wide shot of the room bathed in low golden afternoon light through a small window; Thuraya's silhouette audible/visible in the next room making tea.
> The afternoon light painted the wall gold. Thuraya's tea-making sounds carried through.

**Panel 7** — John lying still on his back, pillow on chest, eyes closed, speaking softly to himself.
> "He is a good man," he said. "Al-Mu'allim Hamad. He is a good man."

**Panel 8** — Safiya sitting down quietly near him, a thoughtful, tender expression.
> Safiya sat near him. She did not say anything at first.

**Panel 9** — Safiya looking at John gently, a small smile.
> "Yes," she said, softly. "He is."

**Panel 10** — Exterior shot: a goat (Malood) standing in the courtyard, bleating once as the golden afternoon settles over the quiet village.
> Outside, a goat — almost certainly Malood — bleated once.


---


## Palm Thirty-One — I Am Afraid Of — And Thuraya's Secret

### Scene One — The Tired Fan and the Topic of Fear

**Panel 1** — Wide shot of a hot, sun-baked village classroom in 1973 Oman: a slow ceiling fan overhead, chalk dust in the air. Safiya stands near the chalkboard holding a small paper, smiling; John already looks sweaty and warm.
> "The morning of 'I am afraid of…' had simply arrived in the room."

**Panel 2** — Close on Safiya at the board, holding up the small paper, smiling brightly at the class of village children seated at their desks.
> "Today, we will talk about fear."

**Panel 3** — Mansoor stands up proudly among the desks, chest out, defiant expression.
> "I am not afraid of anything."

**Panel 4** — Khalil leans toward Mansoor, muttering under his breath with a smirk.
> "Liar."

**Panel 5** — John at the chalkboard, writing the phrase in large letters, chalk in hand.
> I am afraid of …

**Panel 6** — John turns to face the class, gesturing for them to repeat; children's mouths open in unison.
> "Repeat, everyone… I am afraid of…"

**Panel 7** — Safiya claps happily, then turns back to the board with chalk in hand.
> "Good! Now we will learn insect words."

**Panel 8** — Safiya drawing crude, childlike chalk sketches on the board: a bee, a grasshopper, a butterfly, a lizard — endearingly bad but enthusiastic.
> Small bad pictures: a bee, a grasshopper, a butterfly, a lizard.

**Panel 9** — Mansoor pointing and laughing at one of the drawings; Ali, offended, cocks his arm back with a piece of chalk.
> "This one looks like Ali!" — Ali throws chalk at him.

### Scene Two — The Confession Game

**Panel 1** — Safiya standing at the front, addressing the seated children, gesturing "one by one."
> "One by one. You say your name and what you are afraid of."

**Panel 2** — Noura stands shyly at her desk, hands clasped, looking down.
> "I am afraid of… bee."

**Panel 3** — John, smiling encouragingly, prompts her; Noura repeats more confidently.
> "I am afraid of bees."

**Panel 4** — Khalil stands tall, proud, chin up, declaring boldly to the class.
> "I am afraid of nothing."

**Panel 5** — A fly buzzes right next to Khalil's ear (small motion lines); he flinches mid-scream, eyes wide, the class bursting into laughter around him.
> Khalil screams — the class explodes with laughter.

**Panel 6** — Ali standing, uneasy, glancing sideways.
> "I am afraid of… snake."

**Panel 7** — Ahmed stands very seriously, solemn expression; classmates around him nodding respectfully, no laughter.
> "I am afraid of… failing exam." — silent respect.

**Panel 8** — Mansoor stands tall like a hero, chest puffed, smiling confidently before his big reveal.
> "I am afraid of… butterfly."

**Panel 9** — The class silent for a beat, then erupting into laughter; Khalil pointing at Mansoor incredulously.
> "Butterfly??? You fight boys but afraid of butterfly?"

**Panel 10** — Mansoor crosses his arms defensively, insisting.
> "They fly… without warning! They are dangerous."

**Panel 11** — Safiya, smiling, raises her own hand for her turn; students stare at her in surprise.
> "My turn. I am afraid of… grasshoppers." — "You eat it!"

**Panel 12** — John jumps onto a table/desk in mock panic as the goat Malood bursts into the classroom straight toward him; the whole class in stitches, Safiya writing on the board behind.
> "I am afraid of goats!" — Mr. John is afraid of Malood.

### Scene Three — Thuraya's Secret

**Panel 1** — Safiya turns to Thuraya, who answers quickly, arms crossed, a beat too fast to be convincing.
> "And you?" — "I am not afraid of anything. Really."

**Panel 2** — Close on Thuraya's eyes flicking toward the classroom window; John notices and follows her gaze.
> John follows her eyes to the window.

**Panel 3** — POV through the window: a small lizard sitting quietly on the outside wall in the sun.
> A small lizard, sitting quietly on the wall.

**Panel 4** — John grinning with a mischievous, dangerous smile, already turning to head outside.
> "Ohhh… Interesting…"

**Panel 5** — John walks back into the classroom holding a small closed box, students watching in rapt silence like an audience.
> "Thuraya, I have something for you."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya stepping backward, hand raised, alarmed, as John opens the box and the lizard leaps out.
> "No." — The lizard jumps out.

**Panel 7** — Thuraya mid-scream, hands flailing, complete loss of composure; wide-eyed students frozen in shock at seeing her this way.
> A real scream — Ms. Thuraya was not, after all, indestructible.

**Panel 8** — Full chaos: Thuraya running, girls screaming, Mansoor climbing on top of a desk, the lizard darting across the floor, Malood the goat snapping at it.
> The class became a war zone. "JOHN!"

**Panel 9** — John doubled over laughing, breathless, pointing at Thuraya across the chaos.
> "I am afraid of nothing — but you are afraid of lizards!"

**Panel 10** — Order restored; Safiya at the board writing a new line while Thuraya stands with arms crossed, glaring.
> Ms. Thuraya is afraid of lizards. — "Delete it."

### Scene Four — The Big Question

**Panel 1** — Ali raises his hand tentatively amid a now-calm classroom.
> "Teacher… what about Shayb Khalaf?"

**Panel 2** — The room goes quiet; Mansoor looks around uneasily.
> "What is he afraid of?"

**Panel 3** — Wide shot: every child's head turned in unison toward the closed classroom door, uneasy stillness, chalk dust drifting in a shaft of light.
> Every head turned, simultaneously, toward the closed door.

**Panel 4** — John swallows nervously; Safiya whispers beside him.
> "Good question…"

**Panel 5** — Close on Thuraya's face, a slow, knowing smile, deciding whether to answer.
> Thuraya smiled slowly, deciding whether to share the answer.

**Panel 6** — Thuraya speaking evenly to the class, the fan turning slowly behind her.
> "No one knows."

**Panel 7** — Exterior establishing shot: the village path outside, distant figure, faint sound of laughter carrying on the wind.
> Outside, somewhere far away… someone was laughing.

### Scene Five — Saeed's Quiet Watching

**Panel 1** — Children pouring out of the classroom for break: Mansoor sprinting first, Khalil chasing him, Noura and Marwa walking together laughing.
> The class went out for break.

**Panel 2** — Saeed alone, standing quietly by the window, looking out at the empty wall where the lizard had been.
> Saeed stayed, looking out at the empty wall.

**Panel 3** — Thuraya approaches and stands beside him gently.
> "Saeed, and you? What are you afraid of?"

**Panel 4** — Saeed pauses, thoughtful, eyes lowered in concentration, choosing his words carefully.
> Saeed did not answer immediately.

**Panel 5** — Saeed looks up at Thuraya, speaking carefully in his small English.
> "I am afraid of… not seeing."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya tilts her head, gently curious.
> "Not seeing what?"

**Panel 7** — Saeed, searching for words, finally settles on a simple answer, looking directly at her.
> "What is true."

**Panel 8** — Thuraya and Saeed standing together in the empty classroom in a long quiet silence, the fan turning, faint smell of cooking drifting from outside.
> Thuraya stood with him, without speaking, for a long moment.

**Panel 9** — Thuraya crouches slightly to his level, speaking softly; Saeed nods.
> "That is a very brave fear, Saeed."

**Panel 10** — Saeed turns and runs off after the other children, out into the sunlight.
> Saeed nodded, and ran out after the others.

### Scene Six — The Small Closing of the Door

**Panel 1** — Thuraya alone in the empty classroom, sitting at the teacher's desk, small private notebook open before her.
> The classroom was empty.

**Panel 2** — Close on her hand writing Arabic script in the notebook, pen in hand, contemplative expression.
> «اليوم تعلّموا كلمة الخوف. وأنا تعلّمت أن الصغير سعيد… يعرف أكثر مني.»

**Panel 3** — Thuraya closes the notebook gently, resting her hand on its cover.
> Today they learned the word "fear." I learned that Saeed knows more than I do.

**Panel 4** — Thuraya sitting still for a moment, listening to the tired ceiling fan turning above her.
> She sat for a minute, listening to the fan.

**Panel 5** — Thuraya stepping outside into bright daylight, joining John and Safiya under the large palm tree, with children playing, running, and sharing dates in the background.
> She went outside, into the bright village afternoon.

**Panel 6** — Wide shot of joyful village children at lunchtime — running, kicking stones, sharing dates — under palm trees, sunlight streaming through fronds.
> Children existing in the unrepeatable joy of being nine years old.

**Panel 7** — Distant shot down the village path: Shayb Khalaf's clay-house door slowly swinging shut.
> Far away, Shayb Khalaf's door closed slowly.

**Panel 8** — Final wide village panorama, calm and sunlit, no sense of unease this time.
> For once, no one wondered what the closing of that door might mean.


---


## Palm Thirty-Two — The Only Thing Shayb Khalaf Was Afraid Of

### Scene 1 — Tap, Tap, Tap

**Panel 1** — Grey-blue pre-dawn interior of a simple Samail mud-brick house; John lies half-asleep in bed, eyes just opening, listening to distant village sounds outside the window.
> "The day was beginning correctly."

**Panel 2** — Wide shot of the sleepy village at dawn: a rooster crowing on a wall, smoke rising from a distant cooking fire, dust lanes still empty.
> A rooster screamed like it was late for work.

**Panel 3** — Inside the bedroom, Sophia/Safiya sits on the edge of the bed carefully tying her headscarf, mid-motion, calm patience on her face.
> "I feel like I'm dressing for a documentary."

**Panel 4** — John, still rubbing his eyes, sitting up in bed, wry half-smile at Safiya.
> "At least documentaries have fans. We have… heat."

**Panel 5** — Close on the bedroom door: a tiny goat hoof tapping against the wood, door slightly ajar.
> Tap. Tap. Tap.

**Panel 6** — Safiya frozen mid-motion, wide eyes turned toward the door in mock dread.
> "Please tell me that is not… him."

**Panel 7** — The door swings open; Malood the baby goat struts in confidently and marches straight to John's shoe, head lowered to the laces.
> Malood walked in like he owned the entire house.

**Panel 8** — John leaning down, tugging his shoe from Malood's mouth, both pulling in a tug-of-war; the shoelace mid-snap between them.
> A silent war — the shoelace snapped.

**Panel 9** — Exterior yard: John standing outside holding the broken lace like a limp trophy; Thuraya at the gate with chalk and notebook, calling out briskly, morning light now fuller.
> "Good morning! Hurry, or the students will arrive before you."

### Scene 2 — The Schoolyard and the Workers

**Panel 1** — Establishing shot of the simple 1973 village school: chalk-dusted yard, plain low buildings, an old hand bell, morning light.
> A school still being built, brick by brick.

**Panel 2** — Students streaming into the yard in small groups, barefoot or in worn sandals, notebooks tucked under arms, chattering.
> Students arrived carrying small notebooks — and curiosity.

**Panel 3** — Mansoor sprinting across the yard toward John, nearly colliding with him, then snapping into an exaggerated salute.
> "HAAAAI!"

**Panel 4** — Group shot of the other children behind Mansoor: Ali calm, Ahmed sitting upright, Khalil already laughing, the girls (Matla, Noura, Rahma, Marwa, Salimah) watching curiously.
> The children who had never seen a foreign teacher before.

**Panel 5** — Saeed half-hidden at the edge of the yard near the wall, watching quietly, apart from the group.
> Saeed stood at the far side — a quiet shadow.

**Panel 6** — Thuraya clapping once to gather the class, chalk in hand, addressing the lined-up students.
> "Okay! Everyone, listen. Today we practice morning assembly."

**Panel 7** — John at the chalkboard writing large block words: LINE UP / STAND STILL / GOOD MORNING / EXERCISES / ANTHEM.
> John wrote large words on a small board.

**Panel 8** — Corner of the schoolyard: a small group of workmen arriving with measuring tools, a rolled paper, a hammer, looking over the old school building.
> "Workers. The school will be rebuilt. This is the new time."

**Panel 9** — Wide shot of the students lined up singing the national anthem, imperfect but full voices; John standing still among them, listening, moved.
> John stood quietly — feeling like he was standing inside history.

### Scene 3 — The Fear Lesson and the Match

**Panel 1** — Close on John's face indoors, a spark of mischievous idea crossing his expression as he thinks of Shayb Khalaf.
> Shayb Khalaf. The village's "wizard."

**Panel 2** — Classroom interior: John at the chalkboard writing "I AM AFRAID OF… / HE IS AFRAID OF… / HE IS NOT AFRAID OF…" while Safiya and Thuraya watch from the side.
> "Good topic. Everyone has fears."

**Panel 3** — Noura standing at her desk, confident posture, answering John's question.
> "I am afraid of… bee."

**Panel 4** — Mansoor leaping up with his arm shooting into the air like it's on fire, eager grin.
> Mansoor raised his hand like his arm was on fire.

**Panel 5** — Mansoor standing proudly, announcing his answer; classroom around him mid-laugh, Khalil toppling sideways off his seat.
> "I am afraid of… butterfly."

**Panel 6** — Wide classroom shot: Khalil sprawled sideways laughing, Ahmed fighting to stay serious, Ali's mouth twitching, Safiya wiping tears of laughter.
> "That is the cutest fear I have ever heard."

**Panel 7** — John leaning forward toward the class, voice lowered, storyteller posture; half the students visibly gasping, some glancing nervously at the door.
> "Now… let's talk about Shayb Khalaf."

**Panel 8** — Close two-shot: Ali and Khalil whispering their answers, faces uneasy; Thuraya watching John closely, suspicious; Safiya narrowing her eyes at him.
> "He is… the fear." / Safiya muttered, "Liar."

### Scene 4 — Dukkan Al-Hallah and the Old Man's Secret

**Panel 1** — Exterior/interior of Dukkan Al-Hallah, a cluttered small village shop: sacks of rice, crates of dates, spices, brass coffee pots stacked in a corner; John walking in with purpose, Safiya and Thuraya following, suspicious.
> John walked to Dukkan Al-Hallah — determined to collect information.

**Panel 2** — Sadu behind the counter, mid-argument with a boy trying to get something for free, then looking up and grinning as John approaches.
> "Jooohen! You come! You want what?"

**Panel 3** — John leaning on the counter, low and direct, Sadu's eyes lighting up with interest.
> "Sadu. I need village information."

**Panel 4** — An elderly man (Shayb Zahran) entering slowly, small bag in hand, white beard, bright mischievous eyes; Sadu leaning in to whisper to John.
> "That is Shayb Zahran."

**Panel 5** — John turning politely to greet Zahran; Zahran smiling slowly back at him.
> "Hello, teacher." / "You speak English."

**Panel 6** — Close on Zahran, smile widening knowingly as John leans in to ask his question.
> "You want ask about Khalaf."

**Panel 7** — Safiya and Thuraya's heads snapping toward John in alarm, both reacting sharply behind him.
> "John!" / "Mr. John…"

**Panel 8** — Zahran leaning in conspiratorially, one finger raised like a lecturer, lowering his voice; Safiya and Thuraya leaning in too, breath held.
> "People think he fear nothing." … "Wrong."

**Panel 9** — Tight close-up on Zahran's face, wicked grin, whispering the secret; the shop around him rendered slightly colder/dimmer.
> "His wife." — the name: منوّه.

**Panel 10** — John straightening up, eyes shining with mischievous excitement; Thuraya gripping his sleeve to stop him, Safiya shaking her head.
> "No. Absolutely no." / "I'm just… practicing English."

### Scene 5 — The Clay House

**Panel 1** — Golden late-afternoon light; John walking alone down a dirt path toward a modest clay house, Safiya and Thuraya trailing at a distance, whispering to each other; Malood trotting along behind everyone.
> "He will do it." "He will definitely do it." "He will die."

**Panel 2** — John standing at the closed wooden door of Khalaf's house, hand raised, heart visibly pounding, resolve on his face.
> His heart beat fast.

**Panel 3** — John pausing, eyes closed briefly, mouthing words to himself for courage.
> "He is not afraid of… anything."

**Panel 4** — The door swinging open dramatically under John's push, him stepping forward into the doorway mid-shout.
> John stepped in and shouted — "Manno—!"

**Panel 5** — John frozen in the doorway, voice cut off, face falling as he registers the empty silent room.
> His voice died in his throat.

**Panel 6** — Interior of the house: a walking stick lying flat on the earthen floor instead of propped against the wall; a brass dallah tipped on its side nearby with dark coffee spilled and spreading across the floor.
> The dallah was tipped over — coffee spread like a stain.

**Panel 7** — Safiya appearing in the doorway behind John, her playful expression gone, replaced by real concern; Thuraya stepping past her slowly into the room, scanning it.
> "John…" / Thuraya whispered, "No…"

**Panel 8** — Malood nosing nervously at the doorway, ears back, uncertain; wind stirring palm fronds visible through a window/opening outside.
> Malood made a small nervous sound.

### Scene 6 — A Village Suddenly Different

**Panel 1** — Distant child figure on the village road, calling out; John, Safiya, and Thuraya still inside/near the house, heads turning toward the sound.
> A child's voice called: "Teacher John!"

**Panel 2** — Interior shot again: the spilled coffee still pooled, the stick still on the floor, untouched — nothing has changed despite the outside call.
> The coffee stayed spilled. The stick stayed on the floor.

**Panel 3** — John backing slowly out of the doorway, expression tense and thoughtful, mind clearly racing.
> Safiya whispered, "Where is he?"

**Panel 4** — Close on Thuraya's face staring fixed at the empty doorway, her usual confident/playful demeanor replaced by unsettled doubt.
> Thuraya stared at the doorway like the village itself had changed shape.

**Panel 5** — John looking between the dark coffee stain and Thuraya's face, low and serious.
> "Tomorrow… we must find out."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya, quiet and subdued, giving a small nod/answer.
> "Yes."

**Panel 7** — Wide shot: the three of them (John, Safiya, Thuraya) walking back into the village at dusk, oil lamps glowing in windows, smoke rising from kitchens, Malood trailing behind chewing a leaf but glancing back uneasily.
> The village had suddenly become a place that held a question they could not yet answer.


---


## Palm Thirty-Three — A Son from Bahrain — The Truth Behind the Story

### Scene 1 — A Morning That Felt Different

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot of Samail village at dawn: swept yards, careful smoke rising from cooking fires, goats and Malood the dog moving unusually slowly, palm trees still.
> "The village had decided, by unspoken agreement, to be ready for something."

**Panel 2** — John stands in the doorway of the small house, holding his notebook, looking out at the quiet village with a curious expression.
> "Today feels like… something is coming."

**Panel 3** — Safiya, tying her headscarf just inside the doorway, smiles at John over her shoulder.
> "In Oman, something is always coming. Usually heat."

**Panel 4** — Thuraya walks up the path toward them carrying her bag, her face thoughtful rather than playful.
> "Not heat today. News."

**Panel 5** — The three of them (John, Safiya, Thuraya) walking together toward the school, Malood trotting behind like a loyal cloud.
> "Good news or village-news?" "Both."

**Panel 6** — Wide shot of the schoolyard: the students sitting silently under the big palm tree instead of running and shouting — Ali, Ahmed, and Mansoor all unusually still.
> "Something was definitely wrong."

**Panel 7** — Thuraya claps her hands at the quiet children, puzzled; Khalil answers softly from the group.
> "Why are you sitting like this?" "We are waiting."

**Panel 8** — Saeed steps forward from the back of the group, uncharacteristically bold, facing the teachers.
> "For Obaid," he said.

**Panel 9** — Close on Thuraya's face softening as she explains to a frowning John and a gasping Safiya, the children listening.
> "Obaid is Shayb Khalaf's son… Now he is coming."

### Scene 2 — The Return

**Panel 1** — Dusty road at the edge of the village, a distant car engine sound implied by dust rising; the schoolchildren's heads all turning toward the noise.
> "The sound came first. A car."

**Panel 2** — The children rising to their feet together, all facing the road, tense with anticipation.
> "The students stood up. Like one body."

**Panel 3** — A man (Obaid) walking fast up the road, carrying two bags, wearing clean city clothes and a neatly trimmed beard, his eyes scanning the houses.
> "His eyes searching… the same searching he had done as a child."

**Panel 4** — A child in the crowd points and shouts toward the clay house.
> "Baba!" someone shouted.

**Panel 5** — Shayb Khalaf emerging slowly from his clay house doorway, leaning on his stick, the village watching.
> Shayb Khalaf came out. Slowly. His stick in his hand.

**Panel 6** — Close on Khalaf's face as he stops and looks down the road; his walking stick falling from his open hand to the dust.
> He looked. The stick fell.

**Panel 7** — Obaid breaking into a run toward his father, arms already open, dust kicking up behind him.
> Then Obaid ran. He didn't walk. He didn't think.

**Panel 8** — Obaid embracing Khalaf tightly, the old man nearly staggering from the force of it; Obaid's face pressed to his father's shoulder.
> "Baba," Obaid said, his voice breaking. "I am back."

**Panel 9** — Khalaf silently holding his son, eyes closed, stick lying forgotten in the dirt beside them; villagers gathering around at a respectful distance.
> He just held him. And held him. And held him.

**Panel 10** — Reaction shots: women dabbing their eyes with headscarves, Safiya's eyes glistening, John swallowing hard, Thuraya watching with a soft, knowing expression.
> "He waited many years."

### Scene 3 — Gifts and Stories

**Panel 1** — Obaid and Shayb Khalaf walking together into the schoolyard, children running up eagerly to greet them.
> Later, Obaid came to the school with his father.

**Panel 2** — Obaid crouching to hand small simple gifts to each child in turn, each child beaming.
> "For each child, a smile. For each smile, a memory."

**Panel 3** — John shaking Obaid's hand warmly in front of the group.
> "Welcome back." "Thank you, teacher."

**Panel 4** — Safiya asking a question, Obaid's eyes lighting up as he answers, small cloth pouches visible in his hands.
> "What do you do in Bahrain?" "I work with herbs. Medicine. Old knowledge."

**Panel 5** — Close on Obaid's hands holding out several small labeled herb bags to the teachers and children.
> "This is for stomach." "This for headache." "This for sleep."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya nodding thoughtfully as she watches him.
> "Your grandfather used to do this."

**Panel 7** — Obaid's smile fading into sadness mid-sentence, his gaze dropping.
> Obaid smiled sadly. "Yes. And my mother…" He stopped.

**Panel 8** — A held silence: Shayb Khalaf looking away toward the horizon, the children and teachers quiet, the unspoken weight of an absent mother hanging over the group.
> No one spoke. Shayb Khalaf looked away.

### Scene 4 — The Truth at Sadu's Shop

**Panel 1** — Interior of Dukkan Al-Hallah, John entering to find Sadu doubled over laughing on a bench.
> He found Sadu laughing. Too much laughing.

**Panel 2** — John standing puzzled in the doorway; Sadu nearly toppling off the bench, pointing at him.
> "What?" "You! You and 'Manooh'!"

**Panel 3** — Close on John's face freezing mid-expression.
> John froze. "What?"

**Panel 4** — Sadu wiping tears of laughter from his eyes, explaining with wide gestures.
> "Zahran. He is crazy. Manooh is HIS wife. Not Khalaf's!"

**Panel 5** — John's mouth hanging open in disbelief, Sadu still chuckling beside him.
> "Zahran mixes names. He forgets. He tells stories. Everyone knows."

**Panel 6** — John leaning in, speaking low, processing the revelation.
> "So… Shayb Khalaf was never afraid of Manooh?"

**Panel 7** — Sadu laughing even harder, slapping his knee.
> "No. He is afraid of nothing."

**Panel 8** — John standing still, eyes closed slowly, the full absurdity of his detective work sinking in.
> John closed his eyes. Slowly. Very slowly.

### Scene 5 — The Big Embarrassment

**Panel 1** — Evening light; John walking up to Shayb Khalaf's house, straightening his posture and clearing his throat before the doorway.
> He stood straight. He cleared his throat.

**Panel 2** — Khalaf standing in his doorway, stick in hand, regarding John.
> "You were coming yesterday?" "Yes. I wanted to say something."

**Panel 3** — John taking a visible deep breath, starting to speak, Khalaf leaning on his stick waiting.
> "I wanted to say… Man—" He stopped.

**Panel 4** — Beat panel: John waiting nervously, nothing happening, no reaction from Khalaf — just a raised eyebrow.
> Khalaf raised an eyebrow. "Say what?"

**Panel 5** — John trying again, mouth forming the syllable, Khalaf's stick arm already drawing back out of frame.
> John tried again. "I wanted to say… M—"

**Panel 6** — Action panel: the stick connecting sharply with John's back, motion lines, John's shocked expression.
> WHACK. The stick hit John's back.

**Panel 7** — John jumping, clutching his back; Khalaf glowering at him.
> "OW!" Khalaf growled. "You think I am stupid?"

**Panel 8** — John red-faced, staring down at the ground; Khalaf standing calm and unbothered beside him.
> "Zahran is crazy." John looked at the ground.

### Scene 6 — The Legendary Chase — and the Light in the Window

**Panel 1** — Sadu popping out from behind the house, laughing loudly, pointing at John.
> "I told you! I told you!"

**Panel 2** — John's eyes darting to the stick still in Khalaf's hand, alarmed realization on his face.
> John saw the stick in Khalaf's hand. Bad idea.

**Panel 3** — John grabbing Sadu by the arm and bolting away from the house.
> He grabbed Sadu and ran.

**Panel 4** — Khalaf charging after them, stick raised, shouting, surprisingly spry for his age.
> "HEY!" Khalaf shouted and ran after them.

**Panel 5** — Malood bounding along after the group, tail wagging, treating it as a game.
> Malood saw running and decided this was a game.

**Panel 6** — The schoolchildren streaming in to join the chase, laughing and shouting.
> The children saw running and joined.

**Panel 7** — Wide comic chase-line shot down the village lane: Sadu in front, John, then Khalaf, then Malood, then the children, dust flying, villagers watching from doorways.
> Someone shouted, "It's a festival!"

**Panel 8** — Night, interior of the small house; John sitting and rubbing his sore back while Safiya looks at him with a knowing half-smile.
> "So… what did we learn today?"

**Panel 9** — John, thoughtful, giving his answer; Thuraya prompting him further.
> "Never trust village gossip." "And?"

**Panel 10** — Final quiet exterior shot: Shayb Khalaf's clay house glowing warmly with lamplight in the dark, the sleeping village silhouetted around it.
> "Some people wait years for love." The village slept peacefully.


---


## Palm Thirty-Four — Then and Now — A Trip to Jibreen Castle

### Scene 1 — A Long Dusty Road and a New Guide

**Panel 1** — A dusty gravel road through palm farms and wadis in 1970s interior Oman; Uncle Nasser's pickup truck moves slowly, kicking up dust, wide desert-and-palm landscape around it.
> "The road to Jibreen Castle was long, dusty, and full of quiet excitement."

**Panel 2** — Inside/on the pickup: John sits up front gazing at the horizon; Safiya beside him with a camera on her lap; Thuraya behind them mid-explanation to the children; Obaid stands in the truck bed holding the side rail.
> A new guide rides along — Obaid, once the returned son, now the guide.

**Panel 3** — Close on Obaid in the truck bed, one hand gripping the side, the other pointing out at the passing land.
> "There. That road did not exist before."

**Panel 4** — John turns back toward Obaid, curious expression.
> "So… how did people travel?"

**Panel 5** — Obaid smiling, gesturing toward the land as the truck rolls on.
> "On animals. On feet. Slowly. Very slowly."

**Panel 6** — Mansoor in the truck bed with wide eyes; Obaid nodding beside him.
> "Even to Nizwa?" — "Especially to Nizwa."

**Panel 7** — Group shot of the children looking at each other, thoughtful, trying to picture a world with no roads.
> The children imagine a world without roads.

**Panel 8** — Safiya leaning close to Thuraya, speaking quietly, camera still on her lap.
> "It's strange. We are living inside a story."

**Panel 9** — Thuraya smiling back at Safiya as the truck continues down the road.
> "Yes. And today, we will teach them how to tell it."

### Scene 2 — The Castle Appears

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot: Jibreen Castle rising suddenly into view as the road curves and the palms thin out, dream-like and imposing against the desert sky.
> Jibreen Castle appeared like something from a dream.

**Panel 2** — Full view of the castle: three storeys of golden stonework, four corner towers, carved wooden window-frames weathered by centuries of sun.
> Tall. Strong. Quiet. Its walls looked like they were holding secrets.

**Panel 3** — Ali standing very still at the castle entrance, looking up in awe; Obaid beside him.
> "It looks like a king's house." — "It was."

**Panel 4** — The group entering the castle's dim stone interior, footsteps echoing, children close together.
> They entered slowly. Their footsteps echoed.

**Panel 5** — Obaid speaking calmly to the gathered children inside a large stone hall.
> "Long time ago, people lived here. They studied here. They fought here. They planned here."

**Panel 6** — Close-up of John's notebook page as he writes, pen in hand.
> lived — studied — fought — planned

**Panel 7** — Safiya facing the children, prompting them; the class repeating together, mouths open in unison.
> "Repeat after me: They lived here." — "They lived here."

**Panel 8** — Obaid gesturing to an old oil lamp niche in the wall; Thuraya nearby adding a comment about the falaj water channel outside.
> "They used oil lamps, not electricity." / "And water… they used falaj."

**Panel 9** — John holding up a small handwritten board to the class, pointing at Ali.
> "In the past, they used to… Now, we…" — "Ali. In the past, they used to…?"

**Panel 10** — Ali thinking hard, then answering; John smiling and nodding approval.
> "They used to… walk… long." — "Good! They used to walk long distances."

**Panel 11** — Ahmed speaking up eagerly; Safiya clapping in encouragement.
> "Now, we… use car." — "Excellent!"

**Panel 12** — Mansoor raising his hand with a grin; the other children laughing; Thuraya smiling and nodding.
> "In the past, they used to… no fan." — "True. No fan. Only patience."

### Scene 3 — From Room to Room

**Panel 1** — The group walking carefully through a stone corridor, children stepping lightly, aware the floors are old.
> They walked from room to room, carefully.

**Panel 2** — Obaid leading with quiet authority through a doorway; children clustered informally behind him, asking questions.
> Obaid moved between the rooms with quiet authority.

**Panel 3** — Obaid pointing up at a painted wooden ceiling, then to a small window, then toward a hidden passage in the wall.
> Obaid showed them the ceilings, the small windows, the secret passages.

**Panel 4** — Obaid standing in a doorway, addressing the children solemnly.
> "In the past, people protected their families here. Now, we protect history."

**Panel 5** — John standing still, a heavy, moved expression on his face, looking toward Thuraya.
> "Your country has strong roots."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya, calm and quiet, meeting John's gaze in the stone hallway.
> "And now… it is growing."

### Scene 4 — Ali's Drawing

**Panel 1** — The group sitting in the shade beside the castle's outer wall; Safiya unpacking colored pencils and paper for the children.
> They sat in the shade near the castle wall.

**Panel 2** — Safiya holding up paper and colors to the curious children.
> "Now, you draw." — "Draw?" — "Draw the castle. Or what you feel."

**Panel 3** — Thuraya adding instruction; John writing a large prompt on big paper for all to see.
> "And write one sentence." / In the past… Now… In the future, I will…

**Panel 4** — Ali sitting apart, very quiet and focused, hand moving slowly and carefully over his page.
> Ali sat very quietly. Very focused.

**Panel 5** — John walking past, stopping, leaning down silently to watch Ali draw.
> He leaned down. Did not speak. Just watched.

**Panel 6** — Close-up on Ali's drawing: the castle, the land, and a large Omani flag (red field, white and green panels, khanjar emblem) rising above it, confident lines and strong colors.
> Ali was drawing the castle, the land, and a big flag above it.

**Panel 7** — Safiya freezing as she looks over Ali's shoulder, whispering to Thuraya, who leans in and smiles in surprise.
> "Thuraya…" — "He has talent."

**Panel 8** — Close-up of Ali's handwriting beneath the drawing.
> In the past, Oman was strong. Now, Oman is growing. In the future, I will help my country.

**Panel 9** — Safiya lifting the drawing to show John; he studies it for a long moment, then speaks; Ali looks down, shy.
> "Ali… you are an artist."

**Panel 10** — Comic beat: Mansoor proudly holding up his own drawing — a castle with five suns and two goats — while a goat, Malood, wanders over and tries to eat the corner of the paper.
> Mansoor's castle had five suns and two goats — and Malood tried to eat it.

### Scene 5 — In the Future, I Will…

**Panel 1** — John standing before the seated children near the castle wall, calling the group to attention.
> "Okay. Let's speak." — He points to Noura.

**Panel 2** — Noura answering shyly; John nodding approval.
> "In the past, they used to… live in castle." — "Good."

**Panel 3** — Khalil speaking up next, confident.
> "Now, we have… school."

**Panel 4** — Ahmed answering with a proud smile.
> "In the future, I will… be teacher."

**Panel 5** — Saeed, quieter, speaking from the back of the group.
> "In the future, I will… learn more."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya's face softening as she looks at Saeed, a quiet, proud teacher's smile.
> Thuraya looked at him. And smiled.

### Scene 6 — The Flag Receding

**Panel 1** — Wide shot: the pickup driving away from Jibreen Castle in low, golden late-afternoon light, the honey-colored stone walls glowing behind it.
> On the way back, the sun was lower.

**Panel 2** — The castle shrinking into the distance behind the truck, framed like a fading memory.
> The castle stayed behind them like a memory.

**Panel 3** — Close-up of Safiya in the truck, holding Ali's drawing carefully on her lap, the colors still wet, the paper slightly curled.
> Safiya held Ali's drawing on her lap.

**Panel 4** — Safiya speaking softly to John beside her, looking down at the drawing.
> "Sometimes… a lesson is not in the book."

**Panel 5** — John nodding, glancing at the drawing too.
> "Sometimes, it's in a child."

**Panel 6** — Thuraya in the front seat, looking ahead at the long road home.
> "And sometimes," she said, "it is in a country."

**Panel 7** — Final wide shot: the pickup moving down the dusty road into the fading light, small figures inside, the landscape opening ahead.
> The pickup moved forward. And history moved with it.


---


## Palm Thirty-Five — The Month When Everything Changed — Ramadan in Samail

### Scene 1 — The First Adhan of the Month

**Panel 1** — Pre-dawn Samail: dark mountains, silhouetted palm trees, the village mosque minaret glowing faintly, sound-wave lines drifting out over the rooftops. No sun yet.
> "It was not the sun that woke Samail. It was the adhan."

**Panel 2** — Inside John and Safiya's bedroom, dim blue pre-dawn light. John's eyes just opening, confused, propped on one elbow. Safiya already sitting upright on the edge of the bed, still, listening.
> "What… is that?"

**Panel 3** — Close on Safiya's face by the window, calm and moved, head tilted toward the sound.
> "It's beautiful. It sounds like the sky is speaking."

**Panel 4** — Wide shot of the village lanes at dawn, empty and hushed, no movement, doors closed.
> Quieter. Kinder. Like it was holding its breath.

**Panel 5** — Thuraya walking alone past John and Safiya's house, light steps, carrying a small bag of dates and bread, pausing to look up at the sky.
> She stopped, looked at the sky, and whispered a short prayer.

**Panel 6** — Empty schoolyard, swings and benches with no children, dust settling.
> That day, no children came to school. No noise. No running.

**Panel 7** — Sadoo's shop, shutters down, a hand-lettered wooden sign nailed to the door.
> "Closed for Ramadan."

**Panel 8** — Mansoor walking slowly down the lane, tired but proud expression, addressing Malood (a donkey/goat) beside him who is reaching for a leaf.
> "I am fasting. You… you don't understand religion."

**Panel 9** — Close on Malood chewing the leaf, unbothered, ignoring the lecture entirely.
> Malood ate the leaf.

---

### Scene 2 — The Travelling Food

**Panel 1** — Late afternoon rooftop view over Samail, thin trails of cooking smoke rising from many houses at once, blending into a shared haze above the village.
> The village started to smell different — onions, bread, spices, meat, all at once.

**Panel 2** — Safiya standing at her window, eyes closed slightly, breathing in the mixed aromas drifting past.
> A layered scent she had never encountered before this exact afternoon.

**Panel 3** — Village lane: women in groups carrying covered plates and trays from door to door, laughing quietly together.
> Plates moved from door to door. Women walked in groups, laughing quietly.

**Panel 4** — Safiya still at the window, pointing out at the passing food-carriers, half amused.
> "The food… it's travelling."

**Panel 5** — John beside her, grinning at the sight below.
> "Like a food parade."

**Panel 6** — Wide shot: sun still high and bright over the village, streets otherwise still, no one eating despite all the food in motion.
> But the sun was still high. And nobody was eating.

**Panel 7** — John checking his wristwatch, puzzled expression, looking to Thuraya who has just arrived at the door.
> "So… they don't eat. They don't drink. All day?"

**Panel 8** — Thuraya standing in the doorway, nodding simply.
> "From dawn to sunset."

**Panel 9** — Safiya blinking in disbelief, hand half-raised.
> "Even water?" — "Yes."

**Panel 10** — John sitting down slowly onto a cushion, taking this in.
> "That is… serious."

**Panel 11** — Safiya and John sitting quietly together, a reflective, thoughtful stillness between them, as if remembering old conversations.
> They had used the word understand. Now something had replaced it.

**Panel 12** — Safiya turning to John with sudden resolve, eyes bright.
> "Let's try." — "Try what?" — "Fasting. Come on. One day."

---

### Scene 3 — The Longest Day

**Panel 1** — Mid-morning, John standing in the small house, hand dramatically on his throat, exaggerated suffering.
> "I think… my mouth is becoming a desert."

**Panel 2** — Safiya glancing at him flatly, arms crossed, unimpressed.
> "Stop it."

**Panel 3** — Noon: John flat on the floor, arm flung over his eyes, defeated.
> "I can hear… water calling me."

**Panel 4** — Safiya sitting beside him on the floor, smirking.
> "Drama queen."

**Panel 5** — Two in the afternoon: John attempting to stand, wobbling, one hand out, failing back down; a ceiling fan spinning above him.
> "I see… light." — "That's the fan."

**Panel 6** — Malood the donkey/goat stepping calmly over John's outstretched leg on the floor, unfazed.
> Malood came, looked at him, and stepped over his leg.

**Panel 7** — Four in the afternoon: John collapsed on the floor again, Safiya cross-legged beside him, mock-lecturing.
> "You wanted to understand the culture. Congratulations."

**Panel 8** — The door opening: Thuraya standing there with a large tray held in both hands, smiling.
> Knock knock. "I brought you iftar."

**Panel 9** — John trying weakly to sit up, gesturing at himself and Safiya.
> "But… we are not fasting."

**Panel 10** — Thuraya setting the tray down on the floor between them, tray loaded with dates, soup, bread, samosas, rice.
> "I know. But you should taste Ramadan."

**Panel 11** — Safiya looking back and forth between the food, Thuraya, and John, then bursting into laughter.
> "Look at us. Not fasting. And dying."

**Panel 12** — Thuraya laughing with them, then leaning in with a teasing, knowing smile, speaking in Arabic.
> "Poor you. One day only, and you almost die." — «صيام بدون صلاة؟» (Fasting without prayer?)

---

### Scene 4 — The Disappearing Afternoons

**Panel 1** — Village lane, mid-afternoon light; John and Safiya quietly slipping out of their house and walking off together, away from school and shop.
> Every day around three, the teachers would disappear.

**Panel 2** — Wide shot showing they bypass the school and the falaj, heading down an unfamiliar side lane.
> They would not go to the school. They would not go to the shop.

**Panel 3** — The same two returning quietly an hour or two later, calm, unremarkable expressions, slipping back into their house.
> They came back quietly.

**Panel 4** — A cluster of children — Mansoor, Khalil, Noura — huddled at a corner, watching the house, whispering to each other.
> "They go to the teacher's house." "Every day." "Why?"

**Panel 5** — The children starting to creep down the lane, trying to follow the trail.
> They tried to follow them.

**Panel 6** — Shayb Khalaf stepping into the road ahead of them, tall and immovable, gripping his walking stick like a barrier.
> But Shayb Khalaf appeared. Like a wall.

**Panel 7** — Close on Khalaf's stern face, stick raised slightly, addressing the children directly.
> "Any one follow them, this bamboo talks."

**Panel 8** — Khalaf turning his gaze pointedly to Thuraya standing nearby; Thuraya crossing her arms, unbothered.
> "First you, Thraywa." — "You like drama."

**Panel 9** — The children turning back down the lane, disappointed, glancing over their shoulders at Khalaf still standing firm.
> He did not move. They turned back.

---

### Scene 5 — A Different Adhan

**Panel 1** — Evening, half of Ramadan gone; the adhan sounding over the village, rendered visually stronger and closer than before (denser sound-wave lines over the rooftops).
> One evening, the adhan was different. Stronger. Closer.

**Panel 2** — John and Safiya walking together with Thuraya slightly ahead of them, like a guide, along the village lane at dusk.
> Not to their house. Not to the teacher's house.

**Panel 3** — Wide shot of the mosque ahead, the three figures walking steadily toward its doorway.
> To the mosque.

**Panel 4** — Villagers along the lane pausing mid-task, turning to watch the three walk past, whispering to one another.
> People stopped. Looked. Whispered.

**Panel 5** — Close on the three sets of feet walking calmly, purposefully, toward the mosque steps — visual focus on the "direction of travel."
> Their feet quietly carried the answer the village had been asking in silence.

**Panel 6** — That night: Shayb Khalaf's courtyard transformed — a long table set outdoors, lit by lanterns, piled with rice, meat, bread, dates, tea; villagers gathering around.
> That night, Shayb Khalaf opened his house. He made a big table. For everyone.

**Panel 7** — Khalaf standing at the head of the table, addressing the gathered crowd simply.
> "Today… we celebrate."

**Panel 8** — John standing among the seated villagers, visibly nervous but steady, all eyes on him.
> "I came to Oman to teach English. But Oman taught me something bigger."

**Panel 9** — Safiya standing beside John, taking his hand, facing the crowd with him.
> "We did not come only for work. We came because we were… searching."

**Panel 10** — John turning to look directly at Thuraya in the crowd as he speaks.
> "At your people. Your life. Your faith. Today… we say it clearly."

**Panel 11** — John and Safiya standing together, hands joined, facing the whole village, declaring together.
> "We are Muslims."

**Panel 12** — The courtyard erupting — villagers rising, smiling, some in tears; Mansoor shouting with fists raised, Malood jumping up onto the table, Khalaf wiping his eyes with his sleeve.
> "Teacher John is one of us!" — Khalaf: "Late… but good."

---

### Scene 6 — The Night of Light

**Panel 1** — The courtyard feast in full swing: villagers eating, laughing, talking together under lantern light.
> They ate together. They laughed. They talked.

**Panel 2** — Safiya mid-bite, gesturing at the spread of food with mock despair, John chuckling beside her.
> "This is dangerous. I will never be thin again."

**Panel 3** — John's gaze drifting to a quiet corner where villagers are praying, rows of figures bowed in the lantern light.
> John looked at the people praying. Quiet. Peaceful.

**Panel 4** — Close on John's face, soft and settled, watching the prayer.
> "I think… this is home."

**Panel 5** — Thuraya watching John and Safiya from across the courtyard, expression full but silent.
> Thuraya looked at them. And said nothing.

**Panel 6** — Wide atmospheric shot of the courtyard: warm lamp on the wooden shelf casting yellow light over every face, Saeed sitting in his usual corner but now leaning into the group instead of watching from outside it.
> Some moments do not need words. Saeed was inside it now — the whole village was.

**Panel 7** — Later, at home: John sprawled flat on the floor, full and content, eyes closed.
> Later, at home, John lay on the floor. Full. Happy.

**Panel 8** — Safiya standing over him, arms crossed, smiling teasingly.
> "You survived fasting. You survived food. Congratulations."

**Panel 9** — John smiling up at her from the floor, quiet and certain.
> "This… was the month that changed everything."


---


## Palm Thirty-Six — The Day the Palm Tree Witnessed Everything — Samail, 1976 → 2026

### Scene 1 — The Quiet Morning of Exams

**Panel 1** — Wide shot of the village schoolroom at dawn: desks lined up unusually straight, chalkboard wiped clean, an old ceiling fan turning slowly, morning light through a window. No one has entered yet; the room itself feels tense.
> "The classroom was not noisy that morning. It was… nervous."

**Panel 2** — Small vignette strip of three children preparing at home before school: Ahmed sitting up wide-awake in the dark, Ali pushing away half a bowl of breakfast, Noura re-stacking pencils into her pencil case for the third time.
> Each child carried the private weight of exam-day nerves.

**Panel 3** — Interior classroom, children now seated and silent. John stands at the front holding exam papers, forcing a steady expression though his hands are tense.
> "Today… exams," John said, trying to sound calm.

**Panel 4** — Safiya beside John, offering the class a warm, reassuring smile to cut the tension.
> "Don't look at me like I am the judge."

**Panel 5** — Thuraya walking slowly down the row of desks, hands clasped behind her back, glancing at each child kindly.
> "Just do your best. That's all."

**Panel 6** — Close-up: exam papers being placed face-down on each desk, a child's hand hovering nervously over the top sheet.
> The papers were given.

**Panel 7** — Wide shot: every head bowed over a paper, pencils moving, Thuraya walking slowly between the rows listening to the silence like a doctor reading a heartbeat.
> Silence fell like a blanket.

**Panel 8** — Split panel of individual reactions across the room: Ahmed writing rapidly, Ali pausing mid-thought, Noura mouthing a question to herself, Khalil biting his pencil, Mansoor glaring at his paper like an enemy, and Malood the goat asleep by the door.
> Ahmed wrote like a machine; Mansoor stared at the paper like it was an enemy.

---

### Scene 2 — The Longest Week

**Panel 1** — Night interior of the small teachers' house: a single oil lamp glowing on a wooden table, shadows on the walls, stacks of exam papers piled high.
> The week of correction was… dangerous.

**Panel 2** — Wide shot: John, Safiya, and Thuraya seated around the table, each bent over a stack of papers, a teapot between them.
> Three teachers leaning over papers, a pot of tea between them.

**Panel 3** — Close on Thuraya's face in lamplight, focused and thoughtful, recognizing a student's handwriting without needing to see the name.
> She knew their handwriting better than her own.

**Panel 4** — Close-up insert: a hand marking a paper with a pencil, red/graphite marks and small comments in the margin.
> Pencil-marks and quiet teacher-comments filled the evening.

**Panel 5** — Safiya holding up a paper, smiling proudly at John and Thuraya.
> "Look at Ali," Safiya said. "His writing improved so much."

**Panel 6** — John nodding approvingly, tapping another paper on the table.
> "And Ahmed," John said. "Perfect grammar."

**Panel 7** — Thuraya laughing softly, holding up a messy but completed paper.
> "And Mansoor…" Thuraya laughed. "At least he wrote something."

---

### Scene 3 — Results Day

**Panel 1** — Wide establishing shot: children standing in neat lines under the great palm tree, the same tree that has witnessed the whole school year, mountains in the background.
> The children stood in lines under the palm tree. The one that saw everything.

**Panel 2** — Group shot of the three teachers facing the children: John holding the results paper, Safiya visibly holding her breath, Thuraya smiling like a mother.

**Panel 3** — Ahmed stepping forward, standing tall and rigid like a soldier as his name is called.
> "Ahmed," John said. "Excellent."

**Panel 4** — Ali stepping forward, eyes bright and shining with emotion.
> "Ali," Safiya said. "Excellent."

**Panel 5** — Noura standing proudly as her name is called.
> "Noura… Very good."

**Panel 6** — Group panel of Mithla, Salima, Marwa, and Rahma standing together, each nodding as their names and results are read in turn.
> "Mithla… Very good." "Salima… Good." "Marwa… Good." "Rahma… Good."

**Panel 7** — Close on Mansoor's face, eyes squeezed shut, bracing himself as his name is called.
> "Mansoor…" He closed his eyes.

**Panel 8** — John pausing for a beat before delivering the verdict, Mansoor still frozen.
> "…Passed."

**Panel 9** — Mansoor erupting — arms thrown up, mouth wide open in a joyful scream, other children laughing and reacting around him.
> The village heard his scream before the mountains did.

**Panel 10** — Wide shot pulling back over the village and distant mountains, the echo of Mansoor's scream carrying across the landscape.
> A narrow pass — but a victory all the same.

---

### Scene 4 — Graduation Day

**Panel 1** — Wide shot of the schoolyard decorated with bright cloth strung between palm fronds, paper flowers, and a hand-painted cardboard banner in Arabic and English.
> The school was decorated with simple cloth and palm leaves.

**Panel 2** — Mothers arriving carrying trays and dishes of food for the celebration.
> Mothers brought food.

**Panel 3** — Fathers standing at the edge of the yard, arms crossed, faces beaming with pride.
> Fathers came with proud faces.

**Panel 4** — Sadoo pulling the shutter down over his shop early, hurrying toward the school.
> Even Sadoo closed his shop early.

**Panel 5** — Shayb Khalaf seated in the front row of chairs, both hands resting on his walking stick as if it were a medal.
> Shayb Khalaf sat in the front, holding his stick like a medal.

**Panel 6** — The children standing in a formal line facing the crowd, straightened up and ready.
> The children stood in a line.

**Panel 7** — The children singing together, mouths open, some swaying, parents watching emotionally.
> They sang. They laughed.

**Panel 8** — Close-up: a child's hands receiving a small rolled certificate tied with string from Thuraya.
> They received small certificates.

**Panel 9** — Triple portrait: Safiya openly crying, John blinking hard and pretending not to cry, Thuraya crying freely and openly.
> Safiya cried. John pretended not to cry. Thuraya cried openly.

**Panel 10** — John standing before the gathered children and families, addressing them directly, arms open.
> "You came here not knowing even one word. Now… you can speak. You can read. You can dream."

---

### Scene 5 — The Goodbye That Was Not a Goodbye

**Panel 1** — Evening wide shot: John, Safiya, and Thuraya sitting together beneath the same palm tree, Malood the goat hopping playfully nearby, the sun beginning to set.
> That evening, they sat under the palm tree. The same one.

**Panel 2** — Safiya turning to the others, a soft, nostalgic expression on her face.
> "Do you remember our first day here?"

**Panel 3** — John smiling, remembering, gesturing as if recounting the story.
> John smiled. "I fell in the smoke."

**Panel 4** — All three laughing together, heads tilted back, the tension of the year finally releasing.
> They laughed.

**Panel 5** — Thuraya gazing upward at the palm tree's branches, quiet and reflective.
> "This place… changed us all."

**Panel 6** — Wide shot: the three sitting in comfortable silence as the sky shifts through pink, orange, and deep purple; distant village sounds — a child laughing, a mother's call, a goat's bleat.
> The village continued. The village had always continued.

**Panel 7** — Final wide dusk shot of the empty lanes and the palm tree silhouetted against the darkening sky, the school year quietly closing.
> In the quiet of that exact evening, the school year ended.

---

### Scene 6 — Suddenly… 2026

**Panel 1** — Montage panel: an old pickup truck rusting and being replaced by newer models over the years; Sadoo's shop sign changing hands to his nephew.
> Half a century passes.

**Panel 2** — Split image of the humble 1973 schoolhouse transforming through two rounds of rebuilding into a modern school building.
> The school, simple in 1973, is rebuilt — twice.

**Panel 3** — Quiet, gentle image of Shayb Khalaf as a very old man, peaceful, with his son Obaid nearby in the village; a small marker noting 1981.
> Shayb Khalaf dies in 1981, an old man, peaceful.

**Panel 4** — Companion quiet image of Al-Mu'allim Hamad, elderly and calm, in the same village setting; a small marker noting 1984.
> Al-Mu'allim Hamad dies in 1984, in the same calm way he had lived.

**Panel 5** — Montage grid of grown former students: Mansoor as a teacher telling the "Bob Jamid" story in a staffroom; Ali as an architect beside a real building he designed; Saeed in a white coat as a quiet, observant doctor.
> Mansoor becomes a teacher. Ali becomes an architect. Saeed becomes a doctor.

**Panel 6** — Continued montage: Noura speaking into a radio-station microphone; Ahmed standing before a classroom as a teacher; Khalil, now a successful shopkeeper, hanging a bucket above his own shop door for fun.
> Noura talks her way into a radio station. Khalil sets up a bucket above his shop door for fun.

**Panel 7** — Thuraya's life montage: as a young teacher, then a bride, then a mother with children, then an older woman surrounded by grandchildren — a quiet unfolding family portrait across decades.
> Thuraya teaches for forty more years. She marries. She raises children.

**Panel 8** — Faded, fragmentary images of John and Safiya — packing bags, returning, leaving again — dissolving gently at the edges of the frame, becoming more distant with each image.
> They stay. Then they leave. Then they come back. Then they leave again.

**Panel 9** — A modest wooden chest in the corner of Thuraya's house, notebooks stacked neatly inside, dust and years visible.
> The notebooks of that year stay with Thuraya. For fifty years.

**Panel 10** — Present day, 2026: an older Thuraya sitting alone in her room, pen in hand, writing carefully in a worn notebook; morning light through a window.
> A woman sitting in her room. Writing.

**Panel 11** — Thuraya closing the notebook slowly and looking toward the doorway as a voice calls from off-panel; small Arabic dialogue caption with translation beneath.
> «ثرياااا… تعالي تغدّي!» — "Thuraaaaa… come for lunch! It's already 2 o'clock!" / «إنزين ماه… جايّه.» — "Okay Mom… I'm coming."

**Panel 12** — Close-up on the notebook's final page and then its worn cover as she sets it on a shelf; cover text visible: "123 Let's Learn English Under the Palm Tree." Final wide shot beneath it: the same palm tree, ancient and patient, standing alone in Samail at golden hour.
> "And this was how it all started… under the palm tree." The palm tree keeps its secrets.


---
